Both were probed against the built compiler beforehand and confirmed silently unchecked (exit 0, zero violations). Fixed by adding a third fact channel, `cbenv` (`string -> fn_sig`), alongside the existing `scope`/`path`: `callback_sig_of_ty` synthesizes a one-parameter `fn_sig` from a `TyArrow (TyRefine (...), _)` domain, reusing `refined_param_ty` so Int/String/ADT/record domains behave identically to a directly-refined parameter (returns `None` for an unrefined or multi-argument domain, out of scope per fact 2 of the plan); `cb_add_binding` populates an alias entry only for a bare-`EVar` RHS that `resolve_call` resolves to a refined function (a call, field access, or lambda RHS is never chased). `EApp`'s named-callee path is tried first; only on a miss does `visit` consult `cbenv`. **Retired at all five binding forms** (`ELet`, `ELetQ`, `ELam`, `ELetFn`, `EMatch` arm binders) exactly like `scope_shadow`/`path_shadow`, each verified NON-VACUOUS by temporarily stubbing the retirement and confirming a false positive appears on correct code — including a genuinely new one found this way: `EBlock`'s per-statement fold only ever retired a fact for `A.ELet`, so a block-level `fn f(...) do ... end` (`ELetFn` as a SIBLING statement, not the dedicated `A.ELetFn` visit arm that only walks that local function's own body) never retired its bound name for the statements that follow it in the same block — fixed alongside the new channel. 10 new tests in a `hof_suite`/`higher-order` group (173 → 183 total): six for the callback-parameter shape (one checked, four silence, one lambda-shadow), four for the local-alias shape (one checked, three silence including a rebind-then-call-again control). Adversarial sweep (14 hand-written programs covering all five shadowing forms for both shapes, a callback passed onward to another callback-taking function, String/record callback domains, and a combined callback+alias+record+ADT+string+relational-postcondition module) all exit 0; two deliberately-violating variants of the String/record cases exit 1, confirming the checks are live rather than vacuously silent. `accept/t77_refine_hof_bypass_limitation.march` (the pre-existing conformance witness for the "direct calls only" limitation) is unaffected and still passes — its callback parameter is declared `Int -> Int` (unrefined), so it demonstrates the boundary that remains open: a caller's contract is enforced only when actually declared refined on the callback type, never inferred from what a concrete argument happens to point to. Docs: `specs/lang/refinement-types.md` (Limitations bullet rewritten, Conformance status paragraph updated), `CHANGELOG.md`, `specs/progress.md`.

- ✅ **`check_post`'s path conditions resolve in the BODY's namespace, not through the return binder (`lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml`, 2026-07-24).** `check_post` translated the path conditions with the same `resolve_var` as the return predicate, in which the binder denotes the RETURN value. A path condition comes from the function BODY, so every name in it is a body name; when a parameter shared the binder's spelling the fact was re-pointed at the returned expression — the same caller/callee conflation already fixed for `check_call`'s path conditions. Live repro, confirmed against the built compiler BEFORE the fix: `fn f(v : Int, k : Int) : {v : Int | v > 0} do if v < 0 do k else 1 end end` reported "does not satisfy its return type constraint" with counterexample `k = -1` — the guard `v < 0` is about the PARAMETER, misread as `k < 0`, which makes `v > 0` (i.e. `k > 0`) definitely false. The same conflation ALSO suppressed genuine violations: with the tail `0` instead of `k`, `v < 0` became `0 < 0`, a contradictory assumption under which the goal discharges vacuously — that shape is a second new test and it was silent before the fix. Path conditions now go through a `path_resolve_var`/`path_resolve_field` pair that resolves through the enclosing scope (`var_const` / `scope_field_resolver`); `_` is left pointing at the return term, since it is not a legal variable in body code and would otherwise declare a constant named `_`. Four tests in `post_suite`: the false positive, the suppressed-violation control, a control that a non-colliding named binder still denotes the return value in the PREDICATE, and one that a guard on the colliding parameter still discharges when the tail IS that parameter.

- ✅ **An unreflectable record field no longer sinks its siblings at a call site (`lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml`, 2026-07-24).** `serve({ port: 9, history: Cons(1, Nil) })` was skipped entirely: `term_fits_sort` rejects `Cons(1, Nil)` (the generic `List`'s element field has the opaque sort `Elem`, where an `Int` term cannot go), `reflect_record_literal` returned `None` for the whole record, and the perfectly checkable `port` field went with it. `reflect_record_literal` gained an OPTIONAL `~opaque` that mints a FRESH constant at the offending field's DECLARED sort; the ill-sorted term is never built, so the channel-poisoning hazard the original skip existed to prevent is untouched. **Soundness argument.** The stand-in carries no assumptions, so it denotes an arbitrary value of its sort. The call-site rule reports only when `¬goal` is VERIFIED — valid under EVERY assignment — so a goal depending on the stand-in is never reported and never discharged, while a goal depending only on the reflected siblings is decided exactly as if the record were fully known. **Deliberately call-site-only:** `check_post` switches to "a SAT model over a concrete record is a definite violation", and a model free to assign the stand-in a bad value would be reported as a counterexample though the real field holds a fine value — a false positive. So the return side never passes `~opaque` and a test pins that it still skips. Two existing tests flipped from silence to a reported error (`{ port: 0, name: n }`, `{ port: 0, history: Cons(1, Nil) }`) — both genuine violations that were being lost, with their comments rewritten to say why the channel-safety rationale still holds. Four new tests pin the other direction: a satisfying `port` beside an opaque field passes; a predicate ABOUT the opaque field is silent whether its real value satisfies it (`count: 1`) or violates it (`count: 5`); and the postcondition side still skips.

- ✅ **Record field facts flow into path conditions (`lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml`, 2026-07-24).** A guard on a record field (`if c.port >= 1 do serve(c)`) was collected into the `path` context like any other condition but then dropped: the path translation passed no `~resolve_field` to `smt_of`, so `c.port` reflected to `None` and the whole condition went away. Two halves were missing, and neither works without the other. **(1) A new `recenv`** — a `name -> SMT sort` env for variables whose DECLARED type is a registered record, refined or not — because `scope` carries only *refined* locals and a plain `c : Config` parameter was invisible to it. It carries no predicate: an entry is a wholly unconstrained datatype constant, so on its own it proves nothing in either direction and can only turn a skip into a check the guards actually decide. Populated from fn/lambda params, annotated `let`s, an unannotated `let c = d` aliasing a known record, and a bare `PatVar` match binder on a record-typed variable scrutinee (all the same value, so one constant is correct). **(2) `check_call`'s `record_self` now also reflects an UNREFINED record variable** to that same constant — previously it required a `scope` entry and returned `None` otherwise, which made `mode = \`Skip\`` and built no goal at all, so a guarded call could never discharge *and* a guard that made the call a definite failure was never reported. Sort-collision guards were the delicate part: a record name is declared at its datatype sort by the new `path_resolve_field`, so `path_resolve_var` and `resolve_var`'s caller-scope fallback both now return `None` for such a name rather than declaring it `Int` — one symbol at two sorts makes z3 emit an `(error …)` that desynchronises the shared `z3 -in` channel and silently disables refinement checking for the remainder of the compilation. New `record-path-facts` group in `test/test_refinecheck.ml` (11 tests). Shadow discipline verified by NEGATIVE CONTROL, not by assertion: with `path_shadow` stubbed to the identity the `let`, lambda-parameter and `match`-binder cases all FAIL (they report a violation on correct code), and pass again with it restored — so those three are genuine false-positive detectors. The `let?` case is honestly non-discriminating and labelled as such in the test: a `let?` binder can carry no type annotation (`let? x : T = …` is a dedicated parse error) and March has no annotated-expression form, so its payload's record type is never visible to `recenv`, the inner call is skipped for that unrelated reason, and the case would stay silent whatever `ELetQ` did. `recenv_shadow` is defense-in-depth rather than load-bearing (stubbing it alone changes no verdict, since `path_shadow` drops any condition *mentioning* the name — a superset); it is kept because the identity channel is the one that would bite if a fact ever reached the callee by another route. Docs: `specs/lang/refinement-types.md` (record section + a pointer from Path Sensitivity), `CHANGELOG.md`.
- ✅ **Or-patterns with bindings — DONE (2026-07-25).** The remaining half turned out to be the typecheck rule only; the lowering needed no change at all. `collect_pat_names` already recursed through `PatOr`, so `pat_binder_vars` was already collecting alternative binders and the call site was already passing them to the n-ary join point — the machinery built for the OUTER-binder case (`4fdcb527`) covered the per-alternative case for free. Replaced the blanket rejection in `infer_pattern`'s `PatOr` arm with: alternatives must bind the same names at the same types. Name disagreement reports which side each name came from; type disagreement gets a dedicated reason naming the binder instead of the generic "all branches of a match must have the same type", which pointed at the wrong thing. Verified byte-identical interpreted vs compiled on both a constructor form (`A(x) | B(x)`) and a two-alternative record form. `types/reject/t82` rewritten to pin the disagreement case it now actually tests; new `types/accept/t91` pins the agreeing case (173 total). `or_pattern.march` golden gained an `unwrap(Left(v) | Right(v))` case — the one that actually exercises argument threading through the join point.
  Original filing, for the record: **Or-patterns with bindings** — `A(x) | B(x) -> x` is rejected today (Task 5 of `specs/plans/2026-07-24-pattern-matching-gaps.md`, `typecheck.ml`'s `PatOr` arm) because every alternative shares ONE arm body and `A(x)`'s `x` and `B(x)`'s `x` would need different values depending on which alternative matched. **Update (2026-07-24, final-review fix set): the n-ary join-point machinery this asked for now EXISTS** — `hoist_fallback_jp` takes an optional `params` list and `lower_match` already passes the arm's OUTER binders (`P(x, 1 | 2)`) as join-point parameters, calling `EApp(clo_var, args)` at each split row. What remains is the per-alternative half: `expand_or_rows` gives every split row the SAME body expression, so each would need to bind its own alternative's variables (whose per-occurrence types the typechecker does not record today, since it rejects them) and pass those as the arguments — plus a rule for alternatives that bind different variable sets, or the same name at different types.

- ✅ **LLVM allocation and closure-trampoline attributes (2026-07-25).**
  `march_alloc` is declared `noalias nonnull` with `allocsize(0)`, matching
  its fresh-`calloc`/exit-on-OOM runtime contract. Canonical `$clo_wrap`
  trampolines are `alwaysinline`, removing their ABI-adaptation call boundary
  when optimization runs. Exact preamble/wrapper tests and the native LLVM IR
  corpus verifier pin both facts. No `nounwind` attributes were added.

- ✅ **Final-review fix set for the pattern-matching work — five defects at feature COMBINATION points (`lib/tir/lower_match.ml`, `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, `lib/tir/tir.ml`, 2026-07-24).** A whole-branch review of `b6cf0468..021b2463` found every isolated feature working and every combination broken; the shipped tests each exercised one feature alone, so the suite was fully green with all five present. **(1) An or-pattern arm that binds ANYTHING failed to compile.** The join point holding the shared arm body is bound OUTSIDE the whole decision tree and was 0-ary, justified by “alternatives bind no variables” — true of the alternatives, false of the ARM (`P(x, 1 | 2)`, `{ code: 1 | 2, msg: m }`, `(1 | 2) as n`). Those binders became free variables of the lifted lambda; Defun resolved them against the global namespace and clang rejected the module (`use of undefined value '@x'`), or, when a top-level fn shared the name, emitted a call to the WRONG function. Fixed by making `hoist_fallback_jp` take an optional `params` list and having both hoist sites (no-guard fast path, guarded path's `or_wrap`) pass the arm's binders — minted with the same source name and `ty_of_span` type the decision tree binds them with — as join-point parameters. Body still emitted once; duplicating per alternative was rejected again (exponential TIR blowup). **(2) A record pattern nested in a constructor payload SIGSEGV'd, compiled only.** `EField` needs the scrutinee's `Tir.ty` to be the record's or `llvm_emit` falls back to `march_record_field_dyn` and decodes the result with the FIELD's type; a statically-laid-out record stores `Float` inline as a raw double, so `march_unbox_float` dereferenced a double's bit pattern. Int/String fields survived by luck, so it was latent for most field types and lethal for Float, and every nested record pattern degraded to a runtime hash lookup instead of a GEP. Fixed by recording a record pattern's own type under its span in `infer_pattern` and re-annotating the scrutinee ATOM in place in `expand_record_column` — no extra `ELet`, so reference counting is untouched. **(3) `(p | q) as n` and `(p as a) as b` were internal compiler errors.** `expand_or_rows` ran before `strip_as_column`, which can PRODUCE a first-column `PatOr`; and `strip_as_column` peels one `PatAs` layer per pass. `compile_matrix_impl` now iterates strip-then-expand to a fixpoint. `expand_record_column`'s catch-all also stops silently dropping an unexpandable row (the B4 failure mode) and fails loudly like the sibling path. **(4) Nested or-patterns defeated exhaustiveness and produced false redundancy warnings.** `norm_pat` mapped a nested `PatOr` to `SPWild`, which in a coverage matrix means “matches everything” — so `Some(1 | 2)` claimed all of `Some(_)` (no non-exhaustiveness warning, runtime panic on `Some(99)`) and `P(x, 1 | 2)` made the next arm look unreachable (the same false positive `7b90133f` carved records out for). `norm_pat_rows` now enumerates the cross-product of alternatives at every depth, capped at `or_expansion_cap` = 256 rows; past the cap it falls back to the widening `norm_pat` and `check_redundant_arms` skips the arm exactly as it skips a record-pattern arm. **(5) `<record pattern> as name` did not typecheck** — `PatAs` dropped `expected`, so the alias got the narrow closed-synthesized record type; one `?expected` fixes it. Minors: the weak `test_or_pattern_exhaustiveness` assertion now matches the message; both live doc copies + `CHANGELOG.md` now state that record arms are excluded from BOTH coverage analyses. Deferred: the misleading closed-synthesis record-pattern error is emitted at the CALL site, so improving it needs type-provenance threading into `unify`'s diagnostics — a design change, not a note. Regression coverage: `test/native/or_pattern.march` (+`classify`/`classify_guarded`/`message` — failed to BUILD before), `test/native/record_pattern.march` (+`boxed`/`wrapped` — exit 139 before), `test/native/as_pattern.march` (+`tier`/`twice` — compiler exit 3 before), and five new `match_diagnostics` tests in `test/test_compiler.ml`. Full gate: compiler 556 / eval 250 / codegen 444 / snapshots 31 (byte-identical) / stdlib 815 of 816 / LSP 332, `dune build @runtest` otherwise green, `@types-check` 172, `@grammar-check` 44, `check-docs.sh` exit 0 — the one non-pass is the pre-existing machine-wide ASAN 30s livelock (`adversarial-regressions 38 MARCH_SANITIZE`).
- ✅ **Redundant-arm warnings restored in checking position (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, 2026-07-24).** `check_expr`'s `EMatch` arm called `check_exhaustiveness` but not `check_redundant_arms`; only the `infer_expr` path ran both. A function body with a declared return type is checked rather than inferred, so every `match` inside a return-annotated function — most matches in practice — silently skipped unreachable-arm analysis. Confirmed pre-fix with a minimal repro: `fn f(o : Option(Int)) : Int do match o do _ -> 9 ; Some(x) -> x ; None -> 0 end end` warned about nothing, while dropping the `: Int` annotation (routing the same body through inference) produced two `redundant_arm` warnings. Fix is the one missing call. Two regression tests in a new `match_diagnostics` group (`test/test_compiler.ml`) pin both the checking and inference paths so the asymmetry cannot silently return. The restored check flagged **no** pre-existing dead arms in `test/` or `stdlib/`: a revert-to-base control run produced a byte-identical failure set (18 `adversarial-regressions` failures on both sides — the known `Vault.update`/`Toml`/`record_put`/`sort_by` host-contention flakes documented in the CSV entry below, reproducing here under 7 concurrent CPU-saturating benchmark processes from other worktrees). Found while auditing the pattern matcher for gaps; see `specs/plans/2026-07-24-pattern-matching-gaps.md` for the remaining four (record patterns, as-patterns, or-patterns, and the open-field-list typing they need).

- ✅ **As-patterns (`p as name`) — Task 2 of `specs/plans/2026-07-24-pattern-matching-gaps.md` (`lib/parser/parser.mly`, `lib/tir/lower_match.ml`, 2026-07-24).** `PatAs` was fully implemented in the AST, interpreter (`eval.ml:1193`), typechecker (`typecheck.ml:3554`), and the `let`-binding lowering path (`lower.ml:240`) from the start, but `parser.mly` had no production that ever built one — confirmed unreachable via `grep -c PatAs lib/parser/parser.mly` returning 0. Fixed with a new `pattern: pattern_no_as AS lower_name` production layered above the renamed `pattern_no_as` (the former `pattern` body) — written non-left-recursively so `p as a as b` is a parse error rather than silently nesting, and using `lower_name` (not `soft_lower_name`, which already accepts `AS` as a plain identifier spelling) for the name after `AS`. Adds **zero** new shift/reduce conflicts (still 9, confirmed via `menhir --explain`, matching the plan's pre-verified fact). Exposed one lowering gap: the match-matrix compiler's `pat_tag_and_subs` returns `None` for `PatAs` (by design — it's not a discriminating pattern), so a **non-trivial** inner pattern (`Some(x) as whole`) reached the fail-loudly "unhandled pattern kind" branch (a trivial inner like `x as y` already worked, routed through `is_trivial_pat`/`bind_trivial_pat`). Fixed with a new `strip_as_column` in `lower_match.ml`, called first thing in `compile_matrix_impl`'s per-scrutinee arm: rewrites a `PatAs(inner, n, _) :: rest` row (non-trivial `inner` only) into `inner :: rest` wrapped in a `let n = scrut in body`, then lets the inner pattern dispatch normally. New `as_patterns` group in `test/test_eval.ml` (2 tests: non-trivial inner via `Some(x) as whole`, trivial inner via `x as y`) plus a compiled round-trip (`test/native/as_pattern.march` — `describe(Some(21))`/`describe(None)`, diffed against `test/native/as_pattern.expected`). Grammar conformance corpus: `specs/lang/grammar/reject/r08_as_pattern_unreachable.march` retired, replaced by `specs/lang/grammar/parse/p29_as_pattern.march` (well-typed, `--check` exit 0) — corpus now 29 parse + 14 reject = 43 total, `grammar-check` green. Docs updated in the same commit: `specs/lang/grammar.md` §6.3 (now covers only `PatRecord`; `PatAs`'s history stated in words per the doc-freshness convention, not deleted), `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` (new `(P-As)` typing rule replacing the old "no rule, unreachable" prose), `specs/lang/pattern-matching.md` + `docs/pattern-matching.md` (new "As Patterns" section, kept in sync), `specs/lang/surface-syntax.md` + `.claude/skills/march-lang/SKILL.md` (quick-reference examples). `scripts/check-docs.sh` green. Full suite: no new failures beyond the pre-existing 18 `adversarial-regressions` host-contention flakes.

- ✅ **Record patterns (`{ x, y: p }`) — Task 3 of `specs/plans/2026-07-24-pattern-matching-gaps.md` (`lib/parser/parser.mly`, `lib/parser/token_filter.ml`, `lib/tir/lower_match.ml`, `lib/tir/lower.ml`, 2026-07-24), field lists exactly matching the record's fields (partial field lists landed in Task 4, see below).** `PatRecord` was fully implemented in the AST, interpreter (`eval.ml:1178`), and typechecker (`typecheck.ml:3544`) from the start, but `parser.mly` had no production that ever built one — confirmed via `grep -c PatRecord lib/parser/parser.mly` returning 0 — and BOTH TIR lowering paths were unready: `lower_match.ml:205` failed loudly, and `lower.ml`'s `bind_subpat` catch-all silently returned the body unchanged for a `let` binding, which would have left every bound name an undefined global fn reference (a silent miscompile, not a loud failure) had the grammar landed without matching lowering. Grammar: new `simple_pattern` alternative `LBRACE record_field_pat,* RBRACE` building `PatRecord`, plus `record_field_pat: name COLON pattern | name` (bare `name` puns to `name: name`); `token_filter.ml`'s `is_pattern_start` gained `LBRACE`. Adds **zero** new shift/reduce conflicts (still 9, confirmed via `menhir --explain`). Match-matrix lowering: new `record_fields_in_column`/`expand_record_column` in `lower_match.ml` — a record has no ctor tag, so instead of dispatching, the first record column is expanded into one fresh-variable column per field name (the sorted union across all rows, so every row expands to the same arity), each field projected via `ELet(f_<name>, EField(scrut, name), …)`, and the recursion continues over the new columns — this makes a refutable sub-pattern inside a record field (`{ code: 404, msg: m }`) dispatch for free, since it lands in an ordinary column. `let`-binding lowering: new `PatRecord` arm in `lower.ml`'s `bind_subpat`, mirroring the existing `PatTuple` arm — one `ELet` per named field, recursing through a fresh intermediate for compound sub-patterns, wildcard fields contributing no binding. Field types matter for both paths: a field var left at `Lower_types.unknown_ty` makes Perceus treat an unboxed scalar as RC-managed (corrupts compiled output); `expand_record_column`'s `field_ty` tries the scrutinee's own structural `TRecord` type first, then the typechecker's recorded type for the sub-pattern's span, only then falls back to `unknown_ty`. New `record_patterns` group in `test/test_eval.ml` (5 tests: match arm, punned fields, `let` binding, function parameter, and a refutable sub-pattern forcing real per-field dispatch) plus a compiled round-trip (`test/native/record_pattern.march` — match arm, `let`, fn param, and the refutable `classify` dispatch — diffed against `test/native/record_pattern.expected`). Note: the typechecker's redundancy/exhaustiveness analysis still treats any top-level `PatRecord` as `SPWild` (`typecheck.ml:3624`, pre-existing, out of this task's scope) — it is a safe over-approximation for the "record is single-constructor, so irrefutable" case this analysis is built for, but it means a `match` with two arms discriminating on different literal sub-patterns of the SAME field (as in the compiled fixture's `classify`) gets a false-positive "this pattern can never be reached" WARNING; the arm still lowers and runs correctly (confirmed: compiled output matches `.expected` exactly), this is a diagnostics-quality gap, not a lowering bug. Grammar conformance corpus: `specs/lang/grammar/reject/r02_record_pattern_in_arm_unreachable.march` and `r07_record_pattern_in_let_unreachable.march` retired, replaced by `specs/lang/grammar/parse/p30_record_pattern_in_arm.march`/`p31_record_pattern_in_let.march` (well-typed, `--check` exit 0 for both) — corpus now 31 parse + 12 reject = 43 total, `grammar-check` green. Docs updated in the same commit: `specs/lang/grammar.md` §6.3 rewritten as a joint historical note for `PatRecord`/`PatAs` (both unreachable until 2026-07-24), §3.4's `LBRACE`-absent claim corrected to `LBRACE`-present, §6.1's `simple_pattern` EBNF gained the 13th alternative; `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` (new `(P-Record)` typing rule replacing the old "no rule, unreachable" prose); `specs/lang/pattern-matching.md` + `docs/pattern-matching.md` (rewritten "Record Patterns" sections — real syntax, punning, and the current exact-field-list restriction — plus the stale multi-head-fn parenthetical); `docs/types.md` (dropped the "not yet supported by the parser" claim); `specs/lang/surface-syntax.md` + `.claude/skills/march-lang/SKILL.md` (quick-reference examples). `scripts/check-docs.sh` green (INDEX.md header/count sites updated to match the corpus move). Full suite: no new failures beyond the pre-existing `adversarial-regressions` host-contention flakes and the machine-wide ASAN 30s livelock (`adversarial-regressions 38 MARCH_SANITIZE`, verified independent — a trivial unrelated `clang -fsanitize=address` hello-world hangs the same way on this host).

- ✅ **Partial (open-field-list) record patterns — Task 4 of `specs/plans/2026-07-24-pattern-matching-gaps.md` (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, 2026-07-24).** Task 3 made record patterns work end-to-end only when the pattern's field list EXACTLY matched the record's own fields: `infer_pattern`'s `PatRecord` arm synthesized a CLOSED `TRecord` from just the mentioned fields, and `unify` (`typecheck.ml:2789`) requires exact field-set equality (no width subtyping, no row variables), so `{ x }` against `{ x: Int, y: Int }` was a type error. Fixed by driving `PatRecord`'s sub-patterns from the EXPECTED type (already threaded as an optional `~expected` arg, used before this change only for constructor-name disambiguation in `PatCon`) instead of synthesizing one: when `expected` resolves to a record (via `expand_record`, accepting both a structural `TRecord` and a nominal `TCon` naming one), each mentioned field is checked against the expected type's field, unmentioned fields are simply not bound, and the pattern's own type is the expected type unchanged (so the caller's `unify` is a no-op); a field the record doesn't have is now a dedicated `unknown_record_field` error, not a unification mismatch. When `expected` doesn't resolve to a record (no annotation reaches the pattern), the ORIGINAL closed-synthesis behavior is the fallback — the only thing that can constrain an otherwise-unconstrained scrutinee at all. Also extended `PatTuple` to thread a per-element expected type to its sub-patterns (from its OWN `expected`, when that resolves to a `TTuple`) — needed for a record pattern nested inside a tuple pattern with an independently-known element type (e.g. two explicitly-annotated function parameters matched together via `match (a, b) do (…, { w: w }) -> … end`); verified this threading is load-bearing by reverting it alone and confirming the same nested-record case starts failing again. **Investigated and found narrower than the task brief assumed:** the brief's own motivating claim — that this also opens a BARE record pattern used directly as a function parameter, e.g. `fn width({ w: w }) : Int do w end` called with a wider `{ w: 8, h: 9 }` — does not hold. Traced empirically (confirmed by compiling the brief's literal fixture and by temporarily reverting `PatTuple`'s threading to check the failure disappears without it): `desugar_fn_def`'s general path (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml:746`) does NOT wrap a single (arity-1) parameter in a tuple — `scrutinee = EVar arg_name` and `pat = p` directly, no `PatTuple` — so `PatTuple` threading never engages for a one-parameter function regardless of pattern shape; and `fn_param`'s grammar (`parser.mly:413`) gives a bare pattern parameter no annotation slot at all (only `name : Type` can carry one), so such a parameter's type has no source but the pattern itself and is necessarily closed to exactly the fields it names, full stop — not something any amount of expected-type threading inside `infer_pattern` can open, since there is no annotation to source an expected type from. This is a genuine, narrow language limitation (not a bug introduced or left by this task): to open a record-pattern parameter's field list, the parameter must be given a name and a type annotation and destructured via an explicit `match` in the body instead (`fn f(r : {w:Int,h:Int}) do match r do {w:w} -> w end end`), which routes back through `PatRecord`'s expected-known path. Similarly, a `let` binding's `infer_pattern` call site (`typecheck.ml`'s `ELet`/`infer_block` arms) never threads `~expected` either — the binding IS what establishes the type, so there is nothing to drive from — meaning `let { x: px } = p` still requires naming every field of `p`'s type; confirmed by direct compile (partial `let` destructure against both a record literal and a nominal record type both still reject with the pre-existing exact-match error). Neither gap was in the brief's file list or its Step 1 TDD tests (which cover only match arms), so left unchanged; flagged here rather than silently over- or under-claimed. `test/native/record_pattern_partial.march`'s golden was adjusted accordingly: `classify` tests the match-arm case (two arms mentioning disjoint field subsets), and `width` tests the `PatTuple`-threading case with two ANNOTATED parameters matched via an explicit `match (dims, scale) do ({w:w}, s) -> … end` — NOT a bare `{ w: w }` parameter as the brief's literal text showed, for the reason above. New tests: `test_partial_record_pattern_typechecks`/`test_record_pattern_unknown_field_rejected` in `test/test_compiler.ml`'s `match_diagnostics` group, `test_eval_record_pattern_partial` in `test/test_eval.ml`'s `record_patterns` group (this one already passed before the typecheck fix, for a legitimate reason — `eval.ml`'s tree-walking interpreter's own `PatRecord` matcher, `eval.ml:1178`, looks fields up by name and was already open-field-list-tolerant independent of the typechecker; only the compiled path, gated by typecheck, actually exercises the fix). Docs updated in the same commit: `specs/lang/pattern-matching.md` + `docs/pattern-matching.md` (rewrote "Record Patterns" — open field lists in `match`, the `unknown_record_field` error, and the two still-closed positions with a worked example for each; also fixed a pre-existing Task 3 doc bug found in passing: `fn area({ w: w, h: h } : { w : Int, h : Int })` is not valid March syntax — a bare pattern parameter cannot carry a `: Type` annotation — confirmed via direct parse failure, replaced with a valid example); `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` (split `(P-Record)` into "expected known"/"expected unknown" rules plus a dated note explaining both the fix and the fn-parameter/`let` limitations). `scripts/check-docs.sh` green. Full suite: no new failures beyond the pre-existing machine-wide ASAN 30s livelock (`adversarial-regressions 38 MARCH_SANITIZE`).

- ✅ **Or-patterns (`p1 | p2 | p3`), non-binding alternatives — Task 5 of `specs/plans/2026-07-24-pattern-matching-gaps.md` (`lib/ast/ast.ml`, `lib/parser/parser.mly`, `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, `lib/eval/eval.ml`, `lib/tir/lower_match.ml`, + one mechanical arm each in `lib/ast/span_remap.ml`, `lib/desugar/desugar.ml`, `lib/dump/ast_json.ml`, `lib/format/format.ml`, `lib/refactor/refactor.ml`, `lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml`, `lsp/lib/analysis.ml`, `lsp/lib/workspace.ml`, 2026-07-24).** The only task in this plan that adds a genuinely NEW `Ast.pattern` constructor rather than wiring up one that already existed — `PatOr of pattern list * span`. Grammar: `pattern_no_as` gained an or layer beneath the existing `as`-alias layer (`pattern: pattern_no_as AS lower_name`, Task 2), with the pre-existing `pattern_no_as` alternatives (constructor/atom/`simple_pattern`) renamed down to a new `pattern_alt`: `pattern_no_as: pattern_alt PIPE separated_nonempty_list(PIPE, pattern_alt) | pattern_alt`. `PIPE` is already `arm_sep` (`NL | PIPE` between match arms), but an arm separator only ever follows a COMPLETE branch (post-`->`), so LR(1) distinguishes the two uses with no conflict — verified empirically before writing the production and reconfirmed after: menhir's shift/reduce conflict count is unchanged at 9. Adding the AST constructor forced the OCaml compiler to enumerate every non-exhaustive `match` over `Ast.pattern` (warning-8-as-error): 8 files needed a one-line arm mirroring the neighbouring `PatTuple`/`PatAs` arm (span_remap, desugar's `pat_binds_conn`/`respan_pat`/`add_pat_vars`, ast_json's `pattern_to_json`, format's `fmt_pat`, refactor's `iter_names`, refine_check's `structural_subvars`, lsp's `workspace.ml`/`analysis.ml` — several `analysis.ml` sites already had a `_ ->` catch-all from before this task and needed no change, since an or-pattern binds nothing valid programs could reach there anyway). **Scope: alternatives that bind no variables** (`1 | 2 | 3`, `:get | :head`, `Red | Blue`). Binding alternatives (`A(x) | B(x)`) are rejected at typecheck with a dedicated diagnostic (`` Or-pattern alternatives cannot bind variables (`x`). ``, code `or_pattern_binding`) — not a workaround, a hard restriction: the arm body is shared across every alternative via `lower_match.ml`'s existing 0-arg join point (`hoist_fallback_jp`), which has nowhere to put a per-alternative binding (`A(x)`'s `x` and `B(x)`'s `x` would need to be bound to DIFFERENT values depending on which alternative matched, but the shared body has only one call site). Lifting this needs n-ary join points (hoist the body as a function of the bound variables, have each split row call it with its own bindings) — filed as a follow-up below, not attempted here; duplicating the arm body per alternative instead was explicitly rejected as a workaround since it reintroduces exactly the exponential TIR blowup `hoist_fallback_jp` exists to prevent. Interpreter: `match_pattern`'s new `PatOr` case tries each alternative in order, first match wins. Exhaustiveness/redundancy: `norm_pat` maps `PatOr` to `SPWild` (conservative, matching the existing `PatRecord` carve-out) but a new `norm_pat_rows` wrapper expands an or-pattern into one `spat` row per alternative — both `check_exhaustiveness`'s matrices and `check_redundant_arms`' prefix now flat-map through it, so `Red | Green` plus a `Blue` arm is correctly seen as exhaustive for a three-constructor type, and `check_redundant_arms` flags an arm redundant only if **every** alternative is already subsumed (`List.exists ... is_useful`, not "any"). Lowering (`lib/tir/lower_match.ml`): new `expand_or_rows` (next to `strip_as_column`) splits a row whose first column is a `PatOr` into one row per alternative sharing the same body; `pat_tag_and_subs` returns `None` for `PatOr` (split before tag dispatch, so it never sees one, like `PatRecord`). To avoid emitting N copies of a shared body once `expand_or_rows` splits it across N leaf positions in the decision tree, both `lower_match`'s no-guard fast path and its guarded path now hoist an or-bearing arm's body into a shared 0-arg join point (`hoist_fallback_jp`) BEFORE the row reaches `expand_or_rows`, referencing the cheap `EApp(clo_var, [])` call at each split position instead of the full body. New `or_patterns` group in `test/test_eval.ml` (2 tests: literal alternatives, nullary-constructor alternatives) plus `test_or_pattern_binding_rejected`/`test_or_pattern_nonbinding_accepted`/`test_or_pattern_exhaustiveness` in `test/test_compiler.ml`'s `match_diagnostics` group, plus a compiled round-trip (`test/native/or_pattern.march`/`.expected`). Grammar conformance corpus: `specs/lang/grammar/parse/p32_or_pattern.march` added (32 parse + 12 reject = 44 total, `grammar-check` green). The binding-rejection witness is a **type** error, not a parse error (`A(x) | B(x)` parses fine), so it lives in the types corpus instead of grammar/reject: `specs/lang/types/reject/t82_or_pattern_binding.march` (82 reject + 90 accept = 172 total, `types-check` green). Docs updated in the same commit: `specs/lang/grammar.md` (new §6.4 for `PatOr`, plus the §6.2 EBNF rewritten to show the full `pattern`/`pattern_no_as`/`pattern_alt` three-layer hierarchy and the `PIPE`-dual-use finding), `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` (new `(P-Or)` typing rule alongside `(P-Record)`/`(P-As)`), `specs/lang/pattern-matching.md` + `docs/pattern-matching.md` (new "Or Patterns" section — syntax, first-match-wins semantics, the no-bindings restriction and its two workarounds), `specs/lang/surface-syntax.md` + `.claude/skills/march-lang/SKILL.md` (quick-reference examples), `CHANGELOG.md`. `scripts/check-docs.sh` green. Full suite: no new failures beyond the pre-existing machine-wide ASAN 30s livelock (`adversarial-regressions 38 MARCH_SANITIZE`).
- Session types: **post-`choose` protocol steps dropped from every projection
  (session-types-review Task 1, 2026-07-24)**. `project_steps`' `ProtoChoice`
  arm projected each branch with the projection call's outer continuation
  (`cont`, which is `SEnd` at top level) instead of `rest_ty ()`, the steps
  that actually follow the `choose ... end` block — so both roles silently
  lost the protocol's tail, consistently enough that binary duality still
  passed and a program that skipped the trailing message typechecked and ran
  clean. Fixed by projecting each branch with `let after_choice = rest_ty ()`
  in place of `cont`. Compile-time-only fix (channel runtime is untyped; no
  lowering/codegen changes). New regression test
  `test_session_choice_tail_survives_projection` in `test/test_compiler.ml`
  asserts every branch of a `Tail` protocol's Client projection carries the
  post-choice `Send(String, End)` tail. New reject-corpus witness
  `specs/lang/types/reject/t91_choice_tail_step_required.march` pins the
  behavior change: pre-fix this program (closes instead of driving the
  trailing `Client -> Server : String`) was wrongly ACCEPTED; post-fix it
  rejects with `` Chan.close: channel is at `Send(String, End)` but I expected
  `End`. ``. `specs/lang/types/INDEX.md` reject table 81 → 82 (172 / 172
  total).
- Session types: **`loop` protocols didn't loop (session-types-review Task 2,
  2026-07-24)**. `project_steps`' `ProtoLoop` arm built the recursive body with
  an `SVar` back-reference to the binder and then called `subst_svar rec_var
  after_loop inner`, REPLACING that back-reference with the post-loop
  continuation — so the `SRec` binder it wrapped around the result contained
  no `SVar` anywhere in it: the projection was one unrolled iteration, and a
  second iteration was rejected with `` channel is at `End` ``. Fixed with the
  standard µ-type encoding `Rec X. S[X]`: the body's continuation IS the
  binder's own back-reference (`SVar rec_var`), so `unfold_srec` re-wraps the
  binder on every unfold and the channel state cycles indefinitely. Since such
  a loop never exits, any protocol step written after a `loop` block is now
  unreachable and rejected at protocol-declaration time (new
  `check_unreachable_after_loop` walk over `pdef.proto_steps` in the
  `Ast.DProtocol` validation, recursing into `ProtoChoice` branches). `subst_svar`
  is no longer called from the loop arm but stays in the file (still referenced
  elsewhere in the `and`-chain). De-vacuumed `test_session_loop_projection`
  (the old assertion `SRec (_, SSend _)` held even after the back-reference
  was destroyed) plus two new tests in `test/test_compiler.ml`:
  `test_session_loop_two_iterations_ok` (a two-role loop typechecks a second
  send/recv round) and `test_session_steps_after_loop_error` (a step after a
  top-level `loop` is now a protocol-declaration error). Fixed
  `test_srec_multi_turn_typechecks` as collateral: it called `Chan.close` on a
  channel still inside a loop, which the old bug's one-unrolled-iteration
  behavior wrongly allowed — a looping channel never reaches `End` so
  `Chan.close` on it is correctly rejected now; the test drops the channel
  instead (legal, since the must-close check fires only at `End`). New
  conformance witnesses `specs/lang/types/accept/t92_loop_protocol_two_iterations.march`
  (two full loop iterations typecheck and run identically interpreted and
  compiled, printing `3`) and
  `specs/lang/types/reject/t93_steps_after_loop_unreachable.march` (a step
  after `loop` is rejected with "can never run"). `specs/lang/types/INDEX.md`
  accept table 90 → 91, reject table 82 → 83 (174 / 174 total).
- Session types: **`Chan.new` on a 3+-role protocol silently returned two
  non-dual endpoints (session-types-review Task 3, 2026-07-24)**. The
  `Chan.new` arm of `infer_expr` (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`) ended in an
  explicit `(* 3+ roles: just return first two as a pair *)` fallback that
  handed back the first two roles' projections as a `(Chan, Chan)` pair with
  no diagnostic — those two endpoints are not duals of each other, so this
  was a real, silent unsoundness. `MPST.new` already had the mirror-image
  "requires at least 3" guard, but nothing on the `Chan.new` side stopped it
  being called on a multi-party protocol. Fixed by replacing the fallback
  with an `Err.error` reporting the protocol name and its actual role count,
  pointing the caller at `MPST.new`, plus `TError`. New regression test
  `test_session_chan_new_multiparty_error` in `test/test_compiler.ml`
  (registered next to `session Chan.new unknown`) and new reject-conformance
  witness `specs/lang/types/reject/t94_chan_new_multiparty_protocol.march`
  (a 3-role protocol passed to `Chan.new`, expecting `` Chan.new: protocol
  `Tri` has 3 roles ``). `specs/lang/types/INDEX.md` reject table 83 → 84
  (175 / 175 total).
- Session types: **offer-label `match` exhaustiveness was backwards
  (session-types-review Task 5, 2026-07-27)**. Matching the `Atom` label
  returned by `Chan.offer` went through the ordinary Atom exhaustiveness
  path — an OPEN universe — so a `match` handling EVERY protocol branch
  still warned `` Non-exhaustive pattern match — missing case: _ `` while a
  `match` that genuinely omitted a branch produced the exact same warning,
  never an error. New helper `check_offer_label_exhaustiveness`
  (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, next to `offer_arm_label`) checks the arms
  against the protocol's CLOSED label set (from `env.offer_labels`) instead
  when the scrutinee is an offer-label variable: all labels covered (with or
  without a catch-all) is silent, a missing label with no catch-all is now
  an `Err.error` naming the branch. Both `check_exhaustiveness` call sites
  try the offer-label check first, falling back to the generic Atom checker
  when the scrutinee isn't a linked offer label. Inherits Task 4's shadowing
  discipline for free (a rebound label name simply isn't an offer label any
  more). Two Task-4-migrated witnesses (`accept/t43_choose_offer_roundtrip`,
  `golden/g39_chan_choose_offer`) keep their now-unnecessary catch-alls
  unchanged (re-verified clean, no new redundant-arm warning);
  `reject/t74_offer_wrong_branch_drive` still rejects for its ORIGINAL
  reason (wrong-branch payload type), confirming the new check stays silent
  there. New regression tests `test_session_offer_all_labels_no_warning` and
  `test_session_offer_missing_label_error` in `test/test_compiler.ml`. New
  reject-conformance witness
  `specs/lang/types/reject/t96_offer_missing_branch_arm.march`. `specs/lang/types/INDEX.md`
  reject table 86 → 87 (178 / 178 total).
- Record field preconditions **integrated with `main`** (String refinements,
  ADT constructor tags, the predicate-vocabulary foundation, and the two
  solver-robustness fixes), 2026-07-24. `sig_of_clause` now collects through
  `main`'s `refined_param_ty` — a record IS a registered 1-constructor ADT, so
  one predicate admits all four refinable bases; a new `is_record_sort` picks
  the record-specific path (field selectors) out of the three things
  `rparam.sort` can now name. The branch's dedicated record preamble was
  dropped: the record path seeds its sort into `main`'s `adt_sorts` and
  inherits the existing deduplication against the measure and `$Str`
  preambles, so a VC mentioning a record AND a tester AND a string declares
  each sort once. Two real defects found and fixed during the sweep:
  `term_fits_sort` was shallow, so `history: Cons(1, Nil)` (an `Int` in the
  generic `List`'s opaque `Elem` field) built a malformed VC; and z3's
  multi-line `(error …)` reply left a continuation line in the pipe that was
  read as the NEXT query's verdict, shifting every later answer by one and
  turning correct calls into reported violations. `term_fits_sort` now
  recurses into constructor arguments; `read_verdict` consumes the whole error
  s-expression. `test_refinecheck` 124 → 137, all green on a cold VC cache.
- Record field **preconditions**: a refinement over a record's fields
  (`{v : Config | v.port >= 1}`) is now checked on parameters at every call
  site, closing the half that return-type record refinements already covered.
  `rparam` carries the record's SMT sort, `sig_of_clause` collects record
  params through `refined_scope_ty`, and `check_call` reflects a record actual
  (a literal, with fields reordered by name to the declaration order; or a
  variable carrying a record-refined local's own predicate) and passes the
  existing `make_field_resolver` plus the record datatype preamble to
  `smt_of`. Definite-failure stance preserved: the record path uses the same
  two-discharge procedure as the Int path, NOT `check_post`'s report-on-SAT
  branch, so a weaker forwarded refinement is skipped while a contradictory
  one is caught; an unrefined record, an unknown field value, or any field
  that cannot be placed at its declared sort skips the whole call.
  Also fixed a pre-existing solver-poisoning bug found while sweeping: a
  non-`Int` record field bound to a variable built a sort-mismatched VC, and
  Z3's error desynchronised the shared `z3 -in` channel, silently disabling
  refinement checking for the rest of the compilation (a `take_n(-3)` two
  functions later went unreported). `reflect_record_literal` is now
  sort-checked against `ctor_field_sorts`; this also closes the same hole on
  the return side. `test_refinecheck` 86 → 97, all green on a cold VC cache.
- String refinements integrated with the predicate-vocabulary foundation and
  ADT constructor-tag refinements (`lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml`,
  2026-07-24). `rparam` now carries a single `sort : string option` covering
  `Int`/`String`/ADT instead of the two branches' parallel flags; `smt_of`
  carries both `?resolve_tester` and `?resolve_str_lit`; path conditions
  resolve in the caller's namespace *and* see the string-literal table. One
  hazard existed only in the combination: `path_resolve_var` would declare a
  `$Str`-sorted caller variable a second time as `Int`, which makes z3 emit an
  error line and silently disables refinement checking for the rest of the
  compilation — it now yields the string-sorted constant, with a
  drop-`Int`-decls-for-`$Str`-symbols guard at VC assembly as backstop. The
  measure, ADT-datatype and `$Str` preambles compose in any combination
  (verified by teeing the solver channel: zero `(error` lines, and a later
  violation in the same file still reported). `test_refinecheck` = 124 tests,
  19/19 suites registered.

- String refinements: `{String | len(_) > 0}` and `{String | _ != ""}` are
  checkable contracts. `String` is encoded as an uninterpreted SMT sort, `len`
  as an uninterpreted `Str -> Int` with a non-negativity axiom, and each
  distinct string literal in a VC as a constant with its BYTE length pinned
  (matching `string_length`, which aliases `march_string_byte_length`) and
  pairwise distinctness asserted — deliberately inside EUF + linear arithmetic,
  with no SMT string theory. `len` overload resolution keys on the value's
  DECLARED base type, never on inference, so list `len` is unchanged. Two
  encoding hazards found and fixed during the work: the SMT symbols now carry a
  `$` (illegal in a March identifier) so a program variable named `len` cannot
  collide with the `len` function declaration, and ill-sorted terms mixing a
  string with an Int are dropped — either one made z3 emit an error line, which
  desynchronised the shared long-lived `z3 -in` channel and silently disabled
  refinement checking for the REST of the compilation. Known gaps (documented):
  an `s == ""` guard establishes no length in the else-branch (no injectivity
  axiom), no prefix/suffix/contains/regex, and String return refinements are
  checked at the definition but do not propagate to call sites.

- Refinement path-fact shadowing + caller/callee namespace split
  (`lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml`, 2026-07-24). Three review-found false
  positives, all "a fact about a *name* read where that name changed meaning".
  (1) `path_shadow` (+ `expr_mentions`) is the path-context companion to
  `scope_shadow`: it drops any path condition mentioning a name a binding
  construct rebinds, applied at all five sites in `visit` (`EBlock` `let`
  threading, `ELam`, `ELetFn`, `EMatch` arm binders, `ELetQ`) and the two in
  `tails`. Fixes both the ADT case (`None -> let x = Some(1); unwrap(x)`) and
  the pre-existing `Int`/`if`-guard case. Over-approximate by design —
  discarding a fact can only produce silence. (2) `check_call` gained
  caller-namespace `path_resolve_var`/`path_resolve_measure`/
  `path_resolve_tester` for the path context, so a path condition is no longer
  re-pointed at the callee's actuals through `rp.binder`/`actual_of_name`; a
  caller variable reflects to `Const name`, the same term an `EVar` actual
  reflects to, so narrowing still links. Predicate translation unchanged.
  (3) `register_builtin_adts`/`register_adt_names`/`register_field_sorts`
  hoisted out of the `measure_axioms` guard — with the flag off, `adt_ctors`
  was empty and the vocabulary warning falsely called `is_Some` unknown.
  Plus: removed `is_adt_base`'s stale `[@@warning "-32"]`; documented the
  `as`-pattern narrowing limit; replaced the vacuous "narrowing does not leak
  past a rebinding pattern binder" test (both arms were `-> 0`, so nothing was
  checked). `test_refinecheck.exe`: 112 tests, exit 0 (was 104); nine new
  cases, each confirmed RED first. Open follow-up: `check_post`'s `resolve_var`
  has the same caller/callee conflation for a *named* return binder.

- ADT constructor-tag refinements (`lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml`,
  `lib/refine/smt.ml`, 2026-07-24). Every constructor of every registered ADT
  implicitly gains an `is_<Ctor>` tester the refinement checker understands
  (`ctor_of_tester`, folded into `known_predicate_fn`), reflected to the Z3
  datatype tester `((_ is Ctor) x)` via a new `Smt.IsCtor`. Two fact sources:
  a constructor literal at the call site (`unwrap(None)`), and `match`-arm
  narrowing, which pushes a synthetic `is_Ctor(s)` path condition so it flows
  through the existing `smt_of` translation. `rparam` gained a `sort` field and
  `refined_param_ty` admits any registered-ADT base type; `check_call` reflects
  the subject with the existing `reflect_dt` and attaches the datatype
  declarations (`adt_vc_preamble`, deduplicated against `measure_preamble`).
  `Option`/`Result` are seeded in `register_builtin_adts` alongside `List` —
  the plan assumed they came from stdlib `DType`s, but they have NO `type`
  declaration anywhere; the typechecker pre-registers them (`builtin_ctors`)
  and the refinement checker must do the same or `is_Some` names nothing.
  Deliberately NOT extended: `refined_scope_ty` / `return_refine_ext` /
  `check_post`, so a tag refinement is discharged at the call site and not
  carried through a binding — extending `scope_facts` would have flipped
  `check_post` into its "SAT = definite error" mode for ADT scope entries whose
  sorts the record preamble does not declare, a false-positive risk with no
  test coverage. Narrowing is skipped for a non-variable scrutinee, an arm that
  rebinds the scrutinee's name, and an ambiguous constructor name (shared by
  two ADTs). `test_refinecheck.exe`: 104 tests, exit 0 (was 94);
  `run_compiler`/`run_eval`/`run_stdlib -q` all exit 0; zero new diagnostics on
  stdlib-using programs.

- Refinement predicate-vocabulary warning: `lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml`
  names the predicate vocabulary in one place (`predicate_operators`,
  `is_predicate_operator`, `known_predicate_fn` = operators ∪ `len` ∪
  registered `@[measure]`s) instead of leaving it scattered across
  `is_measure`/`is_nonneg_measure`/inline `"len"` checks, and adds
  `is_string_base`/`is_adt_base` base-type predicates for the ADT-tag and
  String-refinement features to dispatch on (no consumer yet). A predicate
  applying a name outside that vocabulary — e.g. `{Int | totally_bogus_fn(_) >
  0}`, which previously compiled clean and enforced nothing — now draws a
  Warning (never an Error; `--check` still exits 0) covering parameter,
  return, and local-binding refinements. Verified zero spurious warnings on a
  real stdlib-using program. `test_refinecheck.exe`: 94 tests, exit 0 (was
  86); `run_compiler`/`run_eval` both exit 0.

- ✅ **Tier 1 relational postcondition propagation (`lib/refinecheck/refine_check.ml`,
  2026-07-24).** A return refinement that mentions a parameter now propagates to
  call sites, closing the Tier 0 gap. `pred_is_closed` (a boolean accept/reject)
  became `classify_pred : binder -> params -> expr -> pred_scope` returning
  `Closed | Relational of string list | Unusable`, and `postcond_of` gained the
  call's actual arguments so it returns a predicate already instantiated in the
  CALLER's namespace — both propagation sites (`scope_add_binding` for a
  `let`-bound call, `reflect_scalar` for an inline one) therefore needed no
  substitution logic of their own. `subst_params` substitutes **simultaneously**
  (so `f(m, 1)` against `{Int | _ < n + m}` gives `_ < m + 1`, not `_ < 1 + 1`)
  and never rewrites an application HEAD, so a formal sharing a measure's name
  cannot turn `len(xs)` into `40(xs)`. Formals map to actuals positionally,
  matching the precondition side's existing `check_call` convention. **Skip,
  don't guess:** if any parameter the predicate mentions has no actual, the whole
  instantiation is abandoned — a partially substituted predicate would mix
  callee and caller namespaces, the exact conflation behind an earlier
  false-positive class. The Tier 0 safety guarantee is inherited unchanged:
  `gate_unverified_posts` still clears `ret` on any postcondition the definition
  side did not POSITIVELY VERIFY (confirmed by probe: `below`'s `_ < n`
  verifies, `shady`'s does not), so a stale or unprovable relational contract
  cannot travel. Known conservatism around pattern parameters — investigated
  2026-07-25 and found LARGER than first recorded, see the dedicated open item
  below. 13-program
  adversarial sweep (name collision, pattern formal, match/if arms, four
  shadowing forms, `len` measure, extra args, recursion) all exit 0 on a cold
  VC cache; the headline `takepos(below(0))` exits 1. `test_refinecheck.exe`:
  154 tests, exit 0 (was 137); `test_refine`/`run_compiler`/`run_eval` exit 0;
  a six-kind multi-violation file reports all six with zero `(error`.

- **OPEN — a destructuring parameter silently discards EVERY parameter
  refinement on that function (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml`, found 2026-07-25).**
  First recorded as "desugar renames params to `__argN` so the predicate
  classifies `Unusable`". Probing shows that understates it. A clause whose
  params include a real destructuring `FPPat` (e.g. `fn f({ w: w, h: h }, n)`)
  misses both fast paths in `desugar_fn_def` and takes the general path, which
  rebuilds the clause with `List.map mk_named_param arg_names` — and
  `mk_named_param` sets `param_ty = None`. So it is not a renaming problem:
  **all parameter type annotations, refinements included, are dropped**, and
  separately the surviving return refinement references parameter names that no
  longer exist. Direction is silence in both cases (an absent refinement is
  never checked; an unresolvable predicate classifies `Unusable`), so this is
  incompleteness, not unsoundness — but it is silent, and a user who writes
  `fn f({ w: w }, n : {Int | _ >= 0})` gets no checking and no warning.

  Live repro, confirmed against the built compiler: with
  `fn destr({ w: w, h: h }, n : Int) : {Int | _ < n} do n - 1 end`,
  the call `takepos(destr({ w: 1, h: 2 }, 0))` exits 0; the identical function
  without the destructuring parameter exits 1.

  NOT a small fix, which is why it is filed rather than done: the general path
  would have to preserve parameter types positionally, changing what the
  typechecker sees for every such function, in the same code path that carries
  the documented tuple-adapter use-after-free hazard (see the `inject_defaults`
  item above). Wants its own task with its own review.

- Tier 0 postcondition propagation: a callee's closed return refinement is
  recorded in `fn_sig.ret` and consumed at call sites, for both `let`-bound and
  inline-argument forms. Relational (parameter-mentioning) postconditions
  remain definition-only. Only postconditions the definition side POSITIVELY
  VERIFIED propagate (an unproven one stays legal but tells callers nothing —
  trusting it would flag correct code); binding constructs now clear shadowed
  refined-scope entries (lambda/`let fn` params, `let` bindings, `match`
  pattern binders); counterexamples render `f$retN` as `f() returns v`.

- ✅ **Self-name-prefixed dotted sibling module unreachable (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml`, `lib/resolver/resolver.ml`, `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, 2026-07-23).** A module could not reference a same-name-prefixed sibling dotted submodule in a multi-file (`MARCH_LIB_PATH`) project — exactly the layout `specs/lang/modules.md`'s "Multi-File Projects" section documents as standard: entry `mod MyApp do ... end` in one file, sibling `mod MyApp.Router do ... end` in another, `MyApp.Router.dispatch(1, 2)` called from `MyApp.main`. Failed with `Unknown module \`Router\`.`; the ONLY previously-working spelling was `alias MyApp.Router as R`. Referencing an unrelated-named sibling (`Other.Router`) always worked, isolating the trigger to the referencing module's own name being a PREFIX of the target's dotted name. **Root cause (three independent bugs, all on this exact path):** (1) `lib/desugar/desugar.ml`'s `strip_entry_self_qual` — which strips a self-qualifying `EntryMod.` prefix so `Foo.bar` inside `mod Foo` converges on TIR's unwrapped-entry bare emission — matched by STRING PREFIX ONLY: `MyApp.Router.dispatch` starts with `MyApp.` so it got mangled to `Router.dispatch`, which resolves to nothing (`Router` is a different top-level module, not a member of `MyApp`). Fixed by checking that the segment right after the stripped prefix actually names something the entry module itself declares (a direct fn/let, or a nested `DMod` — so genuine nesting like `Outer.Inner.wrapped` inside entry `mod Outer` still strips correctly) before stripping. (2) `lib/resolver/resolver.ml`'s `import_refs` took only the FIRST segment of a `use`/`alias` path as the module to load — correct for a submodule nested inside one file (`use Bastion.Channel` where `Channel` nests inside `bastion.march`) but wrong for the documented one-mod-per-file dotted convention (`use MyApp.Router` needs to load `my_app/router.march`, not `my_app.march`). Fixed by trying every dotted prefix of the path, longest (most specific) first, and loading whichever one maps to a real file. (3) `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`'s `DUse`/`Ast.UseSingle` case was a pure no-op for ANY dotted `use A.B` (pre-existing, not specific to the name-collision case — confirmed via an unrelated-name control `use Other.Router` + bare `Router.dispatch` failing identically pre-fix): a single-segment `use Foo` needs no bindings (`Foo.bar` already matches the qualified key directly), but a dotted `use A.B` never re-exported `A.B.bar` under the natural bare-last-segment form `B.bar`. Fixed by re-exporting under the last path segment, mirroring how `alias A.B as C` already re-exports under `C` (a same-segment no-op for single-segment paths, so existing behavior is preserved exactly). Verified: the reported repro now typechecks and runs (interpreted and compiled, prints `3`) via fully-qualified `MyApp.Router.dispatch(...)`, via `use MyApp.Router` + bare `Router.dispatch(...)`, and via the pre-existing `alias` workaround; the unrelated-name sibling case (regression control) still resolves correctly. New regression tests in `test/test_compiler.ml`'s module-system group. Full suite: no new failures.
- ✅ **Two unbounded busy-spins in the actor scheduler fixed (`runtime/march_runtime.c`, `runtime/march_scheduler.c`, 2026-07-23).** `task_wait_done`'s in-scheduler branch (`task_await`/`task_await_unwrap`'s wait path) and `march_sched_wake`'s `PROC_PARKED`-wait spin had no deadline/fallback — either could peg an OS thread at ~100% CPU forever if the proc it waited on never made progress in bounded time (suspected cause of multi-hour CPU-pegged compiled-test hangs under host oversubscription; see `project_scheduler_unbounded_spin_hangs` memory for the full investigation). Both now spin-yield for a generous grace period, then fall back to a cheap `nanosleep(1ms)`-based poll instead of a tight spin — `task_await`'s wait-forever semantics are unchanged, only how the wait is performed. `task_wait_done`'s grace period is wall-clock (`CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, 5s), not an iteration count — an iteration-count version was tried first and A/B-benchmarked to silently collapse legitimate heavy-parallelism throughput (`bench/par_fib.march`-shaped workload: ~390% CPU → ~15% CPU) because each `march_sched_yield()` round trip takes longer when the scheduler is genuinely busy, so a fixed iteration count elapses in far less wall-clock time than intended. `march_sched_wake`'s spin stays iteration-count-based (its transition is a bounded, wait-free-ish critical section independent of arbitrary user-code progress, confirmed safe via the same A/B benchmark). Verified: full suite (`run_compiler` 538 / `run_eval` 240 / `run_codegen` 444 / `run_stdlib` 816, one pre-existing unrelated failure — a 100%-reproducible macOS/ASAN dyld-init livelock confirmed independent of March via a bare `clang -fsanitize=address` hello-world reproducing it identically), `bench/list_ops.march` + `bench/parallel.march` no regression, `par_fib`-shaped fork-join workload below its scaling limits restored to full parallel throughput. **Known follow-up (not fixed here, tracked separately):** profiling a fork-join workload that crosses March's task-count scaling cliff (~14K→22K concurrent `task_spawn`s) found this CPU-backoff patch alone does not fix that cliff — it cuts CPU usage as designed but the workload still doesn't complete, revealing a separate, deeper LIFO-starvation bug in `sched_loop`'s local-deque dispatch policy (an awaiter re-queuing itself starves its own not-yet-run sibling task). See `project_scheduler_unbounded_spin_hangs` memory, "second follow-up" section, for the live-profiled evidence and candidate fix directions.

- ✅ **`Csv.read_all`/`Csv.each_row_with_header` compiled-only crash REGRESSED a second time — this time via an opaque native-handle tagging bug, not the original niche/Boxed misclassification (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, 2026-07-23).** A release-readiness check reported `test/native/csv_niche_row.march` (the regression test for the 2026-07-21 `CsvRow` niche-repr fix, see the entry below) crashing again with exit 138/139, nondeterministically — sometimes a clean-looking `row count: 0` (no crash), sometimes SIGBUS, sometimes SIGSEGV, across otherwise-identical runs of the same binary. The report hypothesized a reoccurrence of the niche/Boxed misclassification (via the large "same-short-name colliding types" FQN-dispatch-identity work landed 2026-07-21) — investigated and RULED OUT: `MARCH_REPR_AUDIT`-style direct instrumentation confirmed `Csv.CsvRow`'s match sites still classify correctly as `Niche(tagged=false)`, not colliding, at every `case` site. **Actual root cause, found by bisecting `git log -- lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml lib/tir/llvm_builtins.ml` between the 2026-07-21 fix and current `main`:** commit `646f895d` ("fix(codegen): coerce builtin call args to declared param types", 2026-07-22 — see the entry below) added a fallback that coerces a builtin call's arguments to the callee's declared C parameter type (via `Llvm_builtins.builtin_param_llvm_tys`, parsed from `declare_sig`) whenever `top_fn_param_tys` has no entry. `csv_open`'s returned "handle" is a raw native `FILE*` pointer smuggled through March's type system as a plain `Int` (an established, pre-existing convention shared by `file_open`, TCP handles, etc.) — `csv_open`/`csv_next_row`/`csv_close`'s C `declare_sig`s all type this parameter as `ptr`. The new fallback saw the LLVM-level mismatch (`handle`'s TIR type `Int` lowers to `"i64"`, the declared param is `"ptr"`) and applied `Llvm_ctx.coerce`'s `("i64","ptr")` case — the boxing convention for a genuine `Int` value entering a generic/erased slot, `(v<<1)|1` then `inttoptr` — corrupting the handle's real heap address before every `csv_next_row`/`csv_close` call. Confirmed via `--emit-llvm`: `%cv9 = shl i64 %ld8, 1 / %cv10 = or i64 %cv9, 1 / %cv11 = inttoptr i64 %cv10 to ptr / call ptr @march_csv_next_row(ptr %cv11)`. Explains every symptom: the tagged/shifted address is garbage, so each call reads whatever happens to live at `(h*2)+1` — sometimes decoding as a plausible-looking immediate EOF (`row count: 0`, no crash), sometimes a wild pointer (SIGBUS/SIGSEGV), depending on what's mapped there. A scratch `git worktree` bisect at `646f895d~1` vs `646f895d` reproduced clean-pass-every-time vs. crash-most-of-the-time deterministically. **Fix:** restricted the new fallback (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, the `EApp` builtin-arg-coercion match) to the ONE direction the original bug actually needed — `"ptr"` (generic/erased slot) → `"i64"`/`"double"` (native scalar param), i.e. unboxing a tagged/boxed value for a builtin's native scalar C parameter. The reverse direction (a concrete `"i64"`/`"double"` argument flowing into a declared `"ptr"` param — the opaque-handle case) now passes through with its own representation unchanged, matching the pre-regression behavior exactly: LLVM's opaque-pointer calls don't require the call-site argument types to textually match the callee's declared prototype (confirmed both by re-extracting the pre-regression `.ll` — `call ptr @march_csv_next_row(i64 %ld5)` against a `declare ptr @march_csv_next_row(ptr %handle)` — and by the 2026-07-22 entry below's own note that clang/LLVM accepts this since `i64`/`ptr` share the same register class). Verified: `test/native/csv_niche_row.march` produces the correct 5-line output across 5+ repeated runs (previously nondeterministic); the ORIGINAL bug `646f895d` fixed (`Some((top, _)) -> int_to_string(top)` printing `7` instead of `3`) still prints `3`, confirming the fix doesn't regress the case it was protecting. Full suite green: compiler 537 / eval 240 / stdlib 780 quick, plus a full (non-`-q`) stdlib pass with output redirected to a plain file (avoiding a `dune`/pipe-buffering artifact that produced flaky `[FAIL]` noise on unrelated `Vault.update`/`Toml`/`record_put` Slow tests under heavy concurrent-session CPU contention on the build host — confirmed those same tests pass cleanly on BOTH the fixed and the pre-fix `llvm_emit.ml`, i.e. pre-existing host flakiness, not caused by this change). One pre-existing, unrelated codegen-suite failure (`llvm_ir_validity_gate`: `test/native/signal_term_suppress.march`/`signal_watch.march` — "Unknown module `Signal`") reproduces identically via `--check` alone (pure typecheck, before this fix's code path ever runs), confirming it predates and is unaffected by this change. Given this is the SECOND time this exact class of bug has hit CSV's compiled path (both times via changes elsewhere in the codegen pipeline, not `stdlib/csv.march` itself), a dedicated unit test asserting `Repr.niche_repr_of_concrete`'s classification of `Csv.CsvRow` directly (not just the end-to-end golden) would catch a third recurrence of the FIRST bug class faster; this fix's own class (builtin-arg representation coercion for opaque-handle builtins) has no such direct unit coverage either and would benefit from one asserting `Llvm_builtins.builtin_param_llvm_tys`-driven coercion is skipped for every opaque-handle builtin (`csv_*`, `file_*` handle params, `tcp_*`, …) — filed as a follow-up, not added here to keep this fix minimal and reviewable against the bisected regression.

- ✅ **Browser cookbook/playground REPL was missing the `Vault` stdlib module — `docs/cookbook/vault.md`'s examples errored with `no member 'new' in module 'Vault'` (2026-07-23, `js/march_browser.ml`, `scripts/gen-browser-stdlib.py`).** Reported by a user hitting the runtime error live on the docs site. `vault.march` is a fully working, fully-tested stdlib module — the interpreter builtins (`lib/eval/eval.ml`) are correct — but it was never added to either `js/march_browser.ml`'s `browser_stdlib_files` load-list (what the browser interpreter session actually loads) or `scripts/gen-browser-stdlib.py`'s `FILES` list (which bundles `docs/assets/march_stdlib.js`, the JS blob the browser reads module source from). So the module simply never entered the playground's environment, and `Vault.new`/etc. resolved as missing members on an otherwise-real module namespace (the exact error text comes from `eval.ml`'s `"no member '%s' in module '%s'"`, which only fires when the qualifier IS a known module). **Fix:** added `"vault.march"` to both lists, regenerated `docs/assets/march_stdlib.js` (`python3 scripts/gen-browser-stdlib.py`), rebuilt `docs/assets/march.js` (`dune build js/march_browser.bc.js`). Verified against the rebuilt bundle directly in Node (not just the OCaml test suite, since this bug is browser-bundle-only and the OCaml suite doesn't exercise it): both the "Basic CRUD" snippet and the full rate-limiter example from `docs/cookbook/vault.md` now run correctly end-to-end (`hit 1: true / hit 2: true / hit 3: true / hit 4 (over limit): false`). **Same report, second bug:** `docs/cookbook/capabilities.md`'s "sandboxed plugin runner" example called `sandbox_eval`, which never existed anywhere in the compiler or stdlib — pure illustrative pseudocode that could never run in the same playground REPL (`unbound variable: sandbox_eval`). Stubbed it inline (`fn sandbox_eval(code : String) : Result(String, String) do Ok("evaluated: " ++ code) end`) so the example compiles and runs as written; the example's actual teaching point (the `PluginCap` capability gate around `run`) is unaffected. Verified: prints `result: evaluated: 1 + 1` with no error. Both landed on `main` (merge commit `88402bcc`). Not investigated as part of this fix, found only while separately looking into an unrelated test hang: `test_erased_option_niche_fbip_no_underflow_compiled` (`test/test_codegen.ml:7668`) intermittently hangs at ~100% CPU under heavy host contention — filed as its own P1 entry below, not a docs bug.
- ✅ **`self()` was typechecked as plain `Int` instead of the enclosing actor's `Pid[state_ty]` (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, 2026-07-22).** Found auditing `specs/lang/actors.md` against the compiler: the global builtin registration (`("self", Mono t_int)`, near line 2141) meant any use of `self()` where a `Pid` was expected — `is_alive(self())`, or passing it through a message field annotated/expected as `Pid` — failed to unify with a confusing "expected `Pid` but got `Int`", even though `self()` is dynamically a valid `Pid` (the `eval.ml` builtin already returns `VPid`, erroring only outside a handler). Fix mirrors how `state` is already scoped inside a handler: `DActor`'s handler-checking loop (`check_decl`, the `Ast.DActor` arm) now also binds `self` in `handler_env` to `Pid(state_ty)` — the exact same type `spawn(name)` produces for this actor — shadowing the global placeholder only within the handler body, where `self()` is actually valid at runtime. Verified via two repros: `is_alive(self())` inside a handler, and `self()` round-tripped unannotated through a message field to another handler's `is_alive` call — both now typecheck and run, printing `true`. `scripts/run-tests.sh -q` (780 tests) green. `specs/lang/actors.md`'s `self()` section restored its explicit-annotation example (previously worked around by leaving `caller`/reply fields unannotated to dodge this exact gap).
- ✅ **`Actor.call`'s reply value was silently corrupted compiled — a scalar reply came back as its raw tagged bit pattern, not the real value (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, 2026-07-22).** Found auditing `specs/lang/actors.md`'s "Synchronous request/response" section against its own pinned golden: `test/native/actor_counter.march` compiled printed `value=11` where both the interpreter and the golden (`test/native/actor_counter.expected`) say `value=5` — `11 = (5<<1)|1`, the standard tagged-immediate encoding of `5`, read back raw. **Root cause:** `Actor.call`'s `Result('a, String)` return type is deliberately left as an unresolved type variable in TIR — the reply's payload type genuinely can't be known statically (any handler can reply with anything), so pattern-matching `Ok(v)` correctly extracts the field generically as `ptr`, and `march_actor_reply`'s call site (already special-cased in `llvm_emit.ml`) correctly tags a scalar reply into that uniform `ptr` representation before it crosses the mailbox's `void*` boundary. The bug was one step further downstream: the general `EApp` call-emission path only coerces an argument to its callee's declared LLVM parameter type for *user-defined* functions (via `ctx.top_fn_param_tys`) — every other scalar-consuming builtin (`int_not`, the bitwise ops, …) works around this with its own dedicated match arm that calls `emit_atom_as` explicitly, but `int_to_string`/`bool_to_string`/`float_to_string` had none. So the `ptr`-typed extracted value flowed straight into `call ptr @march_int_to_string(ptr %v)` against a `declare ... (i64 %n)` signature — a declared-type mismatch LLVM's parser doesn't reject and that "works" at the ABI level (`ptr`/`i64` share a register), silently handing the C function the raw tagged bits instead of the untagged integer. Confirmed via `--emit-llvm` on the failing golden (showing the exact mismatched call) and by checking that `int_to_string`'s normal (non-erased, concretely-typed) call sites were unaffected (their argument is already natively `i64`, so the missing coercion is a no-op there). **Fix:** added dedicated `EApp` match arms for `int_to_string`/`bool_to_string`/`float_to_string`, each calling `emit_atom_as ctx "i64"/"double"` before the C call — mirroring the existing `int_not`/bitwise-op pattern exactly. Scoped to these three builtins (the only scalar-consuming ones with no existing dedicated arm); the general per-builtin argument-coercion gap this exposes is broader but out of scope for this fix. Verified: `test/native/actor_counter.march` compiled now matches `.expected` (`value=5`); the other wired `Actor.call` golden, `test/native/actor_call_timeout.march`, is unaffected (still matches `timeout_ok`); normal `int_to_string`/`bool_to_string`/`float_to_string` calls smoke-tested unchanged. Full suite green: compiler 527 / eval 238 / codegen 438 (including the LLVM IR validity gate over the full native corpus) / stdlib clean. This was a live CI gap: `scripts/run-tests.sh`'s fast path invokes the four Alcotest driver binaries directly and bypasses dune's ~57 native compile-and-run rules entirely (see `feedback_release_ci_verification_gaps` project note) — a real `dune build && dune runtest` would have failed on this golden, but the fast-path runner used throughout recent sessions could not catch it.
- ✅ **Typechecker soundness gap: I/O builtins registered with an unconstrained polymorphic `Result` error type instead of their real concrete error type (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, 2026-07-22)** — `file_read`'s signature was `poly1 (fun e -> TArrow (t_string, t_result t_string e))`: the error type `e` was a free type variable, so a function declared `Result(_, String)` doing `let? content = File.read(path)` typechecked with **zero diagnostics** and then panicked at runtime (`builtin ++: expected two strings`) the moment the bound error was used as a `String` — its real runtime value is always a `File.FileError` constructor (`NotFound`/`Permission`/`IsDirectory`/`NotEmpty`/`IoError`, per `eval.ml`'s `file_error_of_unix`/`file_error_of_sys`), never an arbitrary caller-chosen type. Swept `typecheck.ml` for the same `poly1 (fun e -> ...)`-around-`t_result` shape and found it was a **repeated pattern**, not a one-off: fixed 8 File builtins (`file_read/write/append/delete/copy/rename/stat/open`), 5 Dir builtins (`dir_list/mkdir/mkdir_p/rmdir/rm_rf` — same `FileError`), `csv_open` (concrete `Csv.CsvError` — confirmed not niche-shaped, so the bare name is fine, unlike `CsvRow` which needs the `Csv.`-qualified name for niche-repr lookup), and — after confirming via `eval.ml` and the runtime C (`runtime/march_tls.c`'s `make_err`, `runtime/march_http.c`, `runtime/march_runtime.c`'s process/tcp builtins) that their real error is always a bare `String` in both interpreted and compiled backends — tightened `tcp_connect/send_all/recv_all/recv_http/recv_http_headers/recv_chunk/recv_chunked_frame`, `http_parse_response`, `process_spawn_sync/lines/async`, and `tls_client_ctx/server_ctx/connect/accept/read/write` from unconstrained `e` to `Mono t_string`, matching the precedent already set by `tcp_listen`/`tcp_accept`/`dns_resolve`/`tcp_recv_exact` (already `Mono`). Verified via the real compiler CLI that the reported repro (`Result(_, String)` + `let? = File.read`) now produces a labeled type-mismatch diagnostic instead of compiling, and that normal usage (unannotated return type, or `Err(e) -> to_string(e)`) still compiles and runs correctly. **A pre-existing test encoded the exact bug and had to be fixed, not the other way around:** `test_cap_deterministic_file_read_ok` (`cap_pure_no_extern_det` suite) declared `fn load(path : String) : Result(String, String) do file_read(path) end` — annotating the very type mismatch this fix now (correctly) rejects.
