Removed the now-invalid `Result(String, String)` annotation (the test's actual point is the `cap deterministic` capability check, not `file_read`'s error shape); left the return type to inference. **Noteworthy side-finding, NOT fixed here (separate, pre-existing bug):** `stdlib/dns.march`'s `DnsError` shares the bare constructor name `NotFound` with `File.FileError` — before this fix, `Err(NotFound(p))` against a `file_read` result "worked" (typechecked) only because the free `e` absorbed whichever type the ambiguous ctor happened to resolve to — with `e` now concrete, that same code correctly fails to typecheck (`expected DnsError but got FileError`) since it's a genuinely ambiguous global-namespace ctor collision that should be qualified. **Also confirmed a deeper, separate divergence while investigating (not fixed — out of scope for this signature fix):** the *compiled* backend's runtime C for `file_read`/`file_write`/`dir_list` etc. (`runtime/march_runtime.c`'s `mk_err_errno`/`mk_err_cstr`) always returns a **bare String** error, never a `FileError`-tagged constructor — `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`'s hardcoded `native_result_ty` table independently pins these to `Result(_, String)` for codegen/ABI purposes. So `File.FileError`'s structured-constructor error is currently an **interpreter-only** behavior; a compiled program pattern-matching `Err(NotFound(path))` would typecheck (correctly, per this fix) but get the wrong runtime shape if compiled. No test or benchmark currently exercises File/Dir/Csv compiled (`grep` across `test/native/*.march` and `bench/*.march` confirms), so this fix introduces no new compiled-mode regression, but the interpreter/compiled parity gap itself remains unaddressed. Regression test: `test_letq_file_read_wrong_error_type` in `test/test_compiler.ml`'s `let?` suite (written test-first, confirmed RED against the original `poly1` signature, GREEN after the fix). **Full suite green: 527 compiler (was 526, +1 regression test) / 238 eval / 438 codegen / 780 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march.**

- ✅ **`Array.push`/`RRB.push` SIGSEGV'd compiled on the SECOND `Float` push — a wildcard-discarded constructor field never resolves through monomorphization (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, `lib/tir/lower_match.ml`, `test/native/rrb_push_float.march`, 2026-07-21).** Found while fixing an unrelated `Parallel.preduce`/`psum_float` LLVM type mismatch: `RRB.push(RRB.push(RRB.empty(), 1.5), 2.5)` compiled (`--opt 0` and `--opt 2` both) then SIGSEGV'd inside `march_decrc` on the second push, faulting address `0x3ff8000000000008` — the IEEE-754 bit pattern of `1.5` plus `march_decrc`'s tag-field offset (`+8`), i.e. a raw unboxed double being read as if it were a heap pointer. **Root cause:** `Array.push` calls `lst_len(tail)`, whose inner `go` discards the list-head field via a wildcard pattern (`Cons(_, t) -> go(t, acc + 1)`). `lower_match.ml`'s pattern-matrix compiler (`compile_matrix_impl`) gives EVERY constructor-field synthetic variable the `TVar "_"` placeholder type at creation. A NAMED field (`Cons(h, t)`) gets rebound downstream (`bind_trivial_pat`) to its real type via `Lower_state.ty_of_span` — the typechecker's own tracked unification variable for that pattern occurrence — which then flows through `mono.ml`'s substitution normally (`subst_branch` substitutes every `br_var`, but only a REAL tracked tyvar, not the `TVar "_"` sentinel `Mono.has_tvar` deliberately excludes, actually resolves). A WILDCARD field is never rebound, so its synthetic var keeps `TVar "_"` forever; `Rc_types.needs_rc (TVar "_")` conservatively answers `true` ("assume heap-carrying"), so Perceus emits a `dec_rc` on it. The intended safety net — `llvm_emit.ml`'s `EDecRC`/`EIncRC` handlers only fire the runtime call when the LOADED value's LLVM type is `"ptr"` — does NOT catch this, because a polymorphic ADT's OWN generic-typed field is *always* loaded as `"ptr"` under the compiler's uniform-slot convention (`llvm_case.ml`, "load every field via `ce_fields`"), regardless of the field's true concrete type; a genuinely scalar Float crossing that boundary is stored via a plain bitcast (`Llvm_ctx.coerce ("double","ptr")` — reinterpret bits, no `march_alloc_float` box), so `dec_rc`'s `ty = "ptr"` guard sees exactly what it would see for a real heap pointer and fires anyway. **Two-part fix:** (1) `typecheck.ml`'s `infer_pattern`, `PatWild` arm — record the wildcard's freshly-inferred unification variable into `env.type_map` keyed by its own span, exactly mirroring `PatVar` (previously only `PatVar` recorded a span → the wildcard's inferred type was computed but silently discarded). (2) `lower_match.ml`'s `compile_matrix_impl` — build each tag group's `sub_vars` by looking up `Lower_state.ty_of_span env (span_of_pat field_pattern)` instead of defaulting unconditionally to `Lower_types.unknown_ty`. Together, a discarded field now flows through `mono.ml`'s existing substitution machinery exactly like a named one — `needs_rc` sees the real, concrete (post-mono) field type, and Perceus stops emitting the spurious `dec_rc`/`inc_rc` in the first place. Verified: the exact reported 2-push repro now runs clean (`RRB.length` prints `2`, exit 0) at both `--opt 0` and `--opt 2`. New compiled native golden `test/native/rrb_push_float.march` + `.expected` + `test/dune` rule (3 sequential Float pushes, `RRB.length` must print `3`) — RED pre-fix (SIGSEGV, no output) / GREEN post-fix. **CORRECTION (2026-07-21, same day, `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`/`lib/tir/llvm_ctx.ml`/`lib/tir/llvm_toplevel.ml`, `test/native/float_generic_field_abi.march`):** this fix's own "Update after rebasing onto `origin/main`" note (previously here) claimed `origin/main`'s independent "float-boxing Stage 2" work alone also resolved the sibling `Array.get`/`RRB.get` wrong-value bug — **that claim was wrong**, based on a verification that did not actually reproduce against a clean `origin/main` build. A scratch `git worktree` build of `origin/main` HEAD, with none of this correction's changes, reproducibly printed `0.` (not `1.5`/`7.`) for `Array.get`/`RRB.fold` on a `Float` array/vector — the wrong-value bug was never actually fixed. Real root cause: `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`'s `EApp` (direct call) emission never coerced an argument's ACTUAL emitted LLVM representation to the callee's DECLARED parameter type — it just passed through whatever `emit_atom` returned. A value extracted from a generic/polymorphic ADT field is always `ptr` at the LLVM level (boxed for scalars, per the uniform-slot convention above) regardless of the concrete instantiation; flowing directly into a monomorphic callee's native scalar parameter (e.g. `Array.push$PVec_Float$Float`'s `elem : Float` → LLVM `double`) produced a call site emitting `call ptr @Array.push$PVec_Float$Float(ptr %v, ptr %elem)` against a callee declared `(ptr, double)` — a caller/callee representation mismatch that LLVM's parser did not reject but that made the callee always read `0.0` for the element. `Array.from_list`'s internal fold hits this on every pushed element. **Fix:** new `Llvm_ctx.top_fn_param_tys : (string, Tir.ty list) Hashtbl.t` (populated alongside the existing `top_fn_ret_ty`/`top_fn_nparams` in `lib/tir/llvm_toplevel.ml`); `EApp`'s non-apply-fn argument path now coerces each argument (`Llvm_ctx.coerce`) to the callee's declared parameter type when known and arity matches — a no-op for the already-correct common case. **Verified for real this time:** both `RRB.fold(RRB.slice(RRB.from_list([1.5, 2.5, 3.0]), 0, 3), 0.0, fn (a,b) -> a +. b)` and the equivalent `Array.get`-based repro now print the correct `7.`; a battery of ~15 isolated repros (bare `Array.get`, `Array.push` chains, `Array.from_list`, standalone `List(Float)` wildcard-discard traversals, an `Int` control) all match interpreted output exactly. Full suite clean (`run_compiler`/`run_eval`/`run_codegen` all pass; `run_stdlib` passes except one pre-existing, load-dependent ASAN-initialization livelock — `test_compiled_sanitize_clean_exit`, confirmed via `lldb` to hang inside `__asan::AsanInitInternal` before any March code runs, reproduces in isolation under heavy concurrent machine load, unrelated to this change). New compiled/native regression test `test/native/float_generic_field_abi.march` + `.expected` (covers both bugs plus an `Int` control), wired into `test/dune`. The previously-filed separate P0 item for `Array.get`/`RRB.get` is now genuinely resolved.

- ✅ **`Vault.update` re-investigated after a third "still broken" report (2026-07-22) — confirmed already fixed; added `--opt 0` regression coverage.** The report's crash reproduced against `/Users/80197052/code/march` (the bare repo directory), which was on local branch `hcr-dispatch-race` at `dc6912e0` — confirmed via `git merge-base --is-ancestor` to contain neither of the two fixes below, with no `origin/main` merge in its reflog. A clean `origin/main` HEAD (`83846efa`), rebuilt from scratch with the CAS cache cleared, runs the minimal repro, the full `test_compiled_vault_update` repro, and the cookbook rate-limiter example correctly at `--opt 0`/default/`--opt 2`. Building the stale commit in an isolated scratch worktree reproduced the exact reported backtrace (`address=0xb`, `march_vault_update + 44`, `ldr w8, [x8, #0x8]`) — the original pre-fix boxed-Option read — confirming the report was against unmerged code, not a new bug. New test `test_compiled_vault_update_opt0` (`test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`, case 19) pins the `--opt 0` path specifically since that's what the report used.

- ✅ **`Vault.update` SIGSEGV'd (exit 139) compiled — decoded `march_vault_get`'s niche Option as boxed, and used a stale closure-invocation ABI (`runtime/march_extras.c`, 2026-07-21).** Found while auditing `docs/cookbook/vault.md` against the compiler: `Vault.update(store, key, fn n -> n + 1)` crashed with no output whenever compiled, for both an inline lambda and a named top-level fn callback (interpreted was always correct — compiled-only). **Bug 1 (the crash):** `march_vault_get` returns a **niche-encoded** `Option(ptr)` — `None = NULL`, `Some(v) = v` itself (`make_some`/`make_none` at `:312-313`; no boxed tag/payload struct) — but `vault_update` decoded it as a **boxed** Option, reading a tag word at `cur+8` and a payload pointer at `cur+16`. For a scalar value (e.g. `Int`, tagged `(n<<1)|1`, so never a valid heap pointer) this read 8 bytes past the end of the value's own machine word, landing on an unmapped page → SIGSEGV on the very first access, before the closure was ever invoked. **Bug 2 (the closure-invocation call itself):** the code additionally called `fn(env, v)` with `env` read from a bogus `f+24` field, instead of the actual convention (`call_closure_1`, `march_http_internal.h`'s `closure_fn_t`) of passing the closure struct pointer itself as the first arg — `fn(f, arg)`. Investigation was initially done against a several-hundred-commit-stale local branch; **merging `origin/main` before landing turned up a THIRD, superseding wrinkle:** upstream (`specs/plans/2026-07-17-closure-scalar-abi-uniform-ptr.md`) had, in the interim, moved a closure apply-fn's *parameters* onto the same uniform-ptr ABI its return already used — the apply fn now conditionally untags a tagged scalar itself at entry (bare heap pointers pass through unaffected) and re-tags on return (`lib/tir/llvm_toplevel.ml`'s `emit_fn`: "Apply wrappers use the uniform ptr ABI for params too... The entry prologue below unboxes such params back to double/i64 for the body"). An initial version of this fix (written pre-merge, when params were still native/untagged) manually untagged the value before calling `fn` — that additional untag, once merged onto the new upstream ABI, double-processed odd/tagged values and silently produced wrong results (`3` incorrectly became `4`, confirmed by a fresh disassemble-and-recompute pass post-merge). **Final fix:** pass `cur` straight through unchanged to `fn(f, cur)` — the closure now handles the uniform-value conditional untag/retag entirely on its own, on both the param and return sides. Verified: the exact minimal repro (inline lambda + named fn) prints `2`/`3` compiled, matching interpreted; a `String`-valued `Vault.update` (`s ++ "!"`) round-trips correctly compiled (guards the heap-pointer branch); `docs/cookbook/vault.md`'s rate-limiter example runs end-to-end compiled with output identical to interpreted. New permanent regression test `test_compiled_vault_update` (`test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`, `adversarial-regressions` Slow group) exercises Int (lambda + named fn) and String, asserting exit 0. `scripts/run-tests.sh -q` (780 tests) and the targeted Slow Vault tests pass post-merge.

- ✅ **TCO self-call freed a freshly-allocated forwarded argument one instruction before reusing it — FIXED (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`, 2026-07-21).** `stdlib/toml.march`'s `string_to_int_digits` (a `String.split`/`String.join` accumulator recursion — `str_to_int(String.join(rest, ""), acc*10+digit_val(c))`) silently miscompiled: `Toml.get_int(_, "port")` on `"port = 9000"` returned `9` (first digit only) compiled while the interpreter (which never runs the Perceus/TCO TIR pipeline) got `9000` right — no crash, no error, just a wrong answer. **Root cause:** Perceus classifies `str_to_int`'s first parameter as borrowed (its only use is `string_split(s, "")`, itself a borrowed-position call), so a self-tail-call whose forwarded arg is dead afterward gets a caller-side post-call `EDecRC` — correct for real (non-TCO) recursion, where that DecRC only fires once the whole nested call has fully returned, by which point nothing below still needs the reference. The TCO loop-rewrite (`llvm_emit.ml`'s two self-TCO interception cases, ~line 680 `ELet`-wrapped and ~line 730 no-temp `ESeq`) ran that DecRC EAGERLY, immediately before storing the very same value into the parameter slot for the next iteration — freeing a freshly-allocated, uncompensated value (refcount 1, no prior `IncRC`, unlike a `Cons(_, t)`-style field extracted from a still-borrowed container, which DOES get a compensating `IncRC` and so survives the same eager-decrement pattern by accident) one instruction before it was reused, corrupting every iteration after the first. **Fix:** both TCO dec-chain executors now skip any `EDecRC`/`EAtomicDecRC`/`EIncRC`/`EAtomicIncRC` whose target variable is itself one of the tail-call's forwarded `args` — that reference is being handed off into the next iteration's slot, not released. Verified against a minimal standalone repro (no stdlib dependency) reproducing 9000→9 compiled/9000 interpreted, confirmed BROKEN pre-fix and FIXED post-fix by toggling `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` alone; the previously-working `Cons(_, t)`-style list-traversal TCO pattern (`test/native/*` and the codegen suite) is unaffected — confirmed via a second standalone repro. **Two other compiled-only bugs surfaced in the same docs audit (`task_54ced6d5` string_to_float/Option(Float) segfault, `task_eabe2334` Csv.read_all/each_row_with_header returns 0 rows) were checked and do NOT share this root cause** — `Csv.collect_loop`'s accumulator is forwarded via a constructor allocation (`Cons(fields, acc)`), not an `EApp` call, so it never reaches Perceus's borrowed-arg post-call-Dec logic at all (verified: a `Cons`-accumulator repro shaped like `collect_loop` was NOT broken pre-fix). Both were independently root-caused and fixed on `main` by a separate session while this branch was in flight — see the `string_to_float` and `Csv` niche-repr type-qualification entries elsewhere in this section; neither is a TCO/Perceus bug. New regression tests: `test/native/tco_fresh_arg_decrc.{march,expected}` (the minimal, stdlib-independent repro — the focused compiler regression test) and `test/native/toml_get_int.{march,expected}` (the actual `Toml.get_int` reproducer — no compiled/native Toml coverage existed before this; `test/stdlib/test_toml.march` via `test_stdlib_march.ml` only runs interpreted), both wired into `test/dune` as native-compile-and-run golden diffs. `scripts/run-tests.sh -q` (779 tests) and a full `dune build @runtest` (native golden corpus incl. the two new tests) both green; remaining full-suite failures (doctest network-bind, `forge check` qualified-call-cycle, property-test generator) are pre-existing and unrelated, confirmed by grep against the failure output for anything TCO/Perceus/toml-related (none).

- ✅ **`Parallel.preduce`/`preduce_n`/`psum_float` compiled with a Float accumulator: LLVM type mismatch (`'%cvN' defined with type 'i64' but expected 'double'`) — FIXED (2026-07-21, `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`).** Root cause: `task_await_unwrap`/`task_await`'s codegen had no `inner_ty = "double"` case for a `Task(Float)`'s inner type at all — it fell into the generic `else` fallback, mislabeling a raw i64-typed SSA value as `"double"` without converting it, so whatever downstream code consumed the mislabeled value hit an LLVM type error (the closure ABI's generic `"double"->"ptr"` coerce, `march_alloc_float`, is what surfaced it, at whichever call site happened to run next). `task_await`'s big comment block had already flagged `"double"` as "ABI-broken through the void*-returning trampoline, separate follow-up" when the sibling i64/ptr bugs were fixed — this closes that follow-up. **Fix:** both builtins gained a `"double"` branch. No runtime changes needed and no new box type: the float-boxing Stage-2 flip (see this file's "open-items master plan... Stage 2 atomic flip" entry) had already extended the closure apply-fn ABI to box a `Float` return value via `march_alloc_float` before handing it to the trampoline, so the pointer `task_spawn`'s thunk hands the trampoline is ALREADY a genuine heap address — the tag/untag round trip through `task[3]` was already lossless, exactly like the existing `"ptr"` case; `task_await_unwrap`/`task_await`'s `"double"` branch just needed to recover that pointer the same way the `"ptr"` case does and then unbox it with the paired `march_unbox_float`. (An earlier version of this fix, written before the float-boxing Stage-2 work was rebased in, invented its own dedicated `march_task_spawn_thunk_float`/`march_float_task_box` boxing mechanism — dropped once rebasing revealed it duplicated the now-canonical infrastructure.) Verified: the exact reported repro (`Parallel.psum_float(RRB.from_list([1.5, 2.5, 3.0]))`) compiles cleanly; a raw `task_spawn`/`task_await_unwrap` round trip over `2.75`/`100000000.25` (values that would trip the PRE-float-boxing lossy bit-shift this bug was originally diagnosed against) prints and sums correctly. `RRB.push`/`Array.push` on `Float` elements — which crashed compiled on the second push while this fix was originally being developed, and looked like a second, separate bug — turned out to *also* already be fixed by the same float-boxing Stage-2 work once rebased onto it. **`Parallel.psum_float`'s actual practical repro and `docs/cookbook/parallel-data.md`'s example are STILL BROKEN compiled, but not by this bug:** verifying end-to-end surfaced a third, more severe, pre-existing bug (also independent of this fix, also reproduces on unmodified `origin/main`) in `RRB.fold`'s own TCO'd loop — see this section's new P0 entry ("A tail-recursive function with a `Float` accumulator that also carries a heap-pointer parameter...").

- ✅ **`Csv.read_all`/`Csv.each_row_with_header` silently returned zero rows compiled (2026-07-21).** Found auditing `docs/cookbook/files.md`'s CSV example: interpreted printed the correct row count and the docs' `Sales.sum_column`-style aggregation summed correctly, but compiled silently returned 0 rows / a wrong total with no error, and `each_row_with_header`'s per-row callback never fired. Root cause: `stdlib/csv.march`'s `CsvRow = CsvEof | Row(List(String))` is niche-shaped (one nullary ctor + one single-payload ctor whose payload is heap-pointer-shaped, i.e. niche-safe), and the C runtime (`runtime/march_runtime.c`'s `march_csv_next_row`) already encoded it as Niche (`NULL` for `CsvEof`) — but `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`'s hardcoded builtin signature for `csv_next_row` declared its return type as the **bare** `TCon("CsvRow", [])`, while the TIR registers user `ptype`s under their **module-qualified** name (`"Csv.CsvRow"`, per `lib/tir/lower_decls.ml`'s `qtname`). That name mismatch made `Repr.niche_repr_of_concrete`'s `find_variant` (`lib/tir/repr.ml`) miss the type definition at the `match csv_next_row(handle) do ...` call site and silently fall back to `Boxed` (confirmed via a temporary `MARCH_REPR_AUDIT=1` instrumentation showing `family=Boxed` for this site) — under `Boxed`, the compiled match reads a tag byte from the heap object, but a raw `NULL` (the runtime's EOF convention) can only be decoded under `Niche`, so every real row was misread as the nullary `CsvEof` immediately. A pre-existing SEPARATE bug in the same function (`csv_row_result` wrapped the row's fields list in an extra un-tagged `march_alloc(24)` box) was also fixed — that wrapper is now redundant/wrong once `Niche` decoding is correctly selected: `Row(fields)`'s value under Niche IS the fields-list pointer directly, no wrapper. Fix: (1) `typecheck.ml:1640` — `TCon("CsvRow", [])` → `TCon("Csv.CsvRow", [])`; (2) `runtime/march_runtime.c`'s `csv_row_result` returns the fields-list pointer directly instead of allocating a wrapper box. Verified interpreted/compiled parity on the reported repro (row count 4, `Sales`-style sum 35, `each_row_with_header` callback fires for all 3 data rows) and confirmed via a baseline diff (reverting just these two files) that the pre-existing `llvm_ir_validity_gate` corpus failures (`Unknown module CRDT`/`IO` — a `test/test_ir_verify.ml` environment gap, unrelated) are NOT caused by this change. New compiled/native regression test `test/native/csv_niche_row.march` + `.expected` + `test/dune` rule (there was previously zero compiled/native coverage for `Csv` — only the interpreted-only `test/stdlib/test_csv.march`).

- ✅ **`to_string` on a non-primitive was broken compiled (`#<tag:N>` garbage) — now routes through the `Show` dispatch (2026-07-17).** `to_string` is a universal formatter semantically identical to `show` (verified: the interpreter's `to_string`/`show` are byte-identical, and for primitives `Show$T.show` delegates to the very same `*_to_string` C helper). Compiled `to_string` was a codegen builtin (`lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml`) whose non-primitive fallthrough called the generic `march_value_to_string`, which cannot format heap objects. Fix: at lowering (`lib/tir/lower.ml`, the interface-method resolution point) a `to_string(x)` call is dispatched through the SAME `resolve_iface_method "show"` path `println` already uses; a Show-having type gets its real `Show$T.show`, a Show-less type falls through to the old builtin (no regression). The perceus borrowed-field-String RC rule (`lib/tir/perceus.ml`) was taught the rewritten name `Show$String.show` so a borrowed field string is not treated as owned and dropped (RC underflow). Also fixes string interpolation of containers (`"${[1,2,3]}"` desugars to `to_string`). Verified: repros interp==compiled; full suite (eval 233 / compiler 514 / snapshots 29 / codegen 421 goldens unchanged / stdlib 809); ASAN clean on the borrowed-field path; oracle `native/to_string_show` MATCH (new fixture + pinned-stdout anchor, `test/test_oracle.ml`). Sibling items 329 (tuple `Show`) and 348 (unpinned `None` link) remain open — distinct root causes.

- ✅ **`march --fmt` collapsed multi-line lambda bodies to a literal `...` placeholder, producing invalid syntax (`lib/format/format.ml`, `test/test_fmt.ml`, 2026-07-13).** Any lambda whose body needed multi-line rendering (a multi-statement `let`-chain) was formatted via the single-line `expr_inline` pretty-printer, whose `EBlock` case returns the literal string `"..."` for any block with more than one statement — a terse-summary stub meant for other diagnostic contexts, never meant to reach formatter output. Hit the dominant real-world shape (a lambda passed as a trailing call argument, e.g. `List.map(xs, fn x -> ...)`, ~167 occurrences across `stdlib/`), a `let`-bound lambda, a pipe stage (`xs |> List.map(fn x -> ...)`), and a lambda nested through wrapper constructors in tail position (`GenTree(w, Thunk(fn _ -> ...))`, real case in `stdlib/gen.march`). **Fix:** `is_multiline` now recognizes `ELam` (via its body) and `EApp`/`ECon` whose argument list ends — possibly through a chain of wrapper calls/constructors — in such a lambda (`trailing_multiline`). The block renderer gained a matching `ELam` case in `emit_stmt` and a new `emit_call_multiline` for the "trailing block" call shape, which walks the same wrapper chain and closes all opened parens together on one line, matching existing idiomatic stdlib style; `emit_pipe_chain` now uses the same renderer per stage instead of unconditionally calling `expr_inline`. Verified via targeted repros (let-bound/call-arg/pipe-stage/nested-ctor lambdas, each checked for idempotence and re-parseability) plus a full `--fmt` sweep of every `stdlib/*.march` file — zero remaining `-> ...` artifacts. 4 new regression tests in `test/test_fmt.exe`. **Two pre-existing, unrelated `--fmt` round-trip bugs found incidentally and filed separately (not fixed here):** float literals reformatted into scientific notation the parser then rejects (`0.0000009537` → `9.537e-07` → parse error), and record literals that fail to re-parse after formatting (`test_record_literal`, already failing on `HEAD` before this fix). See `specs/progress.md`'s "`march --fmt` multi-line lambda body corruption fixed" entry for the full writeup.
- ✅ **Formatter emitted `{ x = x, y = y }` for record literals/updates — round-tripped source failed to re-parse (2026-07-13).** `lib/format/format.ml`'s `expr_inline` `ERecord`/`ERecordUpdate` cases printed each field as `name = expr`, but the grammar's `record_field_expr` (`lib/parser/parser.mly:1268`) only ever accepted `name : expr` — `=` was never a valid record-literal field separator. Any program containing a record literal or update failed `test/test_fmt.exe`'s "record literal" round-trip check ("formatted source does not parse"). Root-caused via `superpowers:systematic-debugging`: reproduced with `test_fmt.exe -e`, traced the parse failure to `parser.mly`'s single `record_field_expr` production, then found the mismatched `=` in `format.ml`. Fixed both `ERecord` and `ERecordUpdate` field-printing to emit `:` instead of `=`. `test/test_fmt.exe` now 24/24 (was 23/24); full quick suite (`scripts/run-tests.sh -q`) green, 779 tests, no regressions.

- ✅ **Self-referencing block-`let` shadowing miscompiled on the native + JS backends — TWO bugs, both fixed (2026-07-10).** `let x = 10 / let x = x + 5 / …x…` printed 15 interpreted but a silently-wrong **10** compiled (and TDZ-crashed or mis-folded on `--target js`). The idiomatic `let x = x + 1` rebinding pattern, so high severity despite the narrow trigger (RHS must reference the binder being shadowed). Root-caused to TWO independent defects, each with a regression test/golden. **(1) `lib/tir/cprop.ml` (shared TIR, hit native + interp-constant-fold + JS):** the `ELet` arm extended the literal/alias/field env with the new binding but never REMOVED the outer binding's stale mapping when the shadowing RHS was not itself a literal/alias/record (e.g. `let x = x + 5` lowers to an `EApp`). So the outer `x -> 10` literal survived into the body and the body's uses of the NEW `x` were const-propagated to the OLD value; a later Fold baked in the wrong constant. Fix: drop `name` from all three envs (`env`/`avar`/`fenv`) before conditionally re-adding the new binding — mirrors the shadow-filtering the `ECase` arm already did for branch-bound vars; RHS look-ups still use the pre-removal envs so a self-alias can inherit outer field shape; `remove_assoc` on an absent name is a no-op so non-shadowing lets are unchanged. Regression: `test_compiled_let_shadowing_parity` (`test/test_codegen.ml`, `iface_impl_mono_codegen`) — chain `((10+5)*2)=30` (buggy=10). **(2) `lib/tir/js_emit.ml` (JS only, surfaced once cprop couldn't fold to a constant, e.g. a param-driven `mix32`):** a shadowing `let x = <expr using x>` emitted `const x = (…x…)` inside a fresh `{ }` block, and the RHS's `x` resolved to the block-HOISTED inner `const x` in its temporal dead zone → `ReferenceError`. Fix: thread a `name_env` (source name → current JS id) through emission; a binding that shadows an in-scope name is emitted under a fresh unique id (`x$sN`), the RHS is emitted BEFORE the new id is installed (so it reads the outer binding), and pattern-variable bindings in `emit_case` are registered the same way (a scrutinee/branch var on a renamed local now resolves correctly — also fixed `scrut_name` to use the rename). `name_env` is saved/restored around nested (`ELetRec`) fns. Golden: `test/native/js_let_shadowing.{march,expected}` (self-ref chain, a binding read before it is shadowed, and a match arm whose pattern var shadows an outer local — `29/129/101/8`, identical on all three backends). With both fixed, `stdlib/random.march`'s `mix32` was restored from the distinct-name workaround (`a`/`b`/`c`) to the classic self-shadowing splitmix32 idiom (`let x = f(x)` chain) and `seed`'s workaround comment corrected; the `js_random_determinism` golden stayed byte-identical. Full suite green: **451 compiler / 231 eval / 396 codegen / 807 stdlib**; all 10 JS goldens pass. Filed by the Random-on-JS work; a dedicated background session (`task_259907b1`) was also spun up.
- ✅ **macOS nightly CI build intermittently failed on `test/native/node_call_loopback.march` — `tcp_connect: connection refused` on a freshly-`listen()`ed loopback socket (2026-07-21).** The macOS-only `Build (macOS)` step failed rarely (passed 2026-07-07, failed 2026-07-20/07-21) with the client-side `Socket.connect("127.0.0.1", port)` refused immediately after the SAME process had already synchronously completed `tcp_listen(port)` (bind+listen, no async gap) — a real TCP race was ruled out (kernel accept-backlog is live the instant `listen()` returns, independent of whether `accept()` has been called), confirmed by 30 clean local runs plus a concurrent-stress run on this exact macOS/arm64 hardware that reproduced only genuine port collisions (running duplicate copies of the same binary), never a same-process refusal. Traced the July-10 scheduler foreign-wake rewrite (`67452c89`) as a candidate and ruled it out — this test's connect/accept are both plain blocking syscalls on the single green thread that also runs `main`, never touching the foreign-thread wake path. Root cause is external: `actions/runner-images#10924` (macOS 15 runners gained a new, OS-level Local Network TCC permission layer with no way to pre-grant consent in a headless CI session, closed WON'T-FIX by GitHub) and its macOS-15-specific TCP-connect-refused duplicate `actions/runner-images#11901` document exactly this class of intermittent same-machine TCP failure on `macos-15`/`macos-15-arm64` runners. Two changes: (1) `runtime/march_http.c` `march_tcp_connect` no longer hardcodes `"tcp_connect: connection refused"` on any `connect()` failure — it now reports `strerror(errno)`, so a recurrence will show the real cause (e.g. `EHOSTUNREACH`/`EPERM` vs. genuine `ECONNREFUSED`) instead of a misleading fixed string; (2) `test/native/node_call_loopback.march` wraps the test's initial connect in `connect_with_retry` (bounded same-process retry, 5 attempts) since March has no `sleep` builtin to back off with — an immediate retry is the workaround reported to clear this class of runner flake. Verified: new binary matches `node_call_loopback.expected` byte-for-byte across 15 runs; full `scripts/run-tests.sh -q` green (779 tests); no golden/test asserts on the old literal error string (`test/stdlib/test_socket.march`'s "connection refused returns Err" only checks `Err(_)`).
- ✅ **`fn main(cap : Cap(IO)) : ()` — the documented entry-point pattern for receiving the initial IO capability (`specs/lang/capabilities.md`, "Putting it together") — was broken on BOTH backends; now actually works, plus a desugar-time arity/type check rejects any other `main` shape with a clear diagnostic (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml`, `lib/eval/eval.ml`, `lib/tir/llvm_toplevel.ml`, 2026-07-21).** Found auditing `docs/cookbook/capabilities.md` against `specs/lang/capabilities.md` (the authoritative reference it cross-references): the tutorial's own canonical `Main` module example (`fn main(cap : Cap(IO)) : ()`) never worked. **Interpreted:** `Eval.run_module` called every `main` with zero arguments regardless of declared arity (`apply v []`) — a 1-parameter `main` became an unapplied partial closure and silently never ran (no crash, no output, exit looked successful). **Compiled:** `march_spawn_main`'s runtime trampoline (`runtime/march_runtime.c`'s `main_fn_green_thread`) invokes the compiled entry point through a bare 0-argument function pointer; a 1-parameter `main` was still emitted with a real `ptr` parameter in its LLVM signature (`Cap(IO)` erases to a generic `TCon`, which `llvm_ty` maps to `ptr`), so the runtime called it through a mismatched-arity function pointer — undefined behavior at the ABI level, observed as a SIGBUS. Neither backend had ever special-cased `main`'s arity at all; a wrong-arity `main` (e.g. `fn main(x : Int)`) was silently accepted and would have hit the same two failure modes. **Decision: implement the documented pattern for real** (not just diagnose-and-redirect to `root_cap`) — `Cap(IO)` is fully runtime-erased (`null` in LLVM, `VUnit` in the interpreter per "Runtime behaviour"), so wiring it up is cheap, and the reference already assumes it throughout ("The initial `Cap(IO)` is provided implicitly by the runtime to `main()` — there is no `root_cap()` call in user code"). **Fix, three sites:** (1) `Desugar.check_main_signature` (new, called from `desugar_module` alongside the existing `check_app_main_exclusivity`) validates `main`'s first clause has either zero parameters or exactly one parameter whose declared type is syntactically `Cap(IO)`, else a hard `Err.error` naming the found arity and showing both valid forms — this is a pure AST-level check (no type inference needed, since the annotation is already explicit) and doubles as the "reject wrong arity with a clear diagnostic" safety net for any signature this fix doesn't special-case. (2) `Eval.run_module`'s main-dispatch reads the entry module's own `main` `DFn` from `m.mod_decls` to get its arity directly (top-level functions bind to a `VBuiltin` recursion wrapper, not a bare `VClosure`, so arity can't be read off the bound value) and calls with `[VUnit]` when arity is 1, matching `root_cap`'s own `VUnit` representation, or `[]` when 0. (3) `Llvm_toplevel.emit_module`'s native/WASI `@main` emission looks up the real `main` `fn_def`'s parameter count; when it's 1, instead of changing `march_spawn_main`'s runtime ABI, it emits a thin private 0-arg adapter (`@march_main_entry_thunk`) that calls the real mangled main with a literal `ptr null` (the erased root capability) and passes the adapter's address to `march_spawn_main` — the WASM-island `_start` path (browser components, a different non-IO-capability entry convention) and the JS backend (which tolerates the arity mismatch naturally — extra/missing JS args are simply `undefined`, no crash) were both left untouched as out of scope. **Tests:** `test/test_eval.ml`'s new `main_entry_point` group — `main(cap : Cap(IO))` actually executes its body (verified via `Eval.test_capture_buf`, the existing internal stdout-capture hook, not OS-level fd redirection), 0-arity `main` is unregressed, and a wrong-arity `main(x : Int)` is rejected at desugar time (`has_errors`). New native compiled golden `test/native/main_cap_io.{march,expected}` + `test/dune` rule, manually verified to compile and run without a SIGBUS, printing the expected output — pins the codegen thunk. **`specs/lang/capabilities.md` needed no changes**: the pattern it already documents is now accurate; no other doc touched this pattern.

- ✅ **`pfn` privacy silently bypassed for a same-file nested-module member whose bare name collides with an unrelated global — typechecked clean, ran correctly interpreted, produced a garbage value compiled (2026-07-21, `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, `test/test_compiler.ml`).** Found auditing `docs/cookbook/basics.md`'s claim that `pfn` cross-module calls are rejected — TRUE in general (the `c0570d16`/`96ec5969` gates below still hold for every plain repro), but `mod Main do mod Auth do pfn hash(s) do ... end fn verify(...) do hash(plain) == stored end end fn main() do println(Auth.hash("x")) end end` typechecked clean, printed the correct `"x-hashed"` interpreted, and printed a garbage 64-bit value (e.g. `-5808529385363204345`) when compiled — a privacy violation that ALSO corrupts the value, not just a missing diagnostic. **Root cause is DISTINCT from the `opaque type` constructor-hiding gap** (see the RESOLVED P2 entry above — that one was an ungated Pass-1 registration path in `prebind_mod_members`, now fixed separately; this one never reaches Pass-1's or Pass-2's registration at all — both correctly refuse to bind `Auth.hash` for a private member, confirmed by tracing and by a non-colliding-name variant (`Auth.totally_unique_secret_name`) already rejecting correctly pre-fix). The actual bug: `EVar`'s "final fallback" in `infer_expr` (`:3556-3568` pre-fix), meant only to resolve multi-component **interface-method** paths like `"Conduit.Storage.workflow_load"` down to their shorter registered form `"Storage.workflow_load"`, has no floor — it strips a failed qualified lookup ALL the way down to a bare, unqualified name and retries `lookup_var` on that. For `"Auth.hash"` this bottoms out at bare `"hash"`, which IS bound globally (the `Hash` interface's built-in method scheme, `typecheck.ml:1342`) — so the fallback silently resolves the call to the wrong, unrelated global instead of ever reaching the private-member check, and typecheck happily assigns the WRONG type to the call site. Codegen still calls the REAL (private) `Auth.hash` by name (eval's and TIR's own module-qualified name resolution are independent of `env.vars` and correctly find it), so the compiled binary computes with a representation mismatch between what typecheck inferred and what's actually invoked — hence the garbage output. Both defects (missing rejection, garbage compiled value) share this one root cause and both are fixed by the same change. **Fix:** new `is_confirmed_private_qualified` helper (checks `env.local_mods` for an in-file nested module and the module registry's `ex_public` for a cross-file one, factored out of `qualified_error_msg`'s existing detection) is consulted BEFORE the dot-suffix fallback in `EVar`'s resolution; a confirmed-private qualified reference now reports `` Function `hash` is private to module `Auth`. `` immediately instead of falling through. The legitimate interface-method suffix case is untouched (it isn't a confirmed-private qualified reference, so the fallback still runs for it). Verified: the exact repro above now rejects at both `--check` and `--compile`; a legitimate same-module `pfn` call, a legitimate cross-module PUBLIC call, and the original non-colliding private-nested-member case (`test_tc_private_nested_member_diagnostic`) all still pass. New regression test `test_tc_private_nested_member_name_collides_with_builtin` (`test/test_compiler.ml`, `typecheck` group). Full suite verified green modulo the pre-existing baseline (1 codegen `llvm_ir_validity_gate` failure — `task_await_ptr`/`task_await_int`/`rpc_hash_live` IR-verify + a distributed-program non-pointer-scrutinee ICE — confirmed present on an unmodified checkout of the same two touched files via `git checkout` + rebuild before this fix was reapplied, so it predates and is unrelated to this change): **452 compiler (was 451) / 231 eval / 779 stdlib quick**, `run_codegen` unaffected by the diff (0 files touched in `lib/tir/`).

- ✅ **Linearity checker soundness gap: a value acquired via `let?`/`with` was never tracked as linear, so double-consuming it went unflagged — FIXED (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`'s `bind_pattern_bindings`, 2026-07-21).** Found while auditing `docs/cookbook/linear-types.md`: the doc claimed reusing a `let?`-chained linear value after it was consumed "would be a compile error (linear value used twice)" — empirically false. Minimal repro: `always_linear type Res = Res(Int); fn acquire(): Result(Res, String) do Ok(Res(1)) end; fn consume(r: Res): Result(Int, String) do ... end; fn f(): Result(Int, String) do let? r = acquire(); let? a = consume(r); let? b = consume(r); Ok(a + b) end` typechecked with **zero errors**, while the identical double-use of a plain-`let`-bound or function-parameter-bound linear value IS correctly rejected ("The linear value `r` is used more than once here."). Same gap reproduces through `with Ok(r) <- acquire() do ... end`. **Root cause:** `bind_pattern_bindings` — the one binding path shared by `let?` (`ELetQ`), `with`'s desugared nested `match`, and ordinary `match` arms — only promotes a binding to `Linear` when its type already carries a `TLin` wrapper or its scrutinee is itself a pre-tracked linear variable; unlike `ELet`'s `auto_lin` and `bind_lam_param`'s `effective_lin`, it never consulted `env.always_linear_types` to auto-promote a binding whose *resolved type name* is declared `always_linear type` — so a handle acquired straight from a fresh call's `Result` (not a pre-tracked variable, not `TLin`-annotated) was bound as an ordinary, untracked variable. **Fix:** added the same `always_linear_types` lookup to `bind_pattern_bindings`'s `Mono` and `Poly` cases (mirroring the existing `ELet`/`bind_lam_param` logic exactly), a single-site, non-behavior-changing addition for every path that already worked. Three new regression tests in `test/test_compiler.ml` (`test_linear_letq_acquire_double_use`, `test_linear_letq_acquire_single_use_ok`, `test_linear_with_acquire_double_use`). **Known related gap, deliberately NOT fixed here (separate, larger issue — filed below):** `check_linear_all_consumed` (the "dropped without being consumed" check) is never invoked for a `let?`/`with`/match-arm binding's continuation at all (only at an `ELet`'s own scope exit and a function body's exit), so a `let?`-acquired linear value that is silently never used slips through regardless of this fix — would need a real scope-exit hook on the CPS-shaped `ELetQ`/`EMatch` continuation, not a one-line change. See `specs/progress.md`'s matching "Current State" entry for the full writeup, including the cookbook doc rewrite this bug was found auditing.

- [ ] **`check_linear_all_consumed` never fires for `let?`/`with`/match-arm bindings — a linear value acquired that way can be silently dropped without ever being consumed.** Found alongside the double-use fix above (same session, same root area, deliberately not bundled into that fix — it needs its own scope-exit design, not a one-line change). `check_linear_all_consumed` is only called at an `ELet` binding's own scope exit (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml` ELet handling) and at a function body's exit (checking only its parameters) — never for the continuation of a `let?`/`with`/match arm. Repro: `let? r = acquire(); Ok(1)` (never referencing `r`) typechecks clean with no "never used" diagnostic, even though `Res` is `always_linear`. Needs a real scope-exit hook threaded through the CPS-shaped `ELetQ`/`EMatch` continuation structure — deferred as a follow-up.

- ✅ **`int_mod_euclid` native codegen was wrong for a negative divisor — fixed + renamed `march_checked_umod` → `march_checked_emod` (2026-07-10).** The runtime helper lowered `int_mod_euclid` to genuinely-*unsigned* `%` (`(uint64_t)a % (uint64_t)b`), which agrees with the interpreter's Euclidean remainder only when the divisor is positive; for a **negative** divisor it diverged silently — e.g. `int_mod_euclid(-7, -3)` gave `-7` compiled vs. `2` interpreted (unsigned `%` returns a huge value cast back to a negative int64). Replaced with signed Euclidean semantics mirroring `eval.ml` byte-for-byte (`r = a % b; if r < 0 then r + abs b else r`) and renamed the symbol to `march_checked_emod` so it reads as the mod-side pair of the new `march_checked_ediv` (the old "u"-for-unsigned prefix was actively misleading once the body became signed). Touched the same multi-site set as the `_ediv` add: `runtime/march_runtime.c` (impl + the shared comment block), both `llvm_emit.ml` mapping sites, the `llvm_builtins.ml` preamble (string table + `PDeclare`), and the `test_codegen.ml` preamble golden. New parity test `test_compiled_int_mod_euclid_parity` covers all four sign quadrants (`[1, 2, 2, 1]`), with the `(-7, -3) → 2` case pinning the regression. Manually confirmed interpreter and compiled now agree. (The `INT64_MIN` divisor edge — `abs(INT64_MIN)` unrepresentable — is left undefended, matching `eval.ml`'s own OCaml `abs min_int` behaviour; no caller reaches it.)

- ✅ **Random + int builtins on `--target js`; portable sfc32 Random core (2026-07-10).** Resolves the 2026-07-09 Starfling P1 ("`Random` unusable on `--target js`"). Three parts. **(1) JS mappings for the int builtins** — `int_and/or/xor/not/shl/shr/popcount/mod/div/mod_euclid/div_euclid` now delegate to new checked implementations in `runtime/march_runtime.mjs` that mirror the INTERPRETER's semantics (`int_shr` is a logical shift of the 63-bit pattern; `int_popcount` flips the sign bit before counting) exactly within the double-exact range ±(2^53−1) and **throw RangeError rather than silently round** beyond it; fast paths (int32, and non-negative split into 2^32-radix words) keep hot code off BigInt entirely (~35 ns/op vs ~53 ns/op BigInt path, node 25). Also mapped: `int_abs`, `unix_time`, `float_abs/floor/ceil`, and `float_round` (half-away-from-zero via `march_float_round` — JS `Math.round` differs on negative halves). First-class `$clo` wrappers added for all of the above. **(2) Unmapped builtin = compile error** — a direct call that resolves to no user fn, letrec-local fn, or extern now raises `Js_emit_error` (sorted, one diagnostic per name) instead of bare-emitting a guaranteed runtime `ReferenceError`; `int_max_value`/`int_min_value` get a tailored message (their value ±2^62 is fundamentally unrepresentable in the JS target's double-backed Int — the reason a "BigInt-backed xoshiro" fix was rejected). Negative golden `test/native/js_unmapped_builtin.{march,expected}` pins the diagnostic. Note: `test/whole_program/zoo.mjs` is now a frozen pre-change artifact — the zoo deliberately exercises tcp/tls/actor/process/file builtins, so its whole-program JS emission now (correctly) fails with 98 unmapped-builtin errors. **(3) `stdlib/random.march` core rewritten from 63-bit xoshiro256\*\* to sfc32 on 32-bit words** (state record shape `{s0,s1,s2,s3}` and the whole public API preserved; `Gen`/`Check`/`SwimDriver` untouched): every intermediate stays below 2^53, so sequences are exact — and IDENTICAL — on the interpreter, native, and JS backends (verified: same 200k-draw checksum on all three; ~0.10 µs/draw native `--opt 2`, ~0.70 µs/draw node). `next_int` is now uniform in [0, 2^52) (was full-63-bit signed) — the widest portable width; `next_float` builds from 52 fresh bits; seeding is splitmix32-style over the seed's base-2^32 digits (extracted without shifting negative words — `int_shr` is logical interpreted but arithmetic native, a pre-existing divergence the old xoshiro silently suffered). Golden `test/native/js_random_determinism.{march,expected}` wired into `test/dune` (three-rule pattern): self-checks range/distinctness, diff pins determinism, and the .expected equals the interpreter/native output byte-for-byte. Full suite green: **451 compiler / 231 eval / 394 codegen / 807 stdlib**; `test/stdlib/test_random.march` 169-test file green (4 pre-existing unrelated doctest failures unchanged). Two new compiler bugs found while building this, both since FIXED (see Done entries): the self-referencing `let`-shadowing miscompile and the `int_div_euclid` native link gap (`main` `cf604dcd`). **Starfling note:** `demo_app/starfling`'s Level generator (on branch `claude/doom-like-browser-game-82db28`) can now swap its module-local 31-bit LCG fallback back to `Random` — Random is portable and exact on JS as of this change (and sfc32 is the better generator); not swapped here per instruction, and note the LCG's sequences differ from Random's, so the swap changes generated levels.
- ✅ **`int_div_euclid` native codegen — the last of the four `int_div`/`int_mod` family missing from the LLVM backend (2026-07-10).** The builtin existed in the interpreter (`lib/eval/eval.ml`, Euclidean quotient) and had a JS-target mapping (`march_int_div_euclid` in `runtime/march_runtime.mjs` via `lib/tir/js_emit.ml`), but had **no native codegen mapping**: compiling any caller failed at link time with `Undefined symbols: _int_div_euclid`. It was already correctly listed in `defun.ml`'s `builtin_names` (and `policy_dce.ml`/`purity.ml`), so it stayed an `EApp`/`ECallPtr` rather than being defunctionalized — the gap was purely the emit + runtime symbol. Fix mirrors its siblings `int_mod`→`march_checked_imod`, `int_div`→`march_checked_idiv`, `int_mod_euclid`→`march_checked_umod`: (1) new `march_checked_ediv(i64,i64)` in `runtime/march_runtime.c` implementing eval.ml byte-for-byte (`q=a/b; r=a-q*b; if r<0 then (b>0 ? q-1 : q+1) else q`), with the same `march_div_by_zero("int_div_euclid")` panic on a zero divisor; (2) the mapping added to **both** `llvm_emit.ml` call-path sites (the `EApp` guard/helper ~1600-1613 and the `ECallPtr` twin ~2090-2103, per the "builtin multi-site" gotcha); (3) the `declare`/`PDeclare` added to `lib/tir/llvm_builtins.ml`'s preamble (string table + `PDeclare` list). Named `march_checked_ediv` (Euclidean) rather than a "u"-prefixed sibling of `umod` because it is signed throughout — commented as such at the def. New parity test `test_compiled_int_div_euclid_parity` (`test/test_codegen.ml`, `iface_impl_mono_codegen` group) exercises all four sign quadrants (`[3, -4, 4, -3]`) via `assert_compiled_interp_parity`. Verified manually: interpreter and compiled both print `[3, -4, 4, -3]` and both panic `int_div_euclid: division by zero` on a `/0`.
- ✅ **Perihelion combat & persistence (`demo_app/perihelion/`, 2026-07-10).** All five requested features: periodic asteroids; counter-orbiting enemy ships with aimed fire (score-gated ramp); spacebar radial weapon (orbit-only, cooldown, mult-scaled scoring); shield-pickup death model (25% ship drops, absorb-then-consume); quick-release combo multiplier (bump-then-score, cap ×5, slow capture resets); localStorage best + last-10 runs (`perihelion.v1`, pure codec, garbage-tolerant, saved on the Playing→Over edge, listed on the game-over overlay). New pure `Perihelion.Combat` module; `Core.update` gained `keys` and a fixed Playing pipeline; all run-endings funnel through `end_run`. Two new `Dom` extern pairs (`key_presses`, `store_get`/`store_set`) following the `taps()` pattern. 47 game tests + headless-driver persistence/reboot/capture verification + browser smoke, all green; every roll LCG-threaded so seeded runs replay identically. Visual polish deferred by user decision. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-10-perihelion-combat-design.md`; plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-10-perihelion-combat.md` (local-only).

- ✅ **Perihelion one-tap orbit game (`demo_app/perihelion/`, 2026-07-10).** First real game on the Canvas API: pure `Perihelion.Core` (single `update(game, taps, dt)` transition; orbit/tangent-release/assist/capture/death/restart; constants in one block; `on_capture` is the named scoring seam) + `Perihelion.Level` (star-ladder generation via a module-local Park–Miller minstd LCG — chosen over stdlib `Random`, which is broken on `--target js`, see the P1 above) + a thin Canvas shell driving `Dom.on_frame(fn _ -> ...)` per the deferred-callback convention. New `Dom.taps(el) : List((Int, Int))` poll-based input extern (JS-side pointerdown buffer per element, drained once per frame). 22 interpreter-run unit tests (orbit math, tangent release both directions, assist bends-never-accelerates, capture, all death edges, restart/best, generation determinism + 50-star bounds chain, 600-tick scripted-run smoke). Verified live in a browser (renders, real taps start/release, orbit animates, zero console/network errors; preview-tab rAF throttling makes real-time play slow-motion there) plus a headless node driver over the same compiled `perihelion.mjs` (fake canvas/rAF, deterministic frame stepping): capture → score 1 + re-orbit, death overlay + best tracking, tap-to-retry restart all confirmed end-to-end. **Also fixed en route: a real js_emit codegen bug** (see next Done entry) that any statement-position match walking a list would hit. Naming gotchas recorded: `Dead` ctor collision (membership.march) → phase is `Ready | Playing | Over`; `init` reserved keyword → `new_game`; stdlib `Gen` shadowing → module named `Perihelion.Level`. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-starfling-clone-design.md (pre-rename filename)`; plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-09-starfling-game.md (pre-rename filename)` (both local-only, gitignored dir). Deliberately not in `test_oracle.ml`/`@oracle` (JS-target-only, established precedent).

- ✅ **js_emit: statement-position match arms fell through the emitted switch — FIXED (`lib/tir/js_emit.ml`, 2026-07-10).** `emit_case`'s constructor-switch path emitted `break;` only for value-position arms (`result_var = Some`); statement-position arms (`None -> emit_stmts`) relied on the body ending in `return`, but a trailing Unit-typed call emits as a bare statement — so after it returned, execution FELL THROUGH into the next case or the non-exhaustive-match default throw. Shape that hits it: any recursive list walk whose `Cons` arm ends in the recursive call (e.g. a Canvas draw loop over entities) — every frame threw "non-exhaustive pattern match" on unwind even though every arm matched correctly; diagnosed via a headless node probe walking `Game.stars` (all elements printed, `Nil` arm printed, THEN the throw). Fix: unconditional `break;` after `emit_stmts` in both the branch arms and the default arm (unreachable and harmless when the body did return). Golden `test/native/js_match_stmt_fallthrough.{march,expected}` + `test/dune` rule pins the shape; all 18 JS-target goldens + quick alcotest suites green.

- ✅ **`Dom.set_timeout`/`set_interval`/`on_frame` callbacks had a never-satisfiable extern signature — FIXED (`stdlib/dom.march`, `runtime/march_dom.mjs`, 2026-07-09).** Resolves the zero-arg-lambda follow-up filed by the Canvas 2D API entry below (`task_f422fd93`). The three externs declared `cb: Unit -> Unit`, but a zero-arg March lambda (`fn -> body`, the documented idiom for a 0-arg callback) types to its body's result DIRECTLY with no arrow wrapper (T-Abs, `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2; `List.fold_right (fun pt acc -> TArrow (pt, acc)) param_tys body_ty` in `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`'s `ELam` inference arm returns `body_ty` unchanged when `param_tys = []`), so it can never unify with a declared arrow type — `call_it(fn -> once())` against a `Unit -> Unit` param failed with "expected `() -> ()` but got `()`" both at the call site and inside the callee. This means the three callbacks were never actually callable from real March source; no existing test exercised them end-to-end. Root cause confirmed via `--check` repro; the bidirectional checker's `check_expr`'s `ELam` arm (`peel` helper) is not buggy — it faithfully implements the same direct-body-type rule in checking mode. **Fix:** followed the codebase's own established idiom for this exact shape (`task_spawn`'s thunk param, `pmap_threshold`, `task_cancel_token_new` all avoid arrow types for 0-arg-like values) — changed all three `cb` params to `Int -> Unit` (a dummy, ignored Int argument), so callers write `fn _ -> body` instead. Updated `runtime/march_dom.mjs`'s `march_dom_set_timeout`/`set_interval`/`request_animation_frame` to call `cb._0(cb, 0)` (was `cb._0(cb)`), matching the `f._0(f, args...)` closure-call ABI (`lib/tir/js_emit.ml`) for a 1-declared-param closure. Documented the limitation in `stdlib/dom.march`'s module doc and each wrapper's `doc` string. Regression: `test/native/js_dom_timeout_callback.{march,expected}` + `test/dune` rule compiles `--target js` and runs under `node`, verifying the callback both typechecks with `fn _ -> ...` and actually fires via the `cb._0(cb, 0)` ABI (prints "fired"); a `march_dom.mjs` copy-rule was added to `test/dune` alongside the existing `march_runtime.mjs` one since this is the first JS test to actually invoke a `Dom` extern (previous `js_dom_available` test only referenced `Dom.body()` in dead code, so `march_dom.mjs`'s import was never exercised). Full suite green (`scripts/run-tests.sh`, 807 tests) plus all `test/dune` JS-target golden tests (`js_dom_available`, `js_dom_timeout_callback`, `js_extern_import`, `js_ffi_async`, `js_ffi_blocking_raises`, `js_ffi_raises`, `js_ffi_cbacked`) diff-clean.
- ✅ **Self-referencing block-`let` shadowing miscompiled on the native + JS backends — TWO bugs, both fixed (2026-07-10).** `let x = 10 / let x = x + 5 / …x…` printed 15 interpreted but a silently-wrong **10** compiled (and TDZ-crashed or mis-folded on `--target js`). The idiomatic `let x = x + 1` rebinding pattern, so high severity despite the narrow trigger (RHS must reference the binder being shadowed). Root-caused to TWO independent defects, each with a regression test/golden. **(1) `lib/tir/cprop.ml` (shared TIR, hit native + interp-constant-fold + JS):** the `ELet` arm extended the literal/alias/field env with the new binding but never REMOVED the outer binding's stale mapping when the shadowing RHS was not itself a literal/alias/record (e.g. `let x = x + 5` lowers to an `EApp`). So the outer `x -> 10` literal survived into the body and the body's uses of the NEW `x` were const-propagated to the OLD value; a later Fold baked in the wrong constant. Fix: drop `name` from all three envs (`env`/`avar`/`fenv`) before conditionally re-adding the new binding — mirrors the shadow-filtering the `ECase` arm already did for branch-bound vars; RHS look-ups still use the pre-removal envs so a self-alias can inherit outer field shape; `remove_assoc` on an absent name is a no-op so non-shadowing lets are unchanged. Regression: `test_compiled_let_shadowing_parity` (`test/test_codegen.ml`, `iface_impl_mono_codegen`) — chain `((10+5)*2)=30` (buggy=10). **(2) `lib/tir/js_emit.ml` (JS only, surfaced once cprop couldn't fold to a constant, e.g. a param-driven `mix32`):** a shadowing `let x = <expr using x>` emitted `const x = (…x…)` inside a fresh `{ }` block, and the RHS's `x` resolved to the block-HOISTED inner `const x` in its temporal dead zone → `ReferenceError`. Fix: thread a `name_env` (source name → current JS id) through emission; a binding that shadows an in-scope name is emitted under a fresh unique id (`x$sN`), the RHS is emitted BEFORE the new id is installed (so it reads the outer binding), and pattern-variable bindings in `emit_case` are registered the same way (a scrutinee/branch var on a renamed local now resolves correctly — also fixed `scrut_name` to use the rename). `name_env` is saved/restored around nested (`ELetRec`) fns. Golden: `test/native/js_let_shadowing.{march,expected}` (self-ref chain, a binding read before it is shadowed, and a match arm whose pattern var shadows an outer local — `29/129/101/8`, identical on all three backends). With both fixed, `stdlib/random.march`'s `mix32` was restored from the distinct-name workaround (`a`/`b`/`c`) to the classic self-shadowing splitmix32 idiom (`let x = f(x)` chain) and `seed`'s workaround comment corrected; the `js_random_determinism` golden stayed byte-identical. Full suite green: **451 compiler / 231 eval / 396 codegen / 807 stdlib**; all 10 JS goldens pass. Filed by the Random-on-JS work; a dedicated background session (`task_259907b1`) was also spun up.

- ✅ **Random + int builtins on `--target js`; portable sfc32 Random core (2026-07-10).** Resolves the 2026-07-09 Starfling P1 ("`Random` unusable on `--target js`"). Three parts. **(1) JS mappings for the int builtins** — `int_and/or/xor/not/shl/shr/popcount/mod/div/mod_euclid/div_euclid` now delegate to new checked implementations in `runtime/march_runtime.mjs` that mirror the INTERPRETER's semantics (`int_shr` is a logical shift of the 63-bit pattern; `int_popcount` flips the sign bit before counting) exactly within the double-exact range ±(2^53−1) and **throw RangeError rather than silently round** beyond it; fast paths (int32, and non-negative split into 2^32-radix words) keep hot code off BigInt entirely (~35 ns/op vs ~53 ns/op BigInt path, node 25). Also mapped: `int_abs`, `unix_time`, `float_abs/floor/ceil`, and `float_round` (half-away-from-zero via `march_float_round` — JS `Math.round` differs on negative halves). First-class `$clo` wrappers added for all of the above. **(2) Unmapped builtin = compile error** — a direct call that resolves to no user fn, letrec-local fn, or extern now raises `Js_emit_error` (sorted, one diagnostic per name) instead of bare-emitting a guaranteed runtime `ReferenceError`; `int_max_value`/`int_min_value` get a tailored message (their value ±2^62 is fundamentally unrepresentable in the JS target's double-backed Int — the reason a "BigInt-backed xoshiro" fix was rejected). Negative golden `test/native/js_unmapped_builtin.{march,expected}` pins the diagnostic. Note: `test/whole_program/zoo.mjs` is now a frozen pre-change artifact — the zoo deliberately exercises tcp/tls/actor/process/file builtins, so its whole-program JS emission now (correctly) fails with 98 unmapped-builtin errors. **(3) `stdlib/random.march` core rewritten from 63-bit xoshiro256\*\* to sfc32 on 32-bit words** (state record shape `{s0,s1,s2,s3}` and the whole public API preserved; `Gen`/`Check`/`SwimDriver` untouched): every intermediate stays below 2^53, so sequences are exact — and IDENTICAL — on the interpreter, native, and JS backends (verified: same 200k-draw checksum on all three; ~0.10 µs/draw native `--opt 2`, ~0.70 µs/draw node). `next_int` is now uniform in [0, 2^52) (was full-63-bit signed) — the widest portable width; `next_float` builds from 52 fresh bits; seeding is splitmix32-style over the seed's base-2^32 digits (extracted without shifting negative words — `int_shr` is logical interpreted but arithmetic native, a pre-existing divergence the old xoshiro silently suffered). Golden `test/native/js_random_determinism.{march,expected}` wired into `test/dune` (three-rule pattern): self-checks range/distinctness, diff pins determinism, and the .expected equals the interpreter/native output byte-for-byte. Full suite green: **451 compiler / 231 eval / 394 codegen / 807 stdlib**; `test/stdlib/test_random.march` 169-test file green (4 pre-existing unrelated doctest failures unchanged). Two new compiler bugs found while building this, both since FIXED (see Done entries): the self-referencing `let`-shadowing miscompile and the `int_div_euclid` native link gap (`main` `cf604dcd`). **Starfling note:** `demo_app/starfling`'s Level generator (on branch `claude/doom-like-browser-game-82db28`) can now swap its module-local 31-bit LCG fallback back to `Random` — Random is portable and exact on JS as of this change (and sfc32 is the better generator); not swapped here per instruction, and note the LCG's sequences differ from Random's, so the swap changes generated levels.
- ✅ **`int_div_euclid` native codegen — the last of the four `int_div`/`int_mod` family missing from the LLVM backend (2026-07-10).** The builtin existed in the interpreter (`lib/eval/eval.ml`, Euclidean quotient) and had a JS-target mapping (`march_int_div_euclid` in `runtime/march_runtime.mjs` via `lib/tir/js_emit.ml`), but had **no native codegen mapping**: compiling any caller failed at link time with `Undefined symbols: _int_div_euclid`. It was already correctly listed in `defun.ml`'s `builtin_names` (and `policy_dce.ml`/`purity.ml`), so it stayed an `EApp`/`ECallPtr` rather than being defunctionalized — the gap was purely the emit + runtime symbol. Fix mirrors its siblings `int_mod`→`march_checked_imod`, `int_div`→`march_checked_idiv`, `int_mod_euclid`→`march_checked_umod`: (1) new `march_checked_ediv(i64,i64)` in `runtime/march_runtime.c` implementing eval.ml byte-for-byte (`q=a/b; r=a-q*b; if r<0 then (b>0 ? q-1 : q+1) else q`), with the same `march_div_by_zero("int_div_euclid")` panic on a zero divisor; (2) the mapping added to **both** `llvm_emit.ml` call-path sites (the `EApp` guard/helper ~1600-1613 and the `ECallPtr` twin ~2090-2103, per the "builtin multi-site" gotcha); (3) the `declare`/`PDeclare` added to `lib/tir/llvm_builtins.ml`'s preamble (string table + `PDeclare` list). Named `march_checked_ediv` (Euclidean) rather than a "u"-prefixed sibling of `umod` because it is signed throughout — commented as such at the def. New parity test `test_compiled_int_div_euclid_parity` (`test/test_codegen.ml`, `iface_impl_mono_codegen` group) exercises all four sign quadrants (`[3, -4, 4, -3]`) via `assert_compiled_interp_parity`. Verified manually: interpreter and compiled both print `[3, -4, 4, -3]` and both panic `int_div_euclid: division by zero` on a `/0`.

- ✅ **Canvas 2D API for `--target js` (2026-07-09).** New `stdlib/canvas.march` + `runtime/march_canvas.mjs`, mirroring `Dom`'s extern-block pattern (`resource Context`/`Image`, JS-target-only). Full 2D surface: state stack, style, rects, paths (including quadratic/bezier curves), text, and `load_image` as a `blocking raises` extern (`Result(Image, String)`, no callback API needed). `Dom` gains `event_x`/`event_y` canvas-relative pointer accessors. New `demo_app/canvas_demo/` verified live in a browser (preview tools): all shapes render, click lands a marker at the exact clicked coordinate, zero console/network errors. New `test/native/js_canvas_available.march` golden fixture (dead-code compile coverage, no real DOM needed); `js_dom_available.march` extended similarly for the new Dom accessors. Docs: `docs/stdlib.md` Canvas section + table row, stdlib count 108 → 109 across `docs/stdlib.md`/`CLAUDE.md`/`SKILL.md`. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-canvas-api-design.md`; plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-09-canvas-2d-api.md`. Deliberately **not** wired into `test_oracle.ml`/`@oracle` — that suite diffs interpreter vs. native compile and cannot exercise `--target js`-only code, confirmed with the user during planning rather than added as a no-op. **Found and filed, not fixed here:** `Dom.on_frame`/`set_timeout`/`set_interval` are uncallable from March source with a zero-arg lambda (a zero-arg lambda types to its body's result directly, per `core-march-types.md`'s T-Abs rule, and never unifies with the externs' declared `Unit -> Unit` arrow type) — a pre-existing latent bug, not introduced by this change; `canvas_demo`'s originally-planned animation loop was replaced with a static rotated shape as a result. See P1 section / spawn_task `task_f422fd93` for the follow-up.

- ✅ **Order-independent multi-module name resolution — bare/qualified type & constructor references resolve the same regardless of compile entry (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, `test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`, 2026-07-08).** A `mod A.Sub` referencing a sibling `mod A`'s constructors/types by BARE name (or the `Type.Ctor` disambiguation form) with no `import`/`use` previously resolved only when `mod A` was Pass-2-checked first — so on a **cyclic** module graph (impossible to topologically order; real case: the `conduit` package, `Conduit` ↔ `Conduit.API`) `forge check` passed or failed depending on which file was the entry (`conduit/lib/conduit/backoff.march` entry → 59 errors, `api.march` entry → 0, identical sources). Root cause: nested-module public types were seeded in Pass-1 prebind under their module-QUALIFIED key only, while the top-level path and records already seeded the bare name; bare cross-module resolution then depended on Pass-2 order, which a cycle makes nondeterministic. **Fix, all in Pass-1 prebind (`prebind_mod_members` + the `_inc` LSP/REPL twin + the unwrapped-entry top-level fold):** (1) seed a nested public type's bare name and bare constructor keys, plus the bare `Type.Ctor` disambiguation key, mirroring the top-level path (`:8929`); (2) the module-qualified constructor now carries `ci_type = <bare type>` (was the qualified `qname`) to match `check_decl` — the lone site emitting a qualified nominal, cause of "expected `Mod.T` but got `T`"; (3) `prebind_fn_scheme` declines to build a scheme from a QUALIFIED type name (falls back to the fresh-var placeholder) instead of emitting a qualified nominal it can't canonicalize env-lessly — fixing an order-dependent signature/argument mismatch without over-canonicalizing distinct same-suffix types (`Conduit.Config` vs stdlib `Config`); (4) bare-constructor seeding is SUPPRESSED for types a sibling `sig` exports opaquely (opacity filter threaded through prebind via an `~opaque` set built from sibling `sig` decls at both nested and unwrapped-entry levels), preserving opaque-type constructor hiding. All ~40 `conduit` files now `--check` clean as the entry, in any order. **Full six-runner suite green modulo the pre-existing baseline (15 Slow compiled adversarial-regressions + 1 codegen IR-gate — all present on `origin/main` before this change, verified by a file-copy baseline diff).** New regression tests `test_tc_cyclic_bare_ctor_order_independent` and `test_tc_qualified_sig_type_order_independent` (`test/test_stdlib_suite.ml`, module-system group) pin the cyclic-bare-ctor and qualified-signature cases; `test_tc_sig_opaque_hides_ctors` guards the opacity carve-out. Directly closes the "whole-program type resolution must be order-independent" constraint (`forge build` typechecks each lib file as its own entry).

- ✅ **Tetris live-compile browser playground + 3 JS-backend/typecheck bug fixes (`docs/tetris-playground/`, `js/march_browser_compile.ml`, `lib/driver/js_pipeline.ml`, `demo_app/tetris{,_logic}/`, 2026-07-08).** New browser demo: edit March source, hit Run, watch the real compiler (parse → typecheck → lower → optimize → js_emit) run entirely in-browser and produce a playable Tetris game in a fresh iframe. New `lib/driver/js_pipeline.ml` extracts the `--target js` TIR pipeline as a standalone library (verified to link under js_of_ocaml — the LLVM-only native-triple C stub dead-code-eliminates); a new `js/march_browser_compile.ml` entry point wraps it for the browser. Game: `demo_app/tetris_logic/` (pure rules, 14 forge unit tests) + `demo_app/tetris/` (Dom wiring, path-deps on `tetris_logic` so `forge test` stays native-safe — Dom externs have no native symbols, discovered the hard way when the first combined project failed to link natively). State lives in the DOM (`#game-state` data-* attrs) since March has no mutable-cell primitive reachable across independent event callbacks. Along the way, found and fixed three real, general compiler bugs (all pre-existing, not introduced by this feature): (1) `lib/tir/js_emit.ml` had no case for `int_div`/`int_mod`/`int_mod_euclid`/the bitwise family/`unix_time`/`int_pow`/`int_abs`/`int_max_value`/`int_min_value` — any `Array` growing past 32 elements, or `Random`, hits this; (2) `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml` never registered `string_from_codepoint`/`string_to_codepoints` at all, breaking any strict typecheck of `stdlib/string.march`; (3) `stdlib/random.march`'s `mix()` had an integer literal exceeding the March lexer's `max_int` — but only when the compiler ITSELF runs as js_of_ocaml-compiled browser JS (32-bit `int` representation there vs. native's 63-bit) — rewritten as an exactly-equal product+sum, a real fix for the pre-existing interpreter REPL playground's `Random` support too. Filed, not fixed: `check_module_full`'s pass-1 flat-merges bare names across many sibling stdlib modules, letting a same-named function in two modules (`fold_left`, in `prelude.march` and `list.march`/`array.march`) shadow across module boundaries — worked around narrowly in the playground bundle. Full suite green throughout (451 compiler / 231 eval / 398 codegen / 773 stdlib quick). See `specs/progress.md`'s matching entry for the full writeup; plan at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-08-tetris-playground.md` (gitignored).

- ✅ **`march check`/`forge check` seed pass 1 from a cached, already-typechecked stdlib env instead of re-typechecking stdlib from scratch every invocation (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, `bin/main.ml`, `specs/plans/2026-07-07-compiler-daemon-perf-plan.md`, 2026-07-07).** Follow-up to a compile/typecheck-speed opportunity sweep: `--timings` showed stdlib typecheck alone is ~68% of `forge check`'s wall time on a small (10-file) real project (`~/code/march_doc`), ~19% on a 16-file one, down to ~5% on an 80-file outlier — a large, avoidable, constant cost paid on every invocation of `run_check_cmd` (the `march check f1 f2 ...` subcommand `forge check`/`forge build` batch onto). **Investigated a persistent-daemon design first (see the plan doc's superseded "REVISED" section) but found it unnecessary:** `Typecheck.check_module_core` gained an additive `?seed_env` parameter — when given, pass 1 starts from it instead of `base_env`, with NO other change to pass 1/1b/2's structure, so every existing caller (passing `None`) is byte-for-byte unaffected. `bin/main.ml`'s `run_check_cmd` now builds (or loads from an on-disk Marshal cache, `get_stdlib_tc_env`, keyed by a content hash of `stdlib_decls`) a `stdlib_tc_env` via `check_module_core` on stdlib decls ALONE, then typechecks just the user's own files seeded from it — UNLESS a user file shadows a stdlib module name (rare; falls back unchanged to the original from-scratch combined check rather than parameterizing the cache key on the shadow set). Verified byte-identical diagnostic output against the unmodified compiler across `march_doc`/`marathon`/`scroll`/`bastion` plus synthetic error/warning fixtures. Measured: `march_doc` `forge check` 0.86s → 0.20s (~4.3x) on a warm cache, `marathon` 0.51s → 0.16s (~3.2x); `scroll`/`bastion` (both shadow a stdlib name via a transitive `depot` dependency) see no change, by design. **A real regression was found and reverted before shipping:** an earlier version also wired `compile` (`--compile`) the same way, but `compile`'s `desugared` value is ALSO used for TIR lowering (which needs stdlib's function BODIES, not just types) — skipping the stdlib prepend there silently dropped stdlib from what gets lowered, breaking every compiled program touching real stdlib internals. Caught by the full test suite (24 codegen + 6 stdlib failures, all "type-incorrect TIR reached codegen" ICEs) before being reported as done; `compile` was reverted to its original always-prepend behavior. Caching stdlib's LOWERED TIR for `--compile` is explicitly out of scope (a separate, riskier follow-up — `lower.ml`'s heavy use of global mutable `Hashtbl` refs makes this a bigger undertaking, not justified by `--compile`'s smaller measured lowering-cost share vs. typecheck's). Full six-runner suite green (804 stdlib / 391 codegen / 428 compiler / 231 eval, 0 failures) after the revert.

- ✅ **Core March widening slice 5 — capabilities/effects (IO permission caps + behavioral module caps), CLOSEOUT (`specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.8.1–§2.8.12, `specs/lang/capabilities.md`, `specs/lang/types/{accept/t45..t56,reject/t36..t48}` + `INDEX.md`, `specs/lang/index.md`, `specs/progress.md`, 2026-07-07, seven tasks — Tasks 5 and 6 are real compiler fixes (F2, F3), Tasks 1–4 and 7 DOCS-ONLY).** Widens `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` past widening slice 4 (session types/protocols/channels) to cover the **capability/effect system** — the IO-permission-cap spine (18-entry hierarchy, `needs`/`Cap(X)` signature enforcement, subsumption, transitive `use`, extern-implied caps, `cap_narrow`/`root_cap` threading, effect inference, realtime exclusion) AND the five **behavioral module caps** (`cap no_panic`/`no_alloc`/`no_extern`/`pure`/`deterministic`). Unlike slices 3/4, this slice landed **two real compiler fixes gated on the full six-runner suite** (Tasks 5–6), both closing genuine soundness holes the capabilities survey found. **The system is purely compile-time/static** — `Cap(X)` is runtime-erased (`null`/`VUnit`), interp==compiled always, so this is a `types/`-only accept/reject slice with **no golden corpus**. **F2 fix (Task 5):** `cap pure`/`cap deterministic`'s banned-builtin sets were hand-maintained name lists referencing NONEXISTENT builtins (`write_file`, `random_int`, `now_ms`) while missing the REAL effectful ones (`file_write`, `random_bytes`, `unix_time_ms`) — both are now DERIVED from the authoritative `builtin_cap_table` effect map, so `cap pure`/`cap deterministic` now actually catch the effectful builtins they were designed to ban. **F3 fix (Task 6):** `cap no_panic` covered only NAMED partial-function panic surfaces (`panic`, `unwrap`, `head`, …) and never consulted the exhaustiveness checker's verdict — a non-exhaustive `match` inside a `cap no_panic` module typechecked clean and panicked at runtime; fixed by recording non-exhaustive-match spans into a new `env.nonexhaustive_match_spans` side-table that `check_no_panic_module` now reads and promotes to an ERROR, while a PLAIN (non-cap) module's non-exhaustive match stays an unpromoted Warning (regression-guarded by `accept/t14`). Both fixes verified with value-revealing probes (programs that accepted pre-fix now reject with the cap's own error message) and `scripts/run-tests.sh` full six-runner suite GREEN, no over-rejection of legitimate pure/deterministic/no_panic modules. **Corpus:** types 78 → **104** (56 accept, 48 reject; `accept/t45`–`t56` IO-cap subsumption/transitive-use/extern-cap/`cap_narrow`-threading/behavioral-caps-correct-cases, `reject/t36`–`t48` per-Check violations including the F2/F3 witnesses that were IMPOSSIBLE to add before their respective fixes landed); `check_types.sh` 104/104, exit 0. **Six findings filed OPEN in `specs/todos.md`'s "Compiler: Capabilities/effects" section (F2/F3 have their own Done entries, distinct from these):** F1 (body-scanned IO caps are WARNING-only, not error — the honest signature-vs-body enforcement reality), F6 (only 10 of 18 hierarchy entries are registered as valid `Cap(X)` type arguments — the other 8 reject as `Unknown module IO` when used as a type argument, though they work fine as `needs` targets), the flaky narrowing HINT (Check 3's narrowing hint is nondeterministic ~1-in-10 on a byte-identical binary+input — exit code unaffected, corpus-safe, but a real undocumented compiler nondeterminism), F5 (cosmetic — `println`/`print` skip the Check-1b body-scan diagnostic entirely), **the proof-cap mint mismatch** (deliberately unlabeled, not a letter — `F1`–`F8` are all already in use elsewhere in this document — the `capabilities.md`-documented proof-cap "mint" idiom doesn't typecheck as written;
