the mechanism that does work, `cap_narrow`, is unrestricted and can mint any nominal proof capability from an ordinary `Cap(IO)` with no covering `needs` — deferred to a later proof-caps-focused slice per the widening plan), and a residual **guarded-match exhaustiveness gap** (a pattern guard short-circuits `check_exhaustiveness` before it ever records a span, so a guarded non-exhaustive match in a `cap no_panic` module is invisible to BOTH the ordinary warning and F3's new error path — pre-existing behavior F3 correctly inherits rather than introduces, not in F3's scope, filed as a residual soundness gap). **`specs/lang/capabilities.md` (the pre-existing 692-line tutorial, already wired into `specs/lang/index.md`'s chapter map before this slice) reconciled (Task 7):** the F1 tutorial overclaims — "absence of a capability declaration is a machine-verified guarantee of purity" (opening), "the build fails with a clear message" (IO-caps intro), "There are no false positives" + "the absence of `needs` is itself a machine-verified guarantee" (What the compiler tells you), "the type checker already enforces `needs` as an error" (capability inference hints) — are all corrected to state the honest three-tier severity (signature `Cap(X)` / transitive `use` / extern `Cap` = ERROR, build-breaking; direct body call to an IO builtin = WARNING-only, `--check` exits 0), each with a live-verified side-by-side transcript; the tutorial's Behavioral-caps section is extended from two caps (`no_panic`/`no_alloc`) to all five, documenting `cap no_extern`/`cap pure`/`cap deterministic` for the first time and noting the F2/F3 fixes inline; the proof-caps section gets a "known mismatch" callout for the proof-cap mint mismatch finding. Every code example this task touched or added was re-`--check`ed live (all pass as claimed post-edit). §2.8's own stale mid-slice state (F2/F3 described as "open"/"UNSOUND" from Task 4's writing, before Tasks 5–6 fixed them; a stale `100/100` illustrative count) is flipped to describe the FIXED, current, 104/104 state, and §6's "PARTIALLY LANDED... Tasks 1-3... still deferred" roadmap bullet is updated to reflect all seven tasks landed. `check-docs.sh` exit 0. See `specs/progress.md`'s "Core March widening slice 5" entry for the full writeup. **Next queued widening slice:** proof caps (closing the proof-cap mint mismatch) or linear types (per the roadmap).

- ✅ **Core March widening slice 4 — session types, protocols, and channels, CLOSEOUT (`specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.11.1–§4.11.6, `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.7.1–§2.7.10, `specs/lang/session-types.md`, `specs/lang/golden/{g38,g39}` + `INDEX.md` + `verify.sh`, `specs/lang/types/{accept/t41,accept/t42,accept/t43,reject/t30..t35}` + `INDEX.md`, `specs/lang/index.md`, `specs/progress.md`, 2026-07-06, six tasks — Task 1 is a real compiler fix, Tasks 2–6 DOCS-ONLY).** Widens the two conformance-tested core-language references past widening slice 3 (actors/messaging/supervision) to cover the **session-typed channel/protocol system**: `protocol` declaration, projection, binary duality, MPST send/recv-pair consistency (typing-only), the `Chan(Role, Proto)` linear endpoint type, per-operation channel-state typing (`Chan.new`/`send`/`recv`/`close`/`choose`/`offer`), and the channel runtime (crossed-FIFO queues, synchronous no-scheduler model). **Task 1 (F1/F2, the in-slice compiler fix):** `Chan.send`'s payload was lowered as a bare untagged `i64` (no dedicated emit arm, fell through the general `EApp` path) while `Chan.recv`'s returned payload went through the standard conditional erased-i64 untag — an asymmetry corrupting every ODD `Int` payload (`43`→`21`) and flipping every `Bool` (`true`→`false`) compiled. Fixed with additive `chan_send`/`chan_choose`/`mpst_send` emit arms (mirroring the `vault_set`/`actor_reply` precedent) coercing via `emit_atom_as ctx "ptr"`; un-vacuumed the previously-vacuous `test_session_*` value assertions to assert an odd payload round-trips compiled. `scripts/run-tests.sh` full six-runner suite GREEN before this slice's golden witnesses were written (426 compiler / 231 eval / 391 codegen / 804 stdlib / 29 snapshots). **Headline property (mechanically pinned, post-fix):** the **binary channel plane** (`Chan.*`, `Int`/`Bool`/`String` payloads, correctly interleaved) is now **byte-identical interpreted vs compiled**, witnessed by two new golden programs (`g38_chan_int_echo` — odd-Int round-trip `42`→`43`; `g39_chan_choose_offer` — `choose`/`offer` over type-distinct branches, both `MATCH` under `verify.sh`). **MPST stays OUT of the golden corpus and is consistently marked typing-only in both references** — every `MPST.*` program segfaults compiled (F3). Golden corpus 37 → **39** (`verify.sh` 39/39, exit 0); types corpus 69 → **78** (accept 40 → 43, reject 29 → 35; `accept/t41`–`t43` protocol/`Chan.new`/MPST-declare/choose-offer-round-trip, `reject/t30`–`t35` per-op session-state violations; `check_types.sh` 78/78, exit 0). **Six findings filed OPEN (not fixed — docs-only for Tasks 2–6; findings 20–25 in `core-march-types.md` §4.1, this file's "Compiler: Session types" section):** F4/finding 20 (MPST merge rule leaks into binary duality, wrongly rejecting a legal same-payload-type `choose`), F5/finding 21 (`Chan.offer` always returns the FIRST branch's continuation regardless of the peer's actual runtime choice — a real soundness gap for differently-typed branches), F3/finding 22 (MPST segfaults compiled, exit 139 — the compiled MPST C runtime is not wired to the lowered representation), F6/finding 23 (no scheduler — `recv`-before-`send` deadlocks at runtime, not caught statically; a documented scope boundary, not a bug), F7/finding 24 (partial linearity — dropping an unclosed `SEnd` channel, or reusing a linear parameter endpoint whose state coincidentally still matches, both slip through the generic linear tracker), F8/finding 25 (an undeclared protocol participant's HINT prints to stderr at typecheck time — confirmed diagnostic noise, does not affect `verify.sh`'s stdout/stdout+stderr comparison). F1/F2 has its own Done entry (this file's "Recently fixed" section, above), distinct from these six. **`specs/lang/session-types.md` (the pre-existing tutorial chapter, already wired into `specs/lang/index.md`'s chapter map before this slice) reconciled:** cross-references to `core-march.md` §4.11 and `core-march-types.md` §2.7 added, plus a note on which plane is byte-identical (binary, post-F1/F2) versus interp-only (MPST, F3) — the same reconciliation pattern slice 3 applied to `actors.md`. `check-docs.sh` exit 0. See `specs/progress.md`'s "Core March widening slice 4" entry for the full writeup. **Next queued widening slice:** effects/capabilities, or error-handling/`let?` (per the roadmap).

- ✅ **`dependency_order_dmod_run` mixed hard (bare ctor/type) and soft (qualified-reference) dependency edges into one DFS, letting a soft-edge reference cycle silently reorder a hard edge — FIXED (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, `forge/test/test_build_check.ml`, 2026-07-07).** Found checking a real downstream project (`conduit`) whole-program: `Conduit` qualified-delegates to `Conduit.API`, which qualified-calls `Conduit.Worker`, which bare-pattern-matches `WorkerError` — a constructor `Conduit` itself declares. `march check` reported `` I don't know a constructor called `WorkerError` `` despite the declaration being correct. **Root cause:** `dependency_order_dmod_run` topologically orders sibling DMods over the UNION of `module_refs_in_decls` (qualified `Mod.fn`/`Mod.Ctor` references) and `unqualified_module_deps` (bare ctor/type references) — but the two are not equally load-bearing. Qualified references are pre-bound as pass-1 Mono placeholders regardless of check order (`prebind_mod_members`), so ordering by them is a precision nicety only (the rationale `0799c745` introduced this edge kind for — avoiding a caller pinning an under-generalized placeholder). Bare references have no such placeholder — the name is only visible once the defining sibling's DMod is actually processed by `check_decl` — so ordering by them is a hard correctness requirement. Real modules commonly form legitimate reference cycles through qualified calls alone (a facade delegating to an internal module that calls back into a module the facade depends on) — previously harmless, since qualified refs tolerate any order. Mixing both edge kinds into one DFS meant a soft-edge cycle could non-deterministically reorder a hard edge: whichever edge type the traversal reached an already-in-progress ancestor through "won", independent of which one actually mattered. **Fix:** compute the existing combined-edge DFS order unchanged (zero behavior change for the common case — every existing regression fixture, including the `0799c745` "dotted sibling module order" precision test, still passes byte-identically), then validate it against the hard edges alone; only when a hard edge is actually violated does it fall back to Kahn's algorithm restricted to hard edges, using the original order purely as a tie-break. New regression test `test_check_qualified_cycle_does_not_break_bare_ctor_ordering` in `forge/test/test_build_check.ml`, minimized to the same three-module facade/worker/core shape (file naming is itself load-bearing there — `Cmd_build.find_march_files` walks sorted-ascending but prepends, so the actual file list handed to `march check` is sorted DESCENDING; the hard dependency's definer has to be discovered first through the real `forge check` path for the bug to reproduce, hence `zcore.march`). Verified against the live repro: `conduit`'s `WorkerError` error is gone; standard runners unaffected (424 compiler / 231 eval / 391 codegen, all exit 0; `forge/test/test_build_check.exe` 12/12).

- ✅ **Core March widening slice 3 — actors, messaging, and supervision, CLOSEOUT (`specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.10.1–§4.10.7, `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.6.1–§2.6.4, `specs/lang/actors.md`, `specs/lang/golden/{g35,g36,g37}` + `INDEX.md` + `verify.sh`, `specs/lang/types/{accept/t39,accept/t40,reject/t28,reject/t29}` + `INDEX.md`, `specs/lang/index.md`, `specs/progress.md`, 2026-07-06, six tasks — DOCS-ONLY, no compiler changes).** Widens the two conformance-tested core-language references past widening slice 2 (modules/visibility) to cover actors: declaration + `spawn`/`Pid` typing, message send/receive, lifecycle (`kill`/`is_alive`), the epoch-stamped capability plane, and `one_for_one` supervision. **Headline property (mechanically pinned):** the **live-message plane** — `spawn`/`send`(live)/`receive`/`run_until_idle`/`is_alive`/`kill` — is DETERMINISTIC and **byte-identical interpreted vs compiled** for any interleaving-independent program, witnessed by three new golden programs (`g35_actor_spawn_send`, `g36_actor_receive`, `g37_actor_lifecycle`, all `MATCH` under `verify.sh`). A seventh item — the flat global message-name namespace — is a documented DESIGN POINT (§2.6.4), not a gap. **`specs/lang/actors.md` (the tutorial) reconciled:** every code example re-verified live (`--check` + run); **three broken examples fixed** — the "Complete Actor Example" did not compile (`Ping` collides with stdlib `WsFrame.Ping` in the flat namespace → renamed `Poke`, verified prints `value = 15`), the "Receiving Messages" example used `Followup` without an `on Followup(…)` handler (not a registered constructor → handler added), and the "`Actor.call`" example used the unparseable `on Call(ref, Get())` nested-pattern handler + undeclared `Get()` (rewritten to the canonical first-handler `on Call(ref, msg)` form, verified prints `count = 2`). The over-broad "all work identically interpreted or compiled" top claim was corrected to name exactly which plane is byte-identical; the capability / `Actor.call`-timeout / `pid_of_int` / `get_actor_field` surfaces marked interp-only with filed-finding cross-refs; the Builtins table gained a backend column; cross-references to §4.10.1–.7 and §2.6.1–.4 added throughout. `check-docs.sh` exit 0. - ✅ **Compiled `Task.await` on a heap (ptr) result crashed at runtime (`march: out of memory` / SIGSEGV) — FIXED (2026-07-06, `lib/tir/llvm_emit.ml` `task_await` case).** The thunk trampoline stores `task[3] = (apply_ret << 1) | 1` — the apply-wrapper's uniform-slot return tagged exactly once. `march_task_await` wraps `task[3]` straight into `Ok(...)`, but a boxed-ADT field must hold the *uniform value* `apply_ret` verbatim: an `Ok(x)` destructure loads a **ptr** field **raw** (no untag, since a normal `Ok(heap_ptr)` stores an even raw pointer), so the Ok payload carried the wild `(addr<<1)|1 ≈ 2*addr` pointer — `march_incrc`/`march_decrc` no-op'd on it (odd ⇒ `IS_HEAP_PTR` false) and the payload deref (`march_string_concat`/`List.length`) hit garbage → OOM/SIGSEGV. Interpreter was always correct; `await!`/`task_await_unwrap` were fine (they use `march_task_await_value` + a type-directed untag). This is the ptr sibling of the i64 double-tag bug fixed on main in `f89b8711` — whose commit message *wrongly assumed* the ptr path was already correct. **Fix:** extend `f89b8711`'s guard from `inner_ty = "i64"` to `"i64" || "ptr"` and `ashr` the freshly-allocated Ok payload field (offset 16) once. `task[3]` is always odd, so a single arithmetic shift is the exact inverse for **both** reps — `4*n+3 → 2*n+1` (i64: the destructure's one conditional untag then yields `n`) and `(addr<<1)|1 → addr` (ptr: the raw pointer the destructure loads directly). `"double"` (Float, ABI-broken through the `void*`-returning trampoline — separate) and other reps left byte-identical. Covers every combinator funnelling through the `task_await` builtin (`await_many`, `async_stream`/`_n`, `all_settled`, `race`). Verified compiled == interpreter for String/List/tuple/ADT/list-of-string payloads and `await_many` over Strings; clean under ASAN (no UAF/overflow); the i64 path (`6*7 → 42`) is fixed as a side effect on this branch, which lacks `f89b8711`. Regression: `test/native/task_await_ptr.{march,expected}` + a `test/dune` native compiled-run golden (String/List/i64, `MARCH_NUM_SCHEDULERS=1`). **391 codegen / 231 eval pass.**
- ✅ **Cross-module visibility bug FIXED: private `pfn`/values are no longer callable across modules (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, `specs/lang/types/{accept/t31,reject/t26}`, 2026-07-06, commit `c0570d16`, widening slice 2 Task 1).** `load_module_into_env` (`typecheck.ml:657–692`) loaded `ExFn`/`ExValue`/`ExType`/`ExRecord` cross-module export entries UNCONDITIONALLY — it never checked `entry.ex_public`, so a private `pfn`/value from another module (e.g. `Array.lst_rev`, a real `pfn` in `stdlib/array.march:39`) was callable from anywhere, while the neighboring `ExCtor` arm already correctly gated private constructors (`` Function `TrieNode` is private to module `Array`. ``). **Fix:** added the `ex_public` gate to the `ExFn` and `ExValue` arms ONLY, mirroring `ExCtor`'s existing check and message shape. **`ExType`/`ExRecord` deliberately left UNGATED (design decision, not an oversight):** March's opaque-type pattern is that a private `ptype`'s bare type NAME stays nominally referenceable cross-module (its constructor is separately gated via `ExCtor`) — gating `ExType` would have broken live, tested stdlib code (`stdlib/consistent_hash.march`'s private `ptype HashRing(a)` is referenced as `ring : ConsistentHash.HashRing(String)` on a PUBLIC parameter in `stdlib/work_dispatch.march`, both auto-loaded and tested). **Verification:** full suite green before/after (`dune build` + `scripts/run-tests.sh` full run, no regressions — nothing in the stdlib or test suite relied on cross-module private access). Corpus: `reject/t26_cross_module_private_fn` pins `Array.lst_rev(...)` now rejecting with `` is private to module `Array` ``; `accept/t31_cross_module_public_and_opaque_ptype` proves the gate is narrow — a PUBLIC cross-module call (`Array.length(Array.empty())`) still resolves, and the opaque-type pattern (a private `ptype`'s bare name usable in a cross-module annotation) still holds. `check_types.sh` green with the additions. Documented in full in `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.5 and `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.7; see `specs/progress.md`'s "Core March widening slice 2" entry for the full slice writeup.

This is the same-file counterpart to the cross-FILE visibility fix directly above (`c0570d16`), landed concurrently by a separate session; see the entry below for the full root-cause/fix writeup, re-confirmed still correct and unregressed by this slice's closeout.
- ✅ **Core March widening slice 2 — modules, imports, and visibility, CLOSEOUT (`specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.7/§4.7.1, `specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.5, `specs/lang/modules.md`, `specs/lang/types/{accept,reject}/t31`–`t38`/`t25`–`t27`, `specs/lang/types/INDEX.md`, 2026-07-06, `specs/plans/2026-07-06-widening-modules-plan.md`, five tasks).** Widens the two conformance-tested core-language references past widening slice 1 (interfaces/impls) to cover module declaration/nesting, bare-vs-qualified name resolution, `use`/`import`/`alias`, and visibility. **Unlike slice 1 (docs-only), this slice included a real compiler fix** — see the cross-module visibility Done entry directly above (`c0570d16`) — made first and gated on the full suite, with Tasks 2–4's documentation describing the corrected post-fix behavior throughout. Corpus grew from 54 to **65 programs** (38 accept / 27 reject); `check_types.sh` 65/65, exit 0, no new CI wiring (rides the existing `types-check` dune alias). **One real gap found and FILED (deliberately left OPEN, not fixed — docs-only slice):** `opaque type`'s constructor-hiding is not enforced against a qualified constructor reference from a separate compilation unit (`prebind_mod_members`, `typecheck.ml:8032–8087` — see this file's own P2 entry for the full trace) — structurally the same class of bug the in-slice compiler fix addressed for `ExFn`/`ExValue`, but on a different registration path and for the `ExCtor`/`ci_vis` check. **`specs/lang/modules.md` (the tutorial chapter) reconciled:** its "Visibility" section previously claimed `ptype` hides "both the type name and its constructors" — live-verified FALSE (a plain `ptype`'s variant constructors default `var_vis = Public` by grammar regardless of the enclosing type's own visibility; only `opaque type` forces `var_vis = Private`, and even that is undermined by the filed gap above) — corrected to match verified current behavior, with cross-references to the new `core-march.md` §4.7/§4.7.1 and `core-march-types.md` §2.5 added. Its opaque-type "known compiler limitation" note (a qualified annotation failing to unify with a bare-typed value) is now marked Resolved (`9001e4c0`, verified live in both directions). **Bonus findings surfaced while reconciling `modules.md`:** four of its own code examples were broken by the one-mod-per-file rule or the file-vs-in-file resolver distinction (a "Qualified Access" example with two top-level `mod`s in one file; a dotted-module-naming example presented as one file when the two modules must live in separate files; two stray top-level statements after a module's closing `end` — the opaque-type and Module-Level-Constants examples; and its "A Full Example" section, which cited `examples/modules.march` — a file that doesn't actually match it — and used `import`/`alias` against an in-file nested module, exactly the file-vs-in-file rejection Task 4 documents) — all fixed and every code example in the chapter re-verified live (`--check` + run to the stated printed value). See `specs/progress.md`'s "Core March widening slice 2" entry for the full writeup and `.superpowers/sdd/modules-task-5-report.md` for this closeout task's own report. **Next queued widening slice:** actors (roadmap Phase-2b/3 phasing).
- ✅ **Same-file access to a PRIVATE nested-module member now reports "private to module", not the misleading "Unknown module" (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, `specs/lang/types/reject/t25_private_nested_member.march`, `test/test_compiler.ml`, 2026-07-06).** Deferred same-file diagnostic-quality item from the modules widening slice (the cross-FILE private-member gate — `load_module_into_env`'s `ExFn`/`ExValue` `ex_public` guard — was already fixed and scoped this as secondary/escape-hatch-OK). Repro: `mod Main do  mod A do  pfn secret()… ; fn pub_fn()…  end  fn main() do A.secret() end  end` was correctly REJECTED (exit 1) but with `` Unknown module `A`. Did you mean `Io`? `` — misleading, because `A` plainly exists in-file; `secret` is merely private. **Root cause:** an in-file `mod A` only ever gives its PUBLIC members an `"A.member"` key in the outer `env.vars` (the `pub_set` export gate in the `DMod` typecheck case), so the `EVar` lookup for `A.secret` missed, fell through `resolve_qualified_var → Module_registry.ensure_loaded "A"` (no `a.march` on disk → fails), and `qualified_error_msg` hit its "Unknown module" branch because the registry has no notion of the in-file module. **Fix:** new `env.local_mods : string list StrMap.t` records each nested module's PRIVATE value/function member names (`pfn` + private `let`) during the `DMod` export step; `qualified_error_msg` (now threaded `env`) checks it first and emits the existing `` Function `secret` is private to module `A`. `` phrasing (the same wording the cross-file gate already uses) when the referenced member is a recorded private member. Genuine unknown-module typos and public-member access are unchanged (verified). Corpus grew 54→**55** (30 accept / 25 reject); `check_types.sh` 55/55. Full suite green: **421 compiler / 231 eval / 391 codegen / 804 stdlib**; `check-docs.sh` passes.
- ✅ **Core March widening slice 1 — interfaces/impls declaration checking, dispatch, and `derive`/`satisfy`, CLOSEOUT (`specs/lang/core-march-types.md` §2.3/§2.4, `specs/lang/core-march.md` §4.4.2–§4.4.4, `specs/lang/types/{accept,reject}/t23`–`t30`/`t18`–`t24`, `specs/lang/types/INDEX.md`, `specs/lang/interfaces.md`, 2026-07-06, `specs/plans/2026-07-06-widening-interfaces-impls-plan.md`, six tasks).** Widens the two conformance-tested core-language references past the core expression fragment to cover user-defined `interface`/`impl` DECLARATION checking (`(T-Interface)` registration, `(T-Impl)`'s seven ordered checks including MANDATORY superclass/`requires` enforcement, `impl_matches_ty` named as its own rule `(T-ImplMatch)`, default methods), the operational method-DISPATCH split (built-in `Show`/`Eq`/`Ord`/`Hash` via a runtime `impl_tbl` hashtable vs. user interfaces via ordinary lexical `env` shadowing), and `derive`/`satisfy` as `DImpl` generators (the closed five-interface set, `Json`'s pseudo-interface special case, `satisfy`'s all-or-nothing name-matching wiring) — the roadmap's stated "natural next widening slice" after constraint-discharge. Corpus grew from 39 to **54 programs** (30 accept / 24 reject); `check_types.sh` 54/54, exit 0, no new CI wiring (rides the existing `types-check` dune alias). **Two real gaps found and FILED (both deliberately left OPEN, not fixed — this is a documentation-only slice):** (1) no impl-coherence check anywhere — two overlapping impls both typecheck silently, and the interpreter (last-registered-wins) and compiled backend (first-registered-wins) disagree at runtime, confirmed live for plain/generic-vs-specific/derive-vs-manual overlap; (2) `derive X for UnknownType` silently no-ops (exit 0, no diagnostic, on both `--check` and RUN) — the one asymmetric exception among every other "declaration target doesn't exist" case in this slice. **Bonus finding while reconciling `specs/lang/interfaces.md`'s stale "Known issue" callout:** that callout (claiming a default `neq`-calling-`eq` "hangs/stack-overflows," blamed on a now-fixed `impl_tbl` dispatch-key collision) was reproduced live and found FALSE — neither backend hangs, and the interpreter is fully correct — so the callout was corrected to a historical note. But a DIFFERENT, real, previously-undocumented bug was found in the same area while reverifying it: a user-interface default method whose body calls a sibling interface method (`neq` calling `eq`, `Ord`'s `lt`/`gt` calling `cmp`) compiles to a lambda that returns the same answer regardless of the actual call arguments — compiled-only, interpreter correct; also FILED (open), not fixed. `interfaces.md`'s "Interface Dispatch" section's over-strong "no vtables or runtime type lookups" claim was also softened/scoped to the compiled backend's statically-resolved common case, with a cross-reference to `core-march.md` §4.4.2's full account. See `specs/progress.md`'s "Core March widening slice 1" entry for the full writeup and `.superpowers/sdd/widening-task-6-report.md` for this closeout task's own report. **Next queued widening slice:** modules or actors (roadmap Phase-2b/3 phasing).
- ✅ **`run_check_cmd` never stripped a stdlib module shadowed by a user file, silently corrupting unrelated typecheck state (`bin/main.ml`, `forge/test/test_build_check.ml`, 2026-07-06).** Found while trying to fully clean up a real dependency (`depot`) via the batched multi-file `march check`: two of depot's constructors (`PgTarget`/`SqliteTarget`) resolved fine when depot was checked alone, but became unresolvable — with no diagnostic pointing at the real cause — as soon as a large downstream consumer project's files were checked alongside it. Bisected (by halving the file set repeatedly) down to a single trigger file whose ONLY relevant property was declaring `mod Crypto do`, shadowing stdlib's own `Crypto` module by name. **Root cause:** the single-file `--check`/`--compile` path (`compile` in `bin/main.ml`) already strips a stdlib module from the combined declaration list when a project file shadows it by name (`extern_mod_names`, added specifically for this exact `mod Crypto do` scenario per its own doc comment) — but the multi-file `check` subcommand (`run_check_cmd`, batched onto by the `forge build`/`forge check` fix above) never had the equivalent guard, so two DMods both named `Crypto` ended up in the same combined module, corrupting unrelated global typecheck state badly enough to make a completely different file's own constructor unresolvable. **Fix:** `run_check_cmd` now filters `stdlib_decls` against the set of module names the checked files actually define (already computed by the dedup pass added in the fix above), mirroring `compile`'s existing `extern_mod_names` logic. New regression test `test_check_stdlib_shadow_does_not_corrupt_unrelated_module` in `forge/test/test_build_check.ml` — minimizing the real repro down to a 3-file case was itself informative: a same-file constructor build+match didn't reproduce it, only a shape where a separate file builds the value via a qualified call and a third file consumes it via a bare (unqualified) constructor pattern, exactly mirroring the real `depot_url.march`/`depot_db_repo.march` shape. Verified against the live repro: fixing this made BOTH `depot`'s `PgTarget`/`SqliteTarget` errors AND several of the downstream consumer's own unrelated errors (`ActionResult`/`ActionOk`/`ActionError`/`PathDep`, previously misattributed as real bugs in that project) disappear — they were all downstream victims of the same stdlib-shadowing corruption, not independent bugs.

- ✅ **A broken constructor field type was re-reported once per instantiation instead of once (`lib/errors/errors.ml`, `test/test_compiler.ml`, 2026-07-06).** Found while triaging the diagnostic output from the `run_check_cmd` fix above on the real ~85-file bastion project: one genuine typecheck error (`type Fallback = Fallback(Conn -> ActionResult -> Conn)` where `ActionResult` doesn't resolve) was printed 4 times, all citing the exact same file/line/column. **Root cause:** `instantiate_ctor` (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml:2464`) re-resolves a constructor's stored surface argument types via `surface_ty` on every instantiation (needed for fresh per-use type variables on polymorphic constructors) — but when one of those argument types fails to resolve, `surface_ty` re-emits the identical `(span, message)` diagnostic on every instantiation (pattern match, constructor call) instead of just the first. **Fix:** `Errors.report` (the single choke point every diagnostic passes through) now skips a diagnostic that exactly matches an already-reported `(severity, span, message)` triple — a byte-identical repeat at the same source location can only be the same fact re-derived, never two independent issues coinciding. New regression test `test_broken_ctor_field_type_reported_once` in `test/test_compiler.ml` (a 3-instantiation repro asserting the bogus-type error is reported exactly once). Verified against the live bastion repro: `router.march:46:35`'s `ActionResult` error dropped from 4 occurrences to 1; project-wide diagnostic count dropped from 54 to 46.

- ✅ **`march check`'s multi-file combined typecheck re-embedded shared/auto-discovered modules once per input file — confirmed superlinear blowup on a real ~85-file project, fixed (`bin/main.ml`'s `run_check_cmd`, `forge/test/test_build_check.ml`, 2026-07-06).** Found immediately after landing the `forge build`/`forge check` batching entry below, by measuring real compile/typecheck time on an external ~85-file project (`~/code/bastion`): the combined `march check f1 f2 ... fn` never finished in 4+ minutes, versus the old per-file behavior completing the whole project in seconds. **Root cause:** `run_check_cmd` called `resolve_imports` once per input file, and each call's `auto_discover` step unconditionally re-walks and re-parses the ENTIRE library search path with dedup tables scoped to that one call only — but the resulting `extra_decls` were then merged INTO that file's own wrapper `DMod`'s body instead of emitted as flat top-level siblings, hiding any cross-file duplicate from a name-based dedup pass and (independently) giving auto-discovered modules the wrong qualified name (`EntryFile.Module.name` instead of `Module.name` — the exact hazard `resolve_imports`'s own doc comment warns nesting causes). Confirmed empirically before the fix: checking N files together, the SAME diagnostic (same file/line/message) appeared exactly N times, and total time compounded far worse than linearly (1 file: 0.97s, 2: 2.10s, 5: 23.71s). **Fix:** `extra_decls` are now returned as flat siblings (`extra_decls @ [own DMod]` instead of merged into the own DMod's `mod_decls`), plus a dedup-by-module-name pass over the combined top-level decl list (first occurrence wins — safe since one-mod-per-file means a shared module name is always the same file). Post-fix: error/warning counts stay flat regardless of file count, time scales roughly linearly (1→20 files: 1.0–1.7s, no blowup), and the full 85-file bastion project completes in ~4.3s (previously never finished). New regression test `test_check_does_not_duplicate_shared_module_diagnostics` in `forge/test/test_build_check.ml` asserts a shared warning is counted exactly once across a 4-file combined check, calling `Cmd_build.check_all` directly to inspect its returned warning count. **Unrelated finding surfaced along the way (not fixed, out of scope):** bastion's own code used `write(path)(content)`-style curried calls (~36 call sites across 3 files) that the recently-landed `f(1)(2)`-is-a-parse-error change now rejects — fixed in bastion itself (a separate project), not in this repo.

- ✅ **`forge build`/`forge check` batch library typecheck into one `march check` subprocess instead of one-per-file (`bin/main.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_build.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_check.ml`, `forge/test/test_build_check.ml`, 2026-07-06).** Second half of the compile/typecheck-speed opportunity sweep. `Cmd_build.check_all` previously invoked `march --check <file>` as a separate subprocess per `.march` file in a library project, each independently re-parsing and re-typechecking the whole ~108-module stdlib plus any shared imports (e.g. a `common.march` imported by two sibling files got parsed/typechecked twice). Batched onto the compiler's existing combined-module `march check f1 f2 ...` subcommand instead — one process, one stdlib load, one typecheck across all files. Two wrinkles handled: (1) that subcommand (`run_check_cmd`) silently dropped warning diagnostics (only printed errors) — fixed to print both, in the same `file:line:col: <severity>: msg` format `count_diagnostics` already parses; (2) the single-file `--check` path had a compiler-side CAS cache making a repeated no-op rebuild near-instant, which the batched subcommand lacks — added a small forge-local cache (`.forge/check-cache/<hash-of-sorted-file-contents+lib_path_env>.clean` marker files) so an unchanged repeat `forge build`/`forge check` short-circuits before invoking `march` at all, rather than regressing relative to the old per-file behavior. Known limitation (documented in code): the cache key does not hash the *contents* of `MARCH_LIB_PATH` directories, only the env-var string, so editing a local `path = "../foo"` dependency in place without touching forge.toml/forge.lock won't invalidate it. 2 new Alcotest cases in `forge/test/test_build_check.ml` (warning surfacing from the combined module; cache-marker existence + mtime-unchanged-on-repeat-check).

- ✅ **`forge deps`/`forge build`/`forge check` now walk transitive dependencies instead of only a project's own direct `[deps]` (`forge/lib/project.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_deps.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_build.ml`, `forge/test/test_build_check.ml`, 2026-07-07).** Found while running a `forge build`/`forge check` timing analysis against a real external project (`~/code/scroll`, depends on `bastion` via path): `forge check` failed with `` Unknown module `Pool` `` (a module belonging to `depot`, which `bastion`'s own `forge.toml` depends on) — scroll never declared `depot` itself. **Root cause:** both `Cmd_deps.run` (installs deps + writes `forge.lock`) and `Cmd_build.lib_path_env` (builds `MARCH_LIB_PATH`) iterated only the current project's own flat `deps` list — neither recursed into an already-installed dependency's own `forge.toml` to discover ITS dependencies. (Note: `Archive_store.dep_lib_paths_for_archive`, used by the separate `forge publish`/archive-packaging path, already walked transitively — this gap was specific to the live dev-build/check path.) **Fix:** new `Project.dep_root_dir` resolves an installed dependency's own root (a `PathDep`'s target, or a git dep's CAS clone under `~/.march/cas/deps/<name>`) so its `forge.toml` can be read. `Cmd_build.collect_transitive_deps` recursively expands `MARCH_LIB_PATH` through every dependency-of-a-dependency, carrying each entry's own declaring root (a nested dep's `PathDep` is relative to ITS root, not the top-level project's). `Cmd_deps.bfs_install` installs wave-by-wave, discovering the next wave from each just-installed dep's own `forge.toml` `[deps]` (must run AFTER install since a git dep's `forge.toml` doesn't exist until cloned) and records every transitively-discovered dep in `forge.lock`. Both dedup by name with nearest-wins semantics and a `visited` set guards against dependency cycles. Verified live against the real scroll → bastion → depot chain (workaround of adding `depot` directly to scroll's `forge.toml` reverted first): `forge deps` now records `depot` in scroll's own `forge.lock`, and `forge check` passes cleanly with no direct scroll→depot dependency declared. New Alcotest case `test_lib_path_env_walks_transitive_path_deps` in `forge/test/test_build_check.ml` (a 3-project A→B→C path-dep chain where A's own code calls a C module directly).

- ✅ **Native codegen: bare sibling calls 2+ levels deep inside a stdlib/imported file mis-qualified — undefined symbols at link time (`lib/desugar/desugar.ml`, `lib/resolver/resolver.ml`, `bin/main.ml`, `test/native/nested_mod_qualcall.march`, `test/dune`, 2026-07-07).** Found trying to get `~/code/march_doc` to `forge build` cleanly: stdlib's `CRDT.PNCounter` (`mod PNCounter do ... end` nested inside `mod CRDT do ... end`) failed to link — `Undefined symbols: _PNCounter.map_inc, _PNCounter.map_sum, _PNCounter.map_max_merge`, referenced from the correctly-named `_CRDT.PNCounter.increment`/`.value`/etc. **Root cause:** `desugar.ml`'s `qualify_module_refs` (rewrites a bare intra-module call to its fully-qualified form so TIR's `rename_tir_vars` has something to match the definition's name against) always started its prefix accumulation at `""`. Since `March_ast.Ast.module_` splits a file's sole top-level `mod X do ... end` into separate `mod_name`/`mod_decls` fields (`mod_decls` is already the unwrapped body — no `DMod` node exists for the file's own name inside it), a stdlib/imported file's OWN top-level mod name was silently missing from the walk: `map_inc`'s bare call site inside `PNCounter.increment` got qualified as only `PNCounter.map_inc` (one level) instead of `CRDT.PNCounter.map_inc` (the true two-level nesting), while TIR lowering (which has direct access to the re-wrapping `DMod` auto-discovery produces) correctly named the DEFINITION `CRDT.PNCounter.map_inc` — reference and definition diverged and the linker rejected the binary outright. **Fix:** `qualify_module_refs` takes an `~entry_prefix` seed; `desugar_module` gained `~is_entry` (default `true`, preserving every pre-existing caller), computing `entry_prefix = if is_entry then "" else m.mod_name.txt ^ "."` — the entry file's own top mod name correctly stays OUT of the prefix (TIR unwraps the entry module; mirrors `Typecheck.cap_qual_prefix`'s existing, correct convention on the typecheck side), while a non-entry file's own name is now correctly folded in. `resolver.ml`'s two auto-discovery desugar call sites and `bin/main.ml`'s `load_stdlib_file` (for every stdlib file except `prelude.march`, which is unwrapped into global scope exactly like an entry module) now pass `~is_entry:false`. Verified: the real `CRDT.PNCounter` shape compiles+links+runs correctly standalone; `~/code/march_doc`'s `forge build` no longer errors on `_PNCounter.*` undefined symbols (only the separate, pre-existing, documented native `http_fetch`/`http_fetch_available` gap remains — eval/browser-only builtins, see the 2026-06-18 entry above). New native golden test `test/native/nested_mod_qualcall.march` (real `CRDT.PNCounter` shape, diffs against `.expected`). **NOT fixed (filed, narrower, lower-impact — see the "self-qualifying entry module" P2 item below):** an entry file explicitly writing out its OWN top-level mod name when self-qualifying a nested call (e.g. `mod Outer do fn main() do Outer.Inner.wrapped(5) end end`) still produces undefined symbols, since nothing strips that redundant self-prefix back off before TIR lowering (which never includes it in the first place).

- ✅ **`--timings` now covers parse/desugar/import-resolution/stdlib-load, not just typecheck-onward (`bin/main.ml`, `test/test_properties.ml`, 2026-07-06).** Investigated as part of a compile/typecheck-speed opportunity sweep: the `--timings` flag's clock (`t_compile_start`) was only started after the frontend (parse, desugar, cross-file import resolution, stdlib load) had already finished, so the cost of re-parsing/re-typechecking the ~108-module stdlib on every invocation — often the dominant cost for small user programs — was invisible. Moved the timer to start right before parsing and added `"parse"`/`"desugar"`/`"resolve-imports"`/`"stdlib-load"` stamps ahead of the existing `"typecheck"`-onward ones (which are unchanged, just now measured from an earlier, more accurate start point). New unit test `test_timings_covers_frontend` in `test/test_properties.ml`.

- ✅ **Resolved surface grammar reference v1 — `specs/lang/grammar.md`, `specs/lang/grammar/{parse,reject}/*.march` (27 programs), `check_grammar.sh`, `INDEX.md`, `grammar-check` CI lane (2026-07-06, `specs/plans/2026-07-06-resolved-grammar-plan.md`, six tasks).** Satisfies roadmap §6's "surface grammar (resolved)" Level-1/2 acceptance criterion for the **core** grammar. Reconciles the three-layer parse pipeline (lexer → `token_filter` → menhir) `lib/parser/parser.mly` leaves implicit into one normative, cited-by-line reference: §2 lexical layer (incl. string-interpolation brace-depth tracking, the one non-context-free lexer behavior); §3 the `token_filter` pre-pass (soft-keyword demotion, `choose…by` disambiguation, and the core newline-glom/match-arm-boundary lookahead — the genuinely non-context-free layer — plus the `is_pattern_start` hand-maintained-shadow-of-the-pattern-grammar hazard, cross-checked live and found in sync as of this pass); §4 the expression precedence ladder (10 strata, associativity witnessed by value-producing corpus programs, not merely transcribed from `parser.mly`); §5 blocks/statements/significant-newline semantics; §6 patterns (incl. the `PatRecord`/`PatAs` unreachable-from-surface-syntax finding, cross-referenced with `core-march.md`/`core-march-types.md`'s prior operational/typing-side notes of the same fact); §7 types; §8 core declaration forms (`mod` one-per-file, `fn`/`pfn` multi-head merge via `group_fn_clauses`, `type`/`ptype`, `use`/`import`/`alias`, `interface`/`impl`, `derive`/`satisfy`, no-`pub`-keyword visibility); §9 a deliberately lighter DSL appendix (actors/`app`/supervision/protocols/transitions/capability directives — shape+citation only, explicitly not fully resolved). Backed by a 27-program `parse/`/`reject` conformance corpus (17 parse + 10 reject, `specs/lang/grammar/INDEX.md` maps every program to the rule it anchors) run via `specs/lang/grammar/check_grammar.sh` (**27/27 passed**) and wired into CI as its own `grammar-check` dune alias (`test/dune`, mirroring `specs/lang/types/`'s `types-check` exactly — a separate slow lane, `dune build @grammar-check`, NOT folded into `runtest`/`oracle`), verified green (exit 0) via the exact command CI runs. Two findings surfaced by the corpus work, both collected in the chapter's own new "Known parser findings" section: (i) the `f(1)(2)`-chained-call position-dependence doc-precision gap (see the "Grammar / lint contradictions" entry above — **left OPEN**, not fixed by this closeout); (ii) the `is_pattern_start` shadow-grammar drift hazard (found in sync this pass, no automated guard against future drift — documented, not fixed). **Follow-up (not done in this pass, tracked here):** full resolution of §9's DSL forms (per-form EBNF, every error-recovery message captured live, dedicated parse/reject corpus programs) to the same depth as §4–§8.
- ✅ **Multi-arm `with ... else` failed to parse entirely (`lib/parser/token_filter.ml`, `test/test_compiler.ml`, 2026-07-06).** Any monadic `with pat <- e do body else ... end` with **two or more** else-arms was a hard parse error — the caret landed on the second arm's `->` — no matter the arm shape (nullary ctors, payload ctors, infix-operator bodies); a single else-arm always worked. This made the entire multi-arm error-handling form documented in `docs/pattern-matching.md`'s "With (Monadic Pattern Matching)" section (including its 3-arm `Err(AuthFailed)`/`Err(NotFound(kind))`/`Err(Timeout)` example) unwriteable. **Root cause:** the grammar parses else-arms with `separated_nonempty_list(arm_sep, branch)` — the same rule as `match` branches, needing NL/PIPE arm separators — but `token_filter.ml` only emitted arm-separator newlines inside a `Match` context. A monadic `with`'s `do` pushed a plain `Block`, so the newlines between else-arms were swallowed as insignificant whitespace (a single arm needs no separator, hence only multi-arm broke). **Fix:** a new `With` context variant marks the with-block — recorded when a `do` follows a `<-` (`GETS`, unique to with-bindings; multiple bindings of one `with` dedup to one pending entry keyed by paren-depth, mirroring `pending_match_depths`) — and at the `else` keyword the `With` context is promoted to `Match`, so the arms are newline-separated exactly like a `match` body; the single `end` pops it. Composes cleanly with the same-day cond-form/when-guard token-filter change (the promoted arms are ordinary pattern arms → `ms_is_cond = false`; verified `match do` boolean-operator arms still parse). The with-body itself stays block-style. `if ... else`, record-update `{ e with ... }` (uses `WITH` but no `GETS`), and `else if` chains are untouched (their `do` pushes `Block`, not `With`, so the `else` handler is a no-op for them). **Known pre-existing limitation (verified identical on base via file-copy revert — NOT a regression; mirrors the `match`-scrutinee case):** a *bare* unparenthesized `do`-block expression (`if`/`with`) as a with-binding RHS steals the pending marker — parenthesize the RHS (a bare `match` RHS works, since `match` has its own pending mechanism). 4 new parser regression tests in `test/test_compiler.ml` (single-arm guard, two nullary-ctor arms, three payload/nested-payload arms, infix-operator arm bodies). **Verification (six runners):** 420 compiler (+4) / 231 eval / 391 codegen / 804 stdlib (full, with Slow) / 29 snapshots exit 0; `test_stdlib_march`'s `global_registry` group (18/18) fails **pre-existing on clean main** (unbound `GlobalRegistry.*`, a module-resolution issue unrelated to this parser-only change — that module has zero `<-` tokens; confirmed by reverting the change and re-running). The `docs/pattern-matching.md` 3-arm example now compiles and runs.

- ✅ **`derive` on a variant type whose name collides with a stdlib RECORD type silently failed to typecheck — "`T` does not implement interface `Eq`/`Show`" (`lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml`, `test/test_codegen.ml`, 2026-07-06).** Found verifying `docs/interfaces.md`'s canonical `type Color = Red | Green | Blue` / `derive Eq, Show for Color` example live: it failed to compile with "`Color` does not implement interface `Eq`", while renaming the type to `Status` made the identical program work. **Root cause:** record types register in `env.records` under their BARE name globally (both `name.txt` and the qualified `qname`), so a user's variant `Color` collides in that flat namespace with stdlib `Plot.Color = { r, g, b }` (always auto-loaded). Both `surface_ty` (the `Ast.TyCon` record-expansion at the impl-type conversion in `check_decl`'s `DImpl`) and `register_impl_shape`'s `lenient_ty` (pass-1 pre-registration) then structurally expanded the variant's derived `impl Eq(Color)` to the record's `TRecord{r,g,b}` shape — confirmed by a structural dump of `env.impls["Eq"]`: `target=TCon(Color/0)` vs `impls=[TRecord{b,g,r}; …]`, `impl_matches_ty` false, so the derived impl was invisible and the type read as "does not implement". (The trigger is purely the type-NAME collision with a stdlib record; the `Plot.Color` fields even leaked into a two-*variant* repro, proving stdlib Plot — not any nested type — was the corruptor in every case.) **Fix:** a new `name_is_variant env name` helper (checks whether any constructor in `env.ctors` has `name` as its parent `ci_type`, bare or `.name`-suffix) now guards all three record-expansion `when` clauses (the two in `surface_ty` — direct + module-registry — and the one in `register_impl_shape`): a name that also denotes a variant is kept nominal (`TCon`) instead of being expanded to a same-named record's structure. A variant is never itself a record, so this only removes incorrect expansions — genuine record impls (`type Point = {x,y}` / `derive Eq, Show for Point`) still expand and dispatch structurally (verified). **Regression tests:** 2 compiled-interp-parity cases in `test/test_codegen.ml`'s `newtype_derived_method_crash` group — a variant colliding with stdlib `Plot.Color`, and a self-contained variant colliding with a *local* nested record (distinct `hue/sat/lum` fields, so it guards the fix even if `Plot.Color` is renamed/removed); both RED pre-fix (verified via file-copy swap to `git show HEAD:…typecheck.ml`), GREEN post-fix. **Gates (six runners):** 400 compiler / 230 eval / 391 codegen / 804 stdlib (full, with Slow) / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots — all exit 0. The `docs/interfaces.md` example needs no change (it now works as written; the suggested `Status` rename was only a stopgap for an unfixed compiler).

- ✅ **Language reference v1 consolidated into `specs/lang/` (roadmap §4.6, 2026-07-06).** The ~21 scattered current-truth language-reference docs (`specs/features/*.md`, `docs/*.md`, root guides) are folded into ONE versioned, structured reference set under `specs/lang/`: an umbrella index (`specs/lang/index.md`) over the two conformance-tested core references (`core-march.md`, `core-march-types.md`), a promoted `surface-syntax.md`, and 19 canonical per-topic chapters (modules, let-propagation, pattern-matching, interfaces, type-system, linear-types, refinement-types, session-types, capabilities, memory-model, safety-by-construction, actors, supervision, parallelism, clustering, standard-library). Compiler internals grouped into a sibling implementation reference (`specs/impl/index.md`) over 9 docs, referenced-not-merged. Superseded docs redirect via stubs; `scripts/check-docs.sh` now globs `specs/lang/` + `specs/impl/`. Real doc-correctness bugs fixed en route: `module-system` `pub`/`then` (obsolete), `let-propagation` "not yet implemented" (`let?` shipped), `refinement-types` `List.nth |> Option.unwrap` (wrong — `nth` returns bare `a`), the `actor-system` compiled-scheduler self-contradiction, and the CAS doc's phantom `driver.ml` pointer. **NEXT spec step (roadmap): the resolved surface grammar** — inputs already scouted (`parser.mly`'s 59 shift/reduce conflicts vs 5 `%prec` annotations; the `token_filter.ml` newline-glom layer with no BNF; pipe/`let?`/comprehensions absent from `core-march.md`'s desugar table; `then` tokenized-but-never-parses; `PatRecord`/`PatAs` unreachable). See `.superpowers/sdd/spec-consolidation-survey.md`.
- ✅ **Core March Static Semantics reference v1 — typing track complete (`specs/lang/core-march-types.md`, `specs/lang/types/{accept,reject}/`, `specs/lang/types/INDEX.md`, `specs/lang/types/check_types.sh`, 2026-07-05).** Companion to the operational `core-march.md`: documents the typechecker's `Γ ⊢ e : τ` judgment (bidirectional HM, `infer_expr`/`check_expr`) for the core fragment — literals, vars, `let`+generalization, lambda/application, `if`, ADTs+match, tuples+records, atoms, full pattern-typing relation (guards, exhaustiveness, `ECond`), local recursive fns (`ELetFn`), and the interface-constraint model (`Num`/`Eq`/`Ord`/`Show` discharge + `&&`/`||`/`not`) — every rule transcribed arm-for-arm from `lib/typecheck/typecheck.ml` and cited by line. 35-program `accept`/`reject` conformance corpus (20 accept, 15 reject), gated by `check_types.sh` and wired into its own `types-check` CI lane (separate from `@runtest`/`@oracle` since `reject/` programs are supposed to fail to typecheck). Together with `core-march.md` this is Level-1 for the Core March fragment (roadmap leveling, `specs/2026-07-04-language-specification-roadmap-design.md`). **The corpus surfaced 3 real typechecker bugs, deliberately left OPEN (not fixed by this docs-only track)** — see the P1 "Compiler (found during Core March language-spec typing track, 2026-07-05)" section below and the `ELetFn` double-report entry under "Compiler: Type System": (i) `let x : T = e` annotations parsed but never enforced; (ii) `when Interface(a)` constraints not re-checked at polymorphic call sites (a narrow soundness gap); (iii) `ELetFn` return-annotation mismatch reported twice (cosmetic). See `specs/progress.md`'s "Core March Static Semantics reference v1" entry for the full writeup.
- ✅ **Interpreter/compiled divergence: `ERecordUpdate` on a missing field — RESOLVED, both backends converged (Core March spec Task 3, `specs/lang/core-march.md`, `lib/eval/eval.ml`, `test/test_properties.ml`, `test/test_codegen.ml`, 2026-07-05).** Adjudicated the normative rule (§4.2.1 of the spec): a functional record update is defined ONLY for a field that already exists on the base record's actual (runtime) shape; updating an absent field is a runtime error — the compiled contract (`march_record_update_dyn`, `runtime/march_extras.c`) was already correct, the interpreter was the outlier. Converged `eval.ml`'s `ERecordUpdate` arm to validate every update name against the base's actual fields before merging and raise `eval_error "record update: no field '%s' in record" k` (wording matched to the compiled panic's "no field ... in record" for cross-backend diagnostic consistency) instead of silently appending the field. This divergence was only ever reachable through an ERASED base (`record_from_list`/`record_put` results — a bare, unconstrained type variable); a statically-typed record literal base already made an unknown-field update a typecheck-time error on both backends (`typecheck.ml:3869`–`3875`), so the vast majority of `{ base with ... }` call sites in stdlib/examples were completely unaffected by this change. Verified clean: all six standard runners green (**400 compiler / 230 eval / 385 codegen / 803 stdlib / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots**, all exit 0) and the `@oracle` conformance sweep green (34 matched, 7 known-divergence pre-existing/unrelated, 0 un-triaged, exit 0). The dedicated documented-skip unit test that used to pin this divergence (`test/test_properties.ml`) was updated to assert convergence instead — both backends now exit nonzero (non-signal) with a "no field" message for `{ record_from_list([("a", 1)]) with z: 99 }` — and renamed `test_record_update_missing_field_on_erased_base_converged`; `test/test_codegen.ml`'s `test_erased_update_missing_field_panics_compiled` doc comment updated to note the interpreter no longer diverges. This entry retires the divergence tracked here since Wave 1's B5 fix (`0d1a829e`, `specs/analysis/2026-07-01-pipeline-deep-review.md`).
- ✅ **`forge publish`/`forge retire` wired to a real forgepm registry over native HTTP/TLS (`forge/lib/registry_client.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_publish.ml`, `forge/lib/cmd_retire.ml`, `forge/tasks/forge_registry.march`, `forge/tasks/test_registry.march`, 2026-07-06).** Closes the "Phase 6" package-registry gap: `forge publish` previously validated locally only and printed "registry push not yet implemented" — no server, no auth, no network fetch. The client-side network/packaging logic (`forge/tasks/forge_registry.march`, renamed from `registry.march` so its `ForgeRegistry` module name matches March's camelCase→snake_case module-resolution convention — otherwise a sibling test file's `import ForgeRegistry` cannot resolve it) already existed and matched forgepm's real `POST /api/v1/packages/:name/releases` / `DELETE /api/v1/packages/:name/releases/:vsn` contract (`forgepm/specs/forge-publish-client.md`); the gap was entirely OCaml-side. Added: (1) a dune rule embedding the `.march` client into the `forge` binary (mirrors the existing `notebook.march` embedding); (2) `Registry_client.run_action`/`validate_registry_url` — a secret-safe subprocess runner using `Unix.create_process_env` (explicit env array + inherited stdin/stdout/stderr file descriptors, no shell string, no pipes) so the API token never appears in argv/`ps` and there's no two-pipe blocking-read deadlock risk, plus an HTTPS-by-default gate (refuses `http://` without `--insecure`/`FORGE_INSECURE=1`); (3) `cmd_publish.ml` wired to it (existing semver-bump pre-flight check unchanged, still runs and can still reject before any network step) plus new `--registry`/`--insecure` flags; (4) new `forge retire <VERSION> [--reason TEXT] [--registry URL] [--insecure]` command (`cmd_retire.ml`); (5) 13 unit tests (`forge/tasks/test_registry.march`) for the client's pure logic (multipart framing incl. binary-safety, response classification incl. friendly `2fa_required`/`version_exists` messages, endpoint URL building, manifest parsing) — required promoting 7 functions from `pfn` to `fn` in `forge_registry.march` (RED/GREEN verified via a file-copy swap, never `git stash` — the stash stack is shared across concurrent sessions on this machine) and adding matching `needs IO.Console`/`NetConnect`/`NetConnect.TLS`/`FileRead`/`FileWrite`/`Random` capability declarations to the test module (the March capability system requires an importing module to redeclare the capabilities its import transitively needs). Exit codes 0/1/2/3/4/5 (success/config/local-validation/transport/4xx/5xx) implemented exactly per the spec. **Live end-to-end verified** against a real local forgepm dev server (Postgres-backed): seeded an admin user + API key directly via SQL (forgepm's own `forge forgepm.seed` one-shot task hit a pre-existing, unrelated Depot `Pool` actor "no reply" timeout when run as a short-lived script — confirmed NOT a registry-client bug by verifying the identical Pool/actor code path works fine inside the long-running `forgepm.server` process; noted as a forgepm/Depot follow-up, out of scope here) — real `forge publish --registry http://localhost:4040 --insecure` against a fixture package returned `published <name> <version> (sha256 ...)` exit 0, verified server-side via a DB row in `packages`/`versions`; `forge retire` similarly returned exit 0 and flipped the version's `retired` flag; the HTTPS gate was confirmed to refuse a bare `--registry http://...` (no `--insecure`) with exit 1 and zero server-side log growth (rejected before any network call). This live test ran over `http://` (`--insecure`, local dev only). **Follow-up (same day): a real production publish against `https://forgepm.org` uncovered and fixed two real march compiler/runtime bugs; the client is fully verified working, but the production publish itself is separately blocked by a genuine forgepm server-side bug (a different project/repo).** The interpreter's `Tls.*` builtins (`lib/eval/eval.ml`) turned out to be intentional stubs for unit-testing only (`tls_read` unconditionally returns `Ok("")`, `tls_write` unconditionally claims success — "real TLS runs via march_tls.c in the compiled native binary" per the code comment) — so `Registry_client.run_action` must compile, not interpret, `forge_registry.march`. Compiling and running it against the real server initially SIGSEGVed; root-caused to two bugs, both fixed: (1) `lib/tir/llvm_builtins.ml`'s `tls_write` entry declared `ret_ty = Some Tir.TUnit` while the C function (`march_tls_write`) actually returns a heap-allocated `Result(Int, String)` — the type mismatch corrupted the compiled backend's handling of the returned pointer; fixed to `Result(Int, String)`, matching `tls_read`/`tls_connect`'s existing pattern. (2) `runtime/march_tls.c`'s client `SSL_CTX` never set `SSL_MODE_AUTO_RETRY`, so a bare `SSL_read()` could return early (`SSL_ERROR_ZERO_RETURN`) after consuming a TLS 1.3 post-handshake record (e.g. a session ticket most real servers send immediately after the handshake) before any application data arrived — reproducible as `Ok("")` on every read against `forgepm.org` even with the write-return-type fix applied; fixed by adding `SSL_CTX_set_mode(ctx, SSL_MODE_AUTO_RETRY)`. Both verified via a minimal 20-line standalone repro getting a real `HTTP/1.1 200 OK` body back from `forgepm.org` compiled — full suite (804 march tests + forge's own suite) still green after both fixes. `Registry_client.run_action` switched from interpreted back to compiled (the correct design all along, per `forge_registry.march`'s own header comment — it just needed these two bugs fixed to actually work). The separate compiled `tcp_recv_http` panic (plain-HTTP-only, unrelated to TLS) remains open — see its own P0 entry above; it does not block a real `https://` publish, which never calls that path. **With both bugs fixed, a real `forge publish` against `https://forgepm.org` correctly TLS-connects, sends a well-formed multipart request (verified byte-identical behavior against a raw `curl` control), and correctly parses/classifies the server's response** — but the actual publish attempt (`marathon`, and independently a brand-new never-seen-before test package) consistently gets `HTTP 502` from the real production forgepm.org server, reproduced identically via plain `curl` with a real, correct-checksum tarball (ruling out anything march/forge-side) — **this is a genuine forgepm production server-side bug (a separate project/repo), not a march or forge issue, and is out of scope here.** The march/forge side of this plan is complete and fully verified; publishing any real package to the current forgepm.org production deployment is blocked until that separate bug is fixed in the forgepm project. **Verification gotcha (worth remembering):** a stale pooled DB connection on the *dev server's* first boot caused one spurious `401 invalid_api_key` on the very first local dev-registry live publish attempt (the connection's transaction snapshot predated the SQL-inserted seed row); restarting the server resolved it — not a registry-client bug, but confusable with one if hit without cross-checking with a direct `psql` query. **Second gotcha:** `march --compile`'s runtime-source discovery (`bin/main.ml`, both `ensure_runtime_so` and the native-compile path, ~3 near-identical copies) is a plain relative path (`"runtime/march_runtime.c"`) resolved against the *process's current working directory*, with executable-relative fallbacks that only resolve for an opam-installed binary (`<opam-prefix>/march/runtime/...`) or the march repo's own root — NOT for an arbitrary invocation directory otherwise. `Registry_client.find_march()` looks for a `march` binary sibling to `forge`'s own executable first, falling back to whatever `march` resolves to on `PATH` (the installed opam one) — so publishing from a package directory like `~/code/marathon` silently compiles via the *installed* `march`, which must be freshly `opam upgrade`d after any runtime/compiler fix lands on `main` or it links against a stale runtime — always re-run `opam upgrade march forge` immediately before re-testing a runtime/compiler fix, not just once at the start of a session. See `forgepm/specs/forge-publish-client.md` for the full client-registry contract.

- ✅ **`Cli` stdlib module — final-review fixes (`stdlib/cli.march`, `test/stdlib/test_cli.march`, 2026-07-05).** Whole-branch review of the completed 4-task `Cli` plan raised four findings, all fixed: (1) *dead ADT variant* — `CliError.InvalidValue(String, String)` was defined and rendered by `error_message` but never constructed by anything (`get_int` swallows a parse failure into `None`, not a `CliError`); deleted the variant and its `error_message` arm and the corresponding test assertion, since `CliError` should only carry variants `parse` can actually produce (`UnknownFlag`/`MissingValue`/`MissingRequired`) — matches the shipped fail-safe/`Option`-returning accessor design, not a `Result`-returning one. (2) *no end-to-end composition test* — added `describe "Cli end-to-end composition"` in `test/stdlib/test_cli.march`: one spec list (`sample_specs()`) drives both `Cli.parse` (read back via `get_string_or`/`get_bool`/`get_int`/`positional` on the same parsed result) and `Cli.help_text`, proving the module's central "one spec list, two consumers" design claim. (3) *negative-number positional misclassified as unknown flag* — `flag_name` treated any `-`-prefixed token as flag-shaped, so `Cli.parse(specs, ["-5"])` wrongly returned `Err(UnknownFlag("5"))`; added private `single_dash_is_negative_number_or_bare` (uses `Char.is_digit` on the character after a single dash) so a lone `-<digit>` (or bare `-`) now returns `None` from `flag_name` (treated as positional), while any `--`-prefixed token (even `--5`) is unaffected and still flag-shaped. New test: `Cli.parse([], ["-5"])` succeeds with `"-5"` in `positional`. (4) *cheap behavior-pinning tests* — added a test asserting a flag passed twice (`["--port","1","--port","2"]`) keeps the FIRST value (`"1"`, current `parse_loop` accumulate-then-reverse semantics, unchanged) and a test asserting empty argv against a spec list with a required flag returns `Err(MissingRequired(...))`. **Verification:** `dune build --root .` clean (removing the `InvalidValue` variant left no dangling references — exhaustiveness checker had nothing to flag since the `error_message` arm was removed in the same edit); `./_build/default/bin/main.exe test test/stdlib/test_cli.march` — all 18 named tests pass (24 doctest failures remain, identical pre-existing `expr_eof`-single-expression gap from Tasks 1–2, none newly introduced); `scripts/run-tests.sh -q` — 773 stdlib-quick tests + compiler/eval/codegen all green, no regressions. Grepped the whole repo for other `Cli`/`InvalidValue` references — none found outside `stdlib/cli.march` and `test/stdlib/test_cli.march` (brand-new module, nothing else depends on it yet).

- ✅ **`Cli` stdlib module — Task 4 (final): `Cli.help_text` auto-generated `--help` output (`stdlib/cli.march`, `test/stdlib/test_cli.march`, 2026-07-05).** Closes out the argument-parsing `Cli` module plan (Tasks 1–3 landed types/constructors, `Cli.parse` + accessors, `Cli.get_int`/`Cli.error_message`). New `Cli.help_text(program_name : String, specs : List(FlagSpec)) : String`: renders a `"Usage: <program_name> [OPTIONS]\n\n"` header followed by one block per spec via `List.fold_left`, each block built by a private `flag_line` (`"  --<long><short_suffix>\n    <help><annotation>\n"`) composed from two smaller privates — `short_suffix` (`", -<s>"` if `spec.short` is `Some`, else empty) and `annotation` (`" (required)"` if `spec.required`, else `" (default: <d>)"` if `spec.default` is `Some`, else empty). 2 new tests in `test/stdlib/test_cli.march` under `describe "Cli.help_text"` (usage line + long/short rendering + help text + default annotation; required annotation), both green — 14/14 named tests in the file pass (`./_build/default/bin/main.exe test test/stdlib/test_cli.march`). **One deviation from the brief, forced by the grammar:** the brief's `List.fold_left(specs, header, fn acc, spec -> acc ++ flag_line(spec))` (bare comma-separated params, no parens) is a parse error — March's lambda grammar requires parenthesizing a multi-param lambda (`fn (acc, spec) -> ...`), confirmed against `List.fold_left`'s own doctest (`stdlib/list.march:421`, `fn (acc, x) -> acc + x`) which already uses the parenthesized form; used that instead, no behavior change. **Doctest note (pre-existing, not a regression):** `Cli.help_text`'s own doctest fails with the same known "doctest parse error" as every other `Cli` doctest that opens with a `let` line or contains a nested `Cli.flag(...)` call inside the assertion — this is the same repo-wide `expr_eof`-single-expression gap already tracked from Tasks 1–2 (shared with `RRB.fold`/`RRB.chunk`), not new here. **Full suite:** `scripts/run-tests.sh -q` — 773 stdlib-quick tests + compiler/eval/codegen all green, no regressions. No stdlib module-count change (this task adds functions to the existing `cli.march`, module #108 from Task 1 — see `specs/progress.md`'s Current State entry).

- ✅ **`Cli` stdlib module — Task 3: `Cli.get_int` typed accessor + `Cli.error_message` (`stdlib/cli.march`, `test/stdlib/test_cli.march`, commit `ca81513b`, 2026-07-05).** Third task of the argument-parsing `Cli` module plan (follows Task 2's `Cli.parse` + string/bool accessors). New in `stdlib/cli.march`: `Cli.get_int(parsed, long) : Option(Int)` (reads the raw string value via `get_string`, then `Result.to_option(String.to_int(s))`; `None` both when unset and when set-but-not-numeric, mirroring `Env.get_int`'s fail-safe behavior — does not consult `FlagSpec.default`, same convention as `get_string`) and `Cli.error_message(err : CliError) : String` (one-line renderer for all four `CliError` cases: `UnknownFlag`/`MissingValue`/`MissingRequired`/`InvalidValue`). Tests added under `describe "Cli typed accessors and error messages"` in `test/stdlib/test_cli.march`, all green. (This entry backfilled retroactively during Task 4's spec-freshness pass — the `ca81513b` commit itself landed the code and tests but did not update `specs/todos.md`/`specs/progress.md` at the time.)

- ✅ **`Cli` stdlib module — Task 2: `Cli.parse` flag/value/positional parsing loop + basic accessors (`stdlib/cli.march`, `test/stdlib/test_cli.march`, 2026-07-05).** Second task of the argument-parsing `Cli` module plan (follows Task 1's types/constructors). New in `stdlib/cli.march`: `Cli.parse(specs, argv) : Result(ParsedArgs, CliError)` — a single left-to-right recursive `parse_loop` that classifies each token via `flag_name` (strips a leading `--`/`-`; `None` means positional), resolves it against `specs` by long or short name (`resolve_spec`/`find_by_long`/`find_by_short`), consumes a following value for `ValueFlag` specs (`Err(MissingValue(long))` if none), records `"true"` for `BoolFlag` specs, accumulates unmatched tokens as positionals, and at end-of-input checks every `required: true` spec got a value (`Err(MissingRequired(long))` via `find_missing_required`) before returning `Ok({values, positional})` (both accumulators reversed once, not consed in output order). Plus 4 accessors: `Cli.get_string`, `Cli.get_string_or`, `Cli.get_bool` (absent → `false`), `Cli.positional`. 9 new tests in `test/stdlib/test_cli.march` under a `describe "Cli.parse"` block (long/short/bool flags, default fallback, unknown flag, missing value, missing required), all green. **Three deviations from the brief, all required for the code to actually parse/run:** (1) the brief's `sample_specs()` test helper was written nested inside the `describe` block — March's grammar does not allow `fn`/`pfn` declarations inside a `describe do...end` body (confirmed against `test/stdlib/test_array.march`'s `pfn make_vec`/`test/stdlib/test_http.march`'s `pfn make_test_req` pattern) — hoisted to a module-level `pfn sample_specs()` before the `describe` blocks instead. (2) the brief's `flag_name` used a 2-branch `if/else if/else` chain closed with a single `end` — March requires one `end` per `if` in an `else if` chain (each `else` branch is a `block_body` that can itself contain a full nested `if...end`, so an N-`if` chain needs N `end`s; confirmed against `stdlib/base64.march`'s existing 3-way `else if` chain) — added the second `end`. (3) the brief's `find_missing_required` and `get_string` used `pair.0`/`pair.1` tuple-index field access on a `(String, String)` tuple — March's grammar has no numeric tuple-index production at all (`lib/parser/parser.mly`'s `expr_field` only accepts `DOT lower_name`/`upper_name`; no `DOT INT`), so `pair.0` fails to parse in any context, not just this one — rewrote both as `match pair do (name, value) -> ... end` pattern destructuring instead (the established March idiom per `stdlib/array.march`/`stdlib/bigint.march`'s existing `let (a, b) = tuple` usage). **All three are language-level constraints, not implementation choices** — the parsing/accessor *logic* itself (loop structure, error cases, accumulator order) matches the brief exactly. **Doctest note (pre-existing, not a regression):** `Cli.parse`'s own one-line doctest passes; but the 6 doctests using the brief's two-line `let parsed = Cli.parse(...)` / `match parsed do ... end` pattern (`Cli.get_string`, `Cli.get_string_or`, `Cli.get_bool`, `Cli.positional`, plus Task 1's carried-over `Cli.flag`/`Cli.value_flag`/`Cli.flag_with`) fail with "doctest parse error" — confirmed identical to the pre-existing `RRB.fold`/`RRB.chunk` doctest failures on a clean checkout of Task 1's commit (`f16cecf2`, via an isolated `git worktree add`), i.e. the doctest runner's single-expression `expr_eof` parser cannot handle a bare `let` as one `march>` line, regardless of module. Not introduced by Task 2, out of scope to fix here. **Verification gotcha:** `dune exec march -- test ...` intermittently ran a stale/wrong `main.exe` in this session (typecheck passed but eval reported "unbound variable: Cli.flag"/"Cli.parse" for functions that demonstrably exist and work when invoked directly) — switched to `./_build/default/bin/main.exe test ...` (the direct-binary invocation this repo's `CLAUDE.md` already recommends for reliability) and all 9 tests passed cleanly; root cause not pinned down further since the direct-binary path is the documented correct invocation anyway. Full `scripts/run-tests.sh -q` (compiler/eval/codegen/stdlib) green, 773 stdlib-quick tests, no regressions.

- ✅ **`Cli` stdlib module — Task 1: `FlagSpec`/`ParsedArgs`/`CliError` types + spec constructors (`stdlib/cli.march`, `test/stdlib/test_cli.march`, `bin/main.ml`, 2026-07-05).** First task of the argument-parsing `Cli` module plan. New `stdlib/cli.march`: `FlagArity = BoolFlag | ValueFlag`, `FlagSpec` record (`long`/`short`/`arity`/`default`/`required`/`help`), `CliError = UnknownFlag | MissingValue | MissingRequired | InvalidValue` (parse-time errors for later tasks), `ParsedArgs` record (`values`/`positional`). Three constructors: `Cli.flag(long, help)` (boolean, no short form, optional), `Cli.value_flag(long, help)` (value-taking, optional, no default), `Cli.flag_with(long, short, arity, default, required, help)` (full constructor). Registered `"cli.march"` in `bin/main.ml`'s `stdlib_file_list` (required for `Cli` to resolve at all via eager stdlib loading — the module-registry lookup by itself only kicks in for explicitly-imported/lib-path modules, not the built-in stdlib set). 3 tests in `test/stdlib/test_cli.march` (spec-constructor field checks), all green. **Doctest note:** the module's three `doc` blocks originally used a call-then-field-access one-liner (`Cli.flag(...).long`) — March's parser does not support field access directly on a call-expression result (`mk().x` fails to parse; confirmed as a general grammar gap, not doctest-specific). Rewritten as two-line `let`-then-field doctests, matching the pattern in `stdlib/rrb_vec.march`; those `let`-as-doctest-line examples are known to fail the doctest runner's single-expression `expr_eof` parse (pre-existing gap — `RRB.fold`/`RRB.chunk` doctests fail identically on unmodified `main`), so `Cli.flag`/`Cli.value_flag`/`Cli.flag_with`'s doctests currently report as failing for the same pre-existing infra reason; not introduced by this task and out of scope to fix here. — `let ((a,b),(c,d)) = ...` emitted references to undefined global functions (`lib/tir/lower.ml`, `test/test_codegen.ml`, `specs/lang/golden/`, `specs/lang/core-march.md`, 2026-07-05).** Found by the Core March golden corpus (Phase 1 Task 2, tuples). A nested tuple destructured in a block-`let` — e.g. `let ((a, b), (c, d)) = ((1, 2), (3, 4))` — ran correctly interpreted (prints `10`) and compiled fine as the equivalent `match ((1,2),(3,4)) do ((a,b),(c,d)) -> … end`, but `--compile` on the `let` form emitted `%gl = call ptr @a()` for every leaf var and clang rejected the module (`error: use of undefined value '@a'`, exit 1). **Root cause:** the block-`let` `PatTuple` lowering (`EBlock (ELet … :: rest)` arm) bound only *direct* `PatVar` tuple elements — its inner fold had `| Ast.PatVar n -> …bind field… | _ -> inner`, so any non-var element (crucially a NESTED `PatTuple`) hit the `| _ -> inner` catch-all and was **silently dropped**. The nested tuple's leaf vars (`a`/`b`/`c`/`d`) were therefore never bound; later uses fell through `resolve_use_alias` and were rewritten into global function references (`@a()`), which don't exist → clang error. (`collect_pat_names` DID recurse — so the names were registered in `_fn_param_types` and typechecked — but the value-binding fold flattened only one level, which is why the failure surfaced at codegen, not typecheck.) **Fix:** replaced the one-level fold with a recursive `bind_subpat scrut scrut_ty pat inner` helper (local to the `PatTuple` arm) that mirrors the match path's `compile_matrix` decomposition — it handles `PatWild` (skip), `PatVar` (bind field directly, preserving the original RC-friendly single-level shape when the element is a leaf), `PatAs` (bind outer name + recurse), and nested `PatTuple` (bind a fresh var to the field via `EField`, then recurse into it, threading each element's concrete type from the parent tuple type so nested scalar fields untag correctly). Records / refutable sub-patterns keep the prior no-decompose behaviour (unchanged; they fall to the outer catch-all `let`). Interpreter and flat-tuple / direct-`PatVar` lowering are unchanged (flat `let (a,b) = …` produces byte-identical TIR — the 29 TIR snapshots stay green with no regeneration). **Regression:** new `nested_tuple_let_codegen` group in `test/test_codegen.ml` (2 compile-and-run tests: the exact repro → `10`; a deeper 3-level-nesting + wildcard-element + interior-nested-tuple program → `15`) — both RED pre-fix with the `@a` clang error, GREEN post-fix. New golden program `specs/lang/golden/g10_nested_tuple_let.march` (output `10`), added to the §5 corpus table in `specs/lang/core-march.md` (the corpus's 10th program — `g09_float_show` is the concurrently-added float golden merged from main; `verify.sh` 10/10 MATCH) so the fix runs in the `@oracle` conformance lane. **Gates (six runners + golden + oracle):** 400 compiler / 230 eval / 387 codegen (+2) / 773 stdlib-quick / 53 stdlib_march / 29 snapshots — all exit 0; golden `verify.sh` 10/10. The full-stdlib (with Slow) 15 `adversarial-regressions` failures and the codegen `llvm_ir_validity_gate #3` failure were verified **pre-existing** (identical failure sets with the fix reverted to base via file-copy swap — `Unknown module IO/Process`, `non-pointer scrutinee` distributed-program ICEs; the `cannot find runtime` ones are CWD/env, all unrelated to and untouched by a TIR-lowering change).

- ✅ **`==` operator gave the WRONG answer (silent, not a crash) compiled on a `Newtype`-repr type with a heap payload (`lib/tir/llvm_eq.ml`, `test/test_codegen.ml`, `test/test_properties.ml`, 2026-07-04).** Found by the differential oracle (Task 4 / Phase 2b) while scoping the derived-method generators; distinct from the named-method fix `6cc676fc` (that fixed the NAMED `eq()`/`compare()` path via desugar span-uniquification + `emit_case` Newtype TVar-recovery; this is the `==` OPERATOR path). For `type W = W(String)` (Newtype repr — single ctor, single field — so the value IS its raw payload, no wrapper cell), compiled `W("x") == W("x")` returned `false` while the interpreter correctly gave `true`. **Root cause:** `ensure_adt_eq_fn` special-cased the Niche (Option-shaped) repr but never consulted `Repr.repr_of_ty` for `Newtype`, so a single-ctor single-field `TCon` fell to the generic Boxed ctor-table strategy, which reads a "ctor tag" at `payload_ptr + 8` and a "field" at `payload_ptr + 16`. For a Newtype the "payload_ptr" IS the raw payload (the String's own heap cell / a Boxed ADT cell), so those offsets land inside the payload's layout and the comparison is garbage. Confirmed by `--emit-llvm`: pre-fix `__march_eq_W` took the full boxed tag-load path; post-fix it is `%ok = call i64 @march_string_eq(ptr %a, ptr %b)  ret i64 %ok`.
